Each pair is queried once per block and prime at its folded position.
Matrix formation costs $\widetilde O(BND)$, with saving
$1-3e/4-3z/4$; the earlier sufficient residue allowance $1-a$ also holds.
All input formation, stored intermediate arrays, sparse retrievals, and
cyclic rotations are included in these terms. Every gap is strict, so
polylogarithmic overhead, the min-plus bit-scaling calls, and APSP
squarings fit the claimed ordinary $O(n^{3-\delta})$ bound.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Deterministic APSP with the SODA 2024 bounds]
\label{cor:det-apsp-lazy}
Use the displayed interpolation from arXiv:2307.07970v2 and take
\begin{gather*}
k=11,\quad c=21.8429984,\quad\theta=0.1125375917,\\
r=0.0254666251,\quad\alpha=0.0478165400,\\
e=0.0613141114,\quad z=0.0003737179,\quad\varepsilon=10^{-8}.
\end{gather*}
Then $a=0.03084391465$, $b=0.03047019675$, and
$\kappa=1+(k-1)b=1.3047019675$. The exact interval certificates give
\[
\begin{array}{lr}
\text{cost term}&\text{saving below exponent }3\\
\hline
\text{boxes}&0.004110896862224\ldots\\
\text{residual queries}&0.004110896885301\ldots\\
\text{stored-box queries}&0.004110896880317\ldots\\
\text{lazy retrievals (certified lower bound)}&0.004110898310961\ldots\\
\text{scans}&0.004110896900000\\
\text{encodings}&0.004110899160297\ldots\\
\text{selection, including }10^{-8}&0.004110887833450
\end{array}
\]
The outer-band and product-capacity slacks are, respectively,
\[
0.002654752597761\ldots\qquad\text{and}\qquad0.789932983443956\ldots.
\]
All exceed their required thresholds, so $\delta=0.0041108$ yields
\[
\boxed{\text{deterministic integer APSP/min-plus/all-edges Exact Triangle
in }O(n^{2.9958892}).}
\]
The lazy-query certificate bounds its small exponential tail by
$3^{-X}<3^{-21}$, where $X=c(1-\alpha)/(1-r)^2>21$, before applying
rational interval logarithms. This improves $2.996261$ without using the
October 2026 rectangular theorem discussed next.
\end{corollary}

\begin{corollary}[A conditional matrix-multiplication dependency]
\label{cor:det-apsp-recent-mm}
Assume the rectangular matrix-multiplication bound of
Uzna\'nski~\cite{U26}, Corollary~1.2:
\[
\omega(1,\kappa,1)\le w(\kappa):=1+\kappa+\frac1{4\kappa}
\qquad(\kappa\ge1/2).
\]
Then the algorithm of Theorem~\ref{thm:det-apsp-refined}, with
Lemma~\ref{lem:cyclic-lazy}, gives deterministic all-edges Exact Triangle,
min-plus product, and APSP on polynomially bounded integer weights in
$O(n^{2.9958323})$ time. This additional bound is recorded separately from
the preceding result because it uses a different, recent external theorem.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
Only the moment-selector exponent changes. Take
\[
\begin{gathered}
k=13,\quad c=21.4287303,\quad\theta=0.1128443719,\quad
r=0.0255360478,\\
\alpha=0.0483646500,\quad e=0.0625769172,\quad z=0.00032059682,
\quad\delta=0.0041677.
\end{gathered}
\]
Thus $a=(e+z)/2=0.03144875701$,
$b=(e-z)/2=0.03112816019$, and
$\kappa=1+(k-1)b=1.37353792228$.
Reserve $\varepsilon=10^{-8}$ in the matrix-multiplication exponent.
The selector saving is
\[
3-w(\kappa)-(k+1)a-\varepsilon
=0.004167749375717\ldots>\delta.
\]
The other cost and geometry inequalities are unchanged. In particular,
$X=c(1-\alpha)/(1-r)^2>21$, so the lazy-query tail is bounded exactly
using $3^{-X}<3^{-21}$ and rational interval logarithms.
Both prefix-query terms, this lazy-query upper bound, boxes, scans,
encodings, matrix formation, and both layout conditions have strict
rational certificates at the displayed parameters.

The external paper uses arithmetic exponents with an arbitrarily small
positive allowance; its Lemma~6.1 fixes a finite exact bilinear identity
before recursing, and it states characteristic-zero validity.
The exact polynomial-algebra and bit-complexity transfer in
Theorem~\ref{thm:det-apsp-refined} therefore applies. Equivalently, a fixed
complex rank identity for a rational tensor has an algebraic solution to
its coefficient equations; all constants then lie in one fixed number
field. Representing that field in a rational basis gives fixed-size
coefficient operations, with $O(\log n)$-bit numerators and denominators
through the recursion. No variable polynomial-ring element is inverted.
The strict gaps absorb the remaining logarithmic factors in filtering,
bit scaling, and repeated min-plus squaring.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Deterministic zero-weight $k$-Clique decision]
\label{cor:det-clique-decision}
Split the $k$ parts into groups of $\alpha=\lfloor k/3\rfloor$, $\alpha$, and
$m=k-2\alpha\ge\alpha$ parts. Then $\mu=m/\alpha\ge1$, the second selection
regime applies, and every saving of Corollary~\ref{cor:det-all-edges-moments}
scales by $\alpha$. Zero-Weight $k$-Clique, decision and search, with polynomially
bounded integer weights therefore takes
$O(n^{k-0.003739\lfloor k/3\rfloor})$ deterministic time for every fixed
$k\ge3$, which improves the deterministic zero-weight bound of
Corollary~\ref{cor:all-k} for every $k\ge3$. For $k=5$ the split $(2,2,1)$,
with $\mu=1/2$, $k'=8$ stages, and
\[
(c,\theta,e,z)=(23.04,\;0.11108,\;0.053230,\;0.0004529),
\]
has savings per unit of $\alpha$ equal to $0.003624049\ldots$, $0.003625265\ldots$,
$0.0036232$, $0.004230855\ldots$, and $0.003711165\ldots$ for boxes,
queries, failed scans, encodings, and selection in the first regime, with
the graph construction $O(R^2+RH)$ saving $\mu$; all exceed $0.00362$, so
Zero-Weight $5$-Clique is decided in $O(n^{4.99276})$ time. The minimum- and
maximum-weight versions follow from the per-pair theorem above.
\end{corollary}

\subsection*{Earlier real-input comparison bound}
Use the source's Lemmas 36--37. Set comparison-list length $d=n^u$ and
bucket size $s=n^{1+u-e}$, so the matrix middle dimension is $O(n^e)$
when $u<e$. One comparison count costs, up to polylogarithms,
\[
n^{2-eG}+|\mathcal P|n^{eq}+n^{2+2u-e}.
\]
The last term comes from $nds+ds^2$; its second summand is smaller since
$u<e$. The explicit input construction and sorting terms are smaller at
the parameters below.

For real 3SUM, the source reduction has $|\mathcal P|=O(n^{2-u})$ and
polylogarithmically many such calls. For real min-plus/APSP, multiplying the
per-count costs by the source's list and call counts gives the same saving
below exponent $3$ rather than $2$. In both cases the saving is
\[
\min\{eG,\ u-eq,\ e-2u\}.
\]
Take $c=20.276$, $\theta=0.104566$, $e=0.059612$, and
$u=e(1+q)/3$. The first saving is $0.003680563566\ldots$ and the other
two are $0.003680624519\ldots$, all strictly above $0.003680$.
The encoding saving $1-eE$ also exceeds this value.
The Las Vegas guarantees of the source reductions are preserved, yielding
expected $O(n^{1.99632})$ real 3SUM and $O(n^{2.99632})$ real APSP/min-plus.

\subsection*{Real inputs: rank-prefix hashing and complementary certification}
The same-block enumeration in the comparison-count reduction can be
removed by changing its algorithm. The replacement below returns exact
counts, with randomness affecting only its running time. It uses only
comparisons on the real inputs: all subsequent hashing acts on their
polynomially bounded integer ranks.

\begin{lemma}[Certified comparison counts]
\label{lem:prefix-count}
There are $n$ row lists and $n$ column lists, each of length at most
$1\le d\le n$, and a prescribed set $\mathcal P\subseteq[n]^2$ of list
pairs. Suppose either (a) every list has at most one occurrence of each
of at most $d$ colors, or (b) all occurrences have a single color.
Put $\ell=\lceil\log_2(2nd+1)\rceil$ and let $D$ be the least power of four
at least $2d^2\ell^5$. Subject to the tiling condition of
Theorem~\ref{thm:refined}, the number of same-color pairs $x<y$ for every
$(r,c)\in\mathcal P$ can be computed in Las Vegas expected time
\[
\softO\left(nD^E+n^2D^{-G}+|\mathcal P|D^q+nD+nd\right).
\]
The same holds for $x\le y$. Here $D=\softO(d^2)$ and also $D\ge d^2$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Sort all occurrences of each color separately, assigning distinct ranks
from $0$ to one less than the number of occurrences of that color. For
strict comparisons, break ties by putting column occurrences before row
occurrences; ties within one side are arbitrary. Thus a row occurrence
$x$ and column occurrence $y$ of the same color satisfy $x<y$ exactly
when their ranks satisfy $r(x)<r(y)$. Every rank is less than $2nd$ and
has a representation in $\ell$ bits, padded with leading zeros.

For bit position $j$, counted from the most significant end, write
$h_j(x)$ for the prefix preceding that bit and $b_j(x)$ for that bit.
Since distinct occurrences have distinct ranks, exactly one first
differing bit exists for every row--column occurrence pair. Therefore
\[
[r(x)<r(y)]=\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}
[h_j(x)=h_j(y),\ b_j(x)=0,\ b_j(y)=1],
\]
and the reversed bit orientation gives $[r(x)>r(y)]$, corresponding to
$x\ge y$. Write $C_<(r,c)$ and $C_{\ge}(r,c)$ for the two exact counts.
Their sum is the number $T(r,c)$ of same-color occurrence pairs.

In case (b), $T(r,c)=|\mathcal R_r|\,|\mathcal C_c|$. In case (a), compute
$T$ at the prescribed positions by multiplying the two color-incidence
matrices. Their inner dimension is at most $d$; zero-pad it to the full
$D$ before applying Theorem~\ref{thm:refined}. This costs one copy of
the claimed preprocessing and query bound. Padding to $D$, rather than
charging the box term at $d$, is important here.

Set $H=d$ in case (a), and $H=d^2$ in case (b), and sieve the primes in
$[P,2P]$, where $P=H\ell^4$. For a sampled prime $p$, replace prefix
equality by equality modulo $p$. More explicitly, for the first
orientation, index the middle coordinate by $(k,j,\sigma)$, where $k$
is the color, $1\le j\le\ell$, and $0\le\sigma<p$, and set
\begin{align*}
X_<[r,(k,j,\sigma)]
 &=\#\{x\in\mathcal R_r:\chi(x)=k,\ b_j(x)=0,
                         \ h_j(x)\equiv\sigma\pmod p\},\\
Y_<[(k,j,\sigma),c]
 &=\#\{y\in\mathcal C_c:\chi(y)=k,\ b_j(y)=1,
                         \ h_j(y)\equiv\sigma\pmod p\}.
\end{align*}
Reverse the bit choices to form $X_{\ge},Y_{\ge}$. The inner dimension
is at most $d\ell p\le2d^2\ell^5$ in case (a), and at most
$\ell p\le2d^2\ell^5$ in case (b); pad to $D$. Each matrix entry is
at most $d$. The two products count the modular matches, so their
answers $U_{<,p}$ and $U_{\ge,p}$ never underestimate $C_<$ and
$C_{\ge}$. Genuine prefix equalities always survive; a false modular
equality contributes only a nonnegative extra term. Each requested
list pair is queried once in each product, with no factor of $p$.

For a fixed list pair, there are at most $H$ same-color occurrence
pairs, hence at most $H\ell$ possible false prefix equalities over
both orientations. Every nonzero prefix difference has magnitude less
than $2nd$, and is divisible by at most $\log(2nd)/\log P$ primes at
least $P$. Since $[P,2P]$ contains $\Omega(P/\log P)$ primes, the
probability that any false contribution occurs for this list pair is
\[
O\left(\frac{H\ell\log(2nd)}P\right)=O(\ell^{-2})\le\frac12
\]
for all sufficiently large $n$. Thus one independently sampled prime
makes both directional answers exact with probability at least $1/2$.
This bound is uniform over the input and the prescribed pairs.

Keep, separately for each pair and direction, the minimum upper count
seen so far. Certify a pair as soon as its two running minima sum to
$T(r,c)$. Both minima are upper bounds on nonnegative exact counts
whose sum is $T(r,c)$, so this equality proves that both answers are
exact. The two minima need not come from the same prime. Continue with
independent primes until every pair certifies. After $t$ rounds, a
fixed pair remains uncertified with probability at most $2^{-t}$; a
union bound and a geometric-tail sum give $O(\log(|\mathcal P|+2))$
expected rounds, without requiring independence between pairs. The
algorithm terminates almost surely and never returns an incorrect count.

Sorting costs $\softO(nd)$, sieving costs $\softO(P)\le\softO(D)$,
and explicitly forming both matrices costs $\softO(nD)$ per round.
Theorem~\ref{thm:refined} gives the remaining terms. Multiplication,
hashing, counters, and ranks use polynomially bounded integers. For
$x\le y$, put row occurrences before column occurrences when values
tie. The identical construction then counts $\le$ and its complement
$>$, and the same total $T$ certifies them. Finitely many small input
sizes can be handled directly.
\end{proof}

Apply the source's Lemma 36 with
\[
(c,\theta,e)=(21.2733,\;0.111731,\;0.056846),\qquad
d=\max\{1,\lfloor n^{e/2}\rfloor\}.
\]
The actual middle dimension is the rounded $D$ of
Lemma~\ref{lem:prefix-count}, so $D=\Theta(n^e\log^5n)$, not literally
$n^e$. The strict tiling inequality $eT<1$ absorbs this polylogarithmic
factor and all rounding. All oracle calls use fresh prime samples,
including when their lists and pair sets depend on earlier calls.

For real 3SUM, the source gives polylogarithmically many comparison
instances of type (b), with $|\mathcal P|=O(n^2/d)$ and
$\softO(n^2/d+nd)$ further work. Their total expected cost is
\[
\softO\left(nD^E+n^2D^{-G}+n^2D^{q-1/2}+nD+n^2D^{-1/2}+nd\right).
\]
For real min-plus, APSP, and Exact Triangle, there are
$\softO(n/d)$ counting calls. Each has $d$ comparison instances of
type (a), whose prescribed pair-set sizes sum to $n^2$. List formation
costs $O(nd^2)$ per counting call. Hence the complete expected bound is
\[
\softO\left(n^2D^E+n^3D^{-G}+n^3D^{q-1/2}
              +n^2D+n^2d+n^3/d\right).
\]
The existing unfolded parameter witness gives
\[
eG=0.003999294616\ldots,\quad
e(1/2-q)=0.003999284085\ldots,\quad
1-eE=0.004000455769\ldots.
\]
All three are strictly greater than $0.003999$; the other displayed
terms have larger savings, and $1-eT=0.004000455769\ldots>0$.
These strict gaps absorb all logarithms, including the expected number
of certification rounds. The source's reductions and the replacement
oracle are both Las Vegas. Consequently real 3SUM takes expected
$O(n^{1.996001})$ time, and real min-plus, APSP, and Exact Triangle take
expected $O(n^{2.996001})$ time, in the same model allowing only
comparisons, additions, and subtractions on real inputs.


\section{Five-term encoding and composite incidence certificates}
\label{sec:five-composite}
This section uses an additional source, Majdoddin~\cite{M26},
Sections 3--7: the five-term partial-product identity, deterministic
re-indexing maps, and squared embedding of Theorem 5.1. We modify its
encoding and query algorithms and use its composite-modulus incidence
certification from Section 15.4. The dependency is separate from the
Alman--Vassilevska Williams construction used above. In particular, the
independent-product batching lemma for the ten-term tensor is not assumed
for this five-term construction. We reproduce the changed cost arguments
and the certification argument below.

Put $D=4^m$, $L=\lceil cm\rceil$, $c>5$, $K=\binom Lm$,
$N_0=4^{L-m}$, and
\[
T_5=c-1+\frac{c\HH(1/c)}{\ln4},\qquad
G_5(c,x)=\frac{c\HH(1/c)-c\HH((1-x)/c)-x\ln4}{\ln2}.
\]
The source's squared embedding has two halves of $L$ coordinates, $m$
inner coordinates in each half, $K_{\rm g}=\Theta(K)$ good sets, and
$2m+1$ degree-split runs. A tile computes the cross products of
$K_{\rm g}$ left and $K_{\rm g}$ right blocks, each of outer size $N_0$.
Its fit condition is $N\ge KN_0=D^{T_5}\operatorname{poly}(m)$.
It does not provide $K_{\rm g}^2$ independent products. The source's
re-indexing maps cost $K(2^m+2^{L-m})\operatorname{poly}(L)$ once;
this is lower order under the stated fit condition.

\begin{lemma}[Compact five-term prefix data structure]
\label{lem:five-prefix}
Fix $0<r\le\theta$, $r+\theta<1$, and
$\theta/(1-r)<3/4$. With $t=\lceil2\theta m\rceil$ and
$\ell=\lfloor rm\rfloor$, the squared five-term construction has
preprocessing time and space
\[
\softO\bigl(ND^{E_5}+N^2D^{-G_5(c,\theta)}\bigr),\qquad
E_5=T_5-G_5(c,r),
\]
and individual exact-integer query time $\softO(D^{q_5})$, where
\[
\begin{split}
q_5&=\max(q_{51},q_{52}),\\
q_{51}&=\frac{2(1-r)\HH(\theta/(1-r))+2\theta\ln3+r\ln8}{\ln4},\\
q_{52}&=\frac{2(1-r)\HH(\theta/(1-r))+2\theta\ln4}{\ln4}.
\end{split}
\]
The original fit condition $N\ge KN_0$ remains necessary.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Label the four nonprivate terms $11,12,21,22$ and the private term $0$.
In the first half their left forms are
\[
f_{11}=x_1+p_1,\quad f_{12}=x_1-p_2,\quad f_{21}=x_2,\quad
f_{22}=x_2+p_2,\quad f_0=-x_1-x_2;
\]
the right forms are $g_{11}=y_1$, $g_{12}=y_2$,
$g_{21}=y_1-q$, $g_{22}=y_2+q$, $g_0=y_1+y_2$.
The second half exchanges left and right. Their sum is the inner kernel
$I=p_1y_1+p_2q$, and the private product is $P=f_0g_0$.
The identities
\[
p_1=f_{11}-f_{12}-f_{22}+f_{21},\quad p_2=f_{22}-f_{21},
\qquad y_1=g_{11},\quad q=g_{11}-g_{21}
\]
compute an $I$ contraction from four nonprivate left forms and two
nonprivate right forms. Successive local transforms shrink each dimension
to two; their work is polynomial in $L$ times the number of source entries.

Choose the first $\ell$ inner coordinates of each half as a balanced
prefix $F$. To make it an initial segment in the box ordering, preprocess
boxes for all $(L+1)^2$ orders determined by two physical cuts: coordinates
up to the respective cuts come first, and all others follow. A query uses
the cuts at its $\ell$-th inner coordinates. This changes cost by only a
polynomial factor. The source's balanced boxes have at most $m$ private
or star symbols per half, at most $2m-t$ in total, and all stars before
private symbols in the chosen order. Replacing the last star by any of
five terms preserves these conditions, so the full dynamic program is
closed. A leaf has at most $2m+1$ preimages for each order.

The prefix identity proved in Lemma~\ref{lem:prefix}, with $2m$ inner
coordinates and four nonprivate labels, reads
\[
I^F\sum_{\substack{A\subseteq Q\setminus F\\|A|<t}}
(-1)^{t-|A|-1}\binom{2m-2\ell-|A|-1}{t-|A|-1}
I^A P^{(Q\setminus F)\setminus A}
\]
for the residual, plus $4^t\binom{2m-2\ell}{t}$ stored boxes.
If $A$ has $a_1,a_2$ positions in the two halves, its input access costs
are bounded by $\operatorname{poly}(L)8^\ell
(4^{a_1}2^{a_2}+2^{a_1}4^{a_2})$.
Since $(1+4x)(1+2x)$ is coefficientwise bounded by $(1+3x)^2$,
all residuals together cost at most
\[
\operatorname{poly}(L)8^\ell
\sum_{j<t}\binom{2(m-\ell)}j3^j.
\]
The cutoff assumption and entropy estimates give $q_{51}$ and $q_{52}$.

Retain only encoded leaves with private counts $j_1,j_2$ satisfying
\[
j_1\le m,\qquad j_2\le m,\qquad j_1+j_2\le2(m-\ell).
\]
Every residual fetch is nonprivate at all $2\ell$ prefix positions;
every box and intermediate box has at most $2m-t\le2(m-\ell)$
private or star positions. Thus all required leaves are retained. A
separate bound $j_i\le m-\ell$ would be insufficient for the boxes.
For the retained count $S$, each retained depth-$d$ prefix has
$4^{2L-d}$ retained all-nonprivate extensions. Hence, if there are $p_d$
such prefixes, $p_d4^{2L-d}\le S$. Every remaining input array has size
at most $4^{2L-d}$, so all internal arrays, their formation, and compact
storage cost $O(LS)$ per run. Dense root initialization costs $12^L\le S$;
no $25^L$ leaf array is allocated.

Writing $\beta_a=\binom L{m-a}4^{L-m+a}$, the geometric ratio is at most
$4m/(L-m+1)<1$. The box count, including its full DP closure, is at most
$\operatorname{poly}(L)\sum_{a_1+a_2\ge t}\beta_{a_1}\beta_{a_2}$.
Concavity of entropy makes its exponential maximum balanced between halves,
so relative to $(KN_0)^2$ its saving is $G_5(c,\theta)$.
The same argument with total deficit $2\ell$ gives
$S=(KN_0)^2D^{-G_5(c,r)}\operatorname{poly}(L)$.
Sharing each band's encodings across tiles yields the claimed $E_5$.
The degree-split runs, box orders, lookups, and exact integer operations
contribute polynomial logarithmic factors. Local coefficients are $0,\pm1$;
encoded sums and the binomial coefficients have polynomial magnitude in
$N$, so arithmetic uses $O(\log N)$ bits.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Fixed-profile inner-basis prefix queries]
\label{lem:five-profile}
Put $D=4^m$, $L=cm+O(1)$, $c>5$, and
\[
 C_K=\frac{c\HH(1/c)}{\ln4},\quad T=c-1+C_K,\quad
 G=\frac{c\HH(1/c)-c\HH((1-\theta)/c)-\theta\ln4}{\ln2}.
\]
Fix $0<r<1$, $0<\theta<1-r$, and a rational $0<y<1$.
Assume $\theta/(1-r)<4/5$. The squared five-term embedding,
restricted to the fixed-profile good sets described below, gives
preprocessing time and space
\[
 \softO\left(ND^{E_{\rm aug}}+ND^{E_{\rm box}}+N^2D^{-G}\right),
 \qquad
 E_{\rm aug}=T-r(C_K-3/2),\quad E_{\rm box}=T-G,
\]
and individual query time $\softO(D^{\max(q_1,q_2)})$, where
\[
 q_1=r+\frac{(1-r)\ln((1+3y)(1+2y))-2\theta\ln y}{\ln4},
 \qquad
 q_2=\frac{2(1-r)\HH(\theta/(1-r))+2\theta\ln4}{\ln4}.
\]
The original tile condition $N\ge KN_0$, with $K=\binom Lm$ and
$N_0=4^{L-m}$, remains sufficient; it has exponent $T$. There is no independent-product batching assumption.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Let $\ell=rm+O(1)$ and $L_{\rm pre}=rL+O(1)$, rounded to integers.
Keep the inner sets $Q$ satisfying
\[
 |Q\cap[L_{\rm pre}]|=\ell,\qquad |Q|=m.
\]
Their number is
\[
 K'=\binom{L_{\rm pre}}\ell\binom{L-L_{\rm pre}}{m-\ell}
     =\binom Lm/\operatorname{poly}(m).
\]
The two regional densities equal $1/c+O(1/m)$. Stirling's inequalities
give the displayed comparison, including any bounded rounding changes.
For every fixed set in this family, the source's independent binary
re-indexing maps make it good with probability
$\mu_m\mu_{L-m}>\mu_\infty^2$. The conditional-expectation algorithm
may sum over this explicit family instead of all $m$-sets. Each summand
and each conditional-rank computation is unchanged, so it deterministically
finds $\Theta(K')$ good sets in
$K'(2^m+2^{L-m})\operatorname{poly}(L)$ time. The source's squared
embedding and its degree-split runs accept this list of good sets.
Thus the outer tile size is $\Theta(K')N_0$, where $N_0=4^{L-m}$;
replacing $K'$ by $K=\binom Lm$ costs only polynomial factors.

Use the source's local forms $f_{ij},g_{ij},f_0,g_0$, with the second
half transposed. Set $I=p_1y_1+p_2q$ and $P=f_0g_0$.
The query prefix $F$ is exactly the inner positions in the fixed physical
prefix of each half, hence $|F|=2\ell$. Add two normal-side input forms
$p_1,p_2$ to the encoder. On the opposite side retain the ordinary forms
$g_{11},g_{21}$ and use $y_1=g_{11}$, $q=g_{11}-g_{21}$.
Each input side therefore requires only $4^\ell$ prefix source
combinations. These are actual extra
stored forms; their entire construction is charged below.

For the suffix use
\[
 p_1=f_{11}+f_0+f_{21},\qquad p_2=f_{22}-f_{21},
 \qquad y_1=g_{11},\qquad q=g_{11}-g_{21}.
\]
At a normal-side coordinate the sources are the private form and three
ordinary forms; at an opposite-side coordinate they are the private form
and two ordinary forms. The outputs are the private form and two inner
coordinates. Private outputs depend only on private inputs, while an inner
output can depend on private or ordinary inputs. Thus every predecessor
of an output with at most $j$ nonprivate coordinates also has at most $j$
nonprivate coordinates. Apply the tensor of these fixed local transforms
on the truncated grid, generating only grid states of degree below
$t=2\theta m+O(1)$. Its source count, including both input sides, is
at most a polynomial in $L$ times
\[
 4^\ell\sum_{a+b<t}\binom{m-\ell}a\binom{m-\ell}b3^a2^b.
\]
The predecessor-closure property bounds every intermediate transform
grid by the same source count up to a polynomial factor. In particular,
one must not enumerate the entire untruncated Cartesian grid first.
Multiply matching transformed output entries and weight every degree-$j$
entry by
\[
 (-1)^{t-j-1}\binom{2(m-\ell)-j-1}{t-j-1}.
\]
The prefix partition identity then gives the exact residual. Outputs
with different inner subsets are multiplied only with matching outputs;
no cross-subset terms are introduced. Since $0<y<1$, the positive
generating polynomial bounds this source count by
\[
 \operatorname{poly}(L)4^\ell y^{-t}
       ((1+3y)(1+2y))^{m-\ell},
\]
which has exponent $q_1$.

The complementary part of the partition uses
$4^t\binom{2(m-\ell)}t$ stored boxes, giving $q_2$.
Order the physical prefixes of the two halves first, then their suffixes.
The stars in $F$ precede every remaining inner position in this one fixed
order. The usual balanced box family, with at most $m$ private-or-star
positions per half, at most $2m-t$ in total, and all stars before private
positions, contains every required box. Replacing the last star by any
ordinary or private term preserves the family, including its intermediate
boxes. A completed leaf has at most $2m+1$ preimages in this family.
The canonical balanced-tail bound therefore gives box cost
$\softO((KN_0)^2D^{-G})$ per tile. This uses the full-family bound,
so it requires no assertion that restricting to profile sets removes boxes.

Two distinct encoders are charged. The ordinary one supplies box base
cases and retains private counts at most $m$ in each half and at most
$2m-t$ in total. Its cost is $\softO((KN_0)^2D^{-G})$ per band.
The augmented query-source encoder has, on its normal half, four ordinary
labels and at most $\ell$ projection labels in the physical prefix, with
no private label there. Its suffix has four ordinary labels and at most
$m-\ell$ private labels. On its opposite half the prefix allows all four
ordinary labels but no private label; the suffix again allows at most
$m-\ell$ private labels. This includes every source just used, including
sources of smaller intermediate degree.

Define $U(n,b,w)=\sum_{j\le b}\binom njw^j4^{n-j}$.
The normal and opposite half counts are respectively
\[
 A=U(L_{\rm pre},\ell,2)U(L-L_{\rm pre},m-\ell,1),\qquad
 B=4^{L_{\rm pre}}U(L-L_{\rm pre},m-\ell,1).
\]
The ratios before their terminal summands are bounded away from one:
asymptotically they are at most $2/(c-1)$ and $4/(c-1)$.
Consequently
\[
 A=O(2^\ell K'N_0),\qquad
 \frac{AB}{(K'N_0)^2}=O\left(\frac{8^\ell}{\binom{L_{\rm pre}}\ell}\right)
      =\softO(D^{-r(C_K-3/2)}).
\]
Incomplete projection prefixes are retained. Every retained prefix in
each encoder has every all-ordinary completion, so its full remaining
array of dimension at most $4^{2L-d}$ is charged to that many distinct
retained descendants. Summing over depths charges every internal array,
its formation, and compact storage. Both encoders include all ordinary
leaves; dense root initialization $12^L$ is smaller than $16^L$ and is
therefore included. The two input sides use transposed block orders.

Sharing each band's encodings across the ordinary grid of tiles converts
these two per-band costs into $ND^{E_{\rm box}}$ and $ND^{E_{\rm aug}}$,
up to polynomial factors in $L$. The full box count becomes $N^2D^{-G}$.
The independent-product layout is never used. The fixed degree-split
runs, source re-indexing maps, integer bit costs, and rounding contribute
polynomial logarithmic factors under the original tile condition.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Rectangular five-term products]
\label{lem:five-rect-tiles}
Under the hypotheses and notation of Lemma~\ref{lem:five-profile}, let
$X\in\mathbb Z^{R\times D}$ and $Y\in\mathbb Z^{D\times C}$ have entries of
magnitude polynomial in $R+C+D$, and suppose
$\min\{R,C\}\ge KN_0\operatorname{poly}(m)=D^T\operatorname{poly}(\log D)$.
Then the fixed-profile five-term data structure preprocesses $XY$ in time
and space
\[
\softO\bigl((R+C)(D^{E_{\rm aug}}+D^{E_{\rm box}})+RC\,D^{-G}\bigr)
\]
and returns any single entry in $\softO(D^{\max(q_1,q_2)})$ time.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Pad $R$ and $C$ upward to multiples of $K_{\rm g}N_0$; the hypothesis makes
this at most a doubling. Partition the rows of $X$ into $R/(K_{\rm g}N_0)$
row bands and the columns of $Y$ into $C/(K_{\rm g}N_0)$ column bands, each
consisting of $K_{\rm g}$ blocks of $N_0$ rows or columns. A tile is one row
band together with one column band; it is exactly the
$K_{\rm g}\times K_{\rm g}$ grid of $N_0$-block products to which the squared
embedding, the fixed-profile good sets, the degree-split runs, the box
family, and the prefix partition identity of Lemma~\ref{lem:five-profile}
apply verbatim, since that lemma's tile is the same grid with
$N=K_{\rm g}N_0$. A left encoding depends only on its row band and a right
encoding only on its column band. Both encoder families are therefore
computed once per band and shared across the tiles of that band, with
transposed block orders on the two sides: the ordinary encoder costs
$\softO((KN_0)^2D^{-G})$ per band and the augmented encoder
$\softO((KN_0)^2D^{-r(C_K-3/2)})$ per band, by the bounds $S$ and $AB$ in
that proof, which charge every internal array and its compact storage.
Multiplying by the $(R+C)/(KN_0)$ bands gives $(R+C)D^{E_{\rm box}}$ and
$(R+C)D^{E_{\rm aug}}$. The balanced box family of one tile costs
$\softO((KN_0)^2D^{-G})$ and there are $RC/(KN_0)^2$ tiles, giving
$RCD^{-G}$. A query for the entry $(i,j)$ lies in one tile; its residual
multiplies the retained truncated sources of the row band of $i$ and the
column band of $j$, and its complementary part reads stored boxes of that
tile, so the two query exponents are unchanged. The re-indexing maps cost
$K(2^m+2^{L-m})\operatorname{poly}(L)\le\min\{R,C\}\operatorname{poly}(L)$
once. No independent-product batching is used, and no $K_1\times K_2$ grid
with $K_1\ne K_2$ arises: restricting the good sets of one side is
equivalent to padding that side.
\end{proof}


\begin{lemma}[Composite incidence certification]
\label{lem:composite-incidence}
Suppose a deterministic counting oracle computes, for any modulus
$d\le h\operatorname{polylog}(n)$ and any chosen set of incidences
$x=(a,b,B)$ with $|B|\le h=\Theta(n^b)$, where $0<b<1$, the exact number $C_d(x)$ of
vertices $v\in B$ for which $d$ divides the original triangle sum.
If every such call, including writing and reading its data, costs
$\softO(n^{3-s})$, then for every fixed integer $R\ge1$ satisfying the
parameter choices below, all-edges Exact Triangle has deterministic time
$\softO(n^{3-s+b/R}+n^2)$. The polylogarithmic power may depend on $R$
and the polynomial input-magnitude bound.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
This is the per-incidence construction of \cite[Section 15.4]{M26}.
We give its proof to specify the oracle interface and account for true
witnesses. Let $Z(x)$ count genuine zero sums and $F_d(x)=C_d(x)-Z(x)$.
If every nonzero triangle sum has magnitude at most $U'\ge1$, put
$\zeta=\max(1,\ln U')$ and choose
$P\ge4\zeta(4h)^{1/R}$, also $P\ge8\zeta$ and sufficiently large.
Enumerate primes in $[P,2P)$ deterministically. After removing fewer than
$R$ previously chosen primes, let $M$ denote the actual number of remaining candidates; then
$M\ge P/(4\ln P)$ for all sufficiently large $n$.
Each nonzero sum has at most $\lambda=\lfloor\ln U'/\ln P\rfloor$
candidate prime divisors. Thus $\lambda/M\le4\zeta/P=:a$.
The finitely many smaller $n$ are handled by brute force.

Fix the active incidence set $\mathcal I$ for one stage. Start at $d=1$.
For $R-1$ steps compute all $C_{dp}(x)$ for every unused candidate prime,
choose $p$ minimizing their sum over $\mathcal I$, and replace $d$ by $dp$.
The true mass $\sum Z(x)$ is independent of $p$, so the false mass
contracts by at most $a$ at each step. Consequently
$\sum_{x\in\mathcal I}F_d(x)\le h|\mathcal I|a^{R-1}$.
Retain the selected modulus's individual counts, not just their total.
For $R=1$, initialize $C_1(x)=|B|$ without an oracle call.

In the last round compute $\mu(x)=\min_p C_{dp}(x)$. Discard an incidence
as false if $\mu(x)=0$; certify its pair YES if
\[
M\mu(x)>\lambda C_d(x).
\]
The first rule is sound since $\mu\ge Z$. The second is sound since a
zero-free incidence has
$M\mu\le\sum_pF_{dp}\le\lambda F_d=\lambda C_d$.
For an uncertified incidence, $\mu\ge1$ and
$M\mu\le\lambda(Z+F_d)$. If $Z=0$ this implies
$\lambda F_d\ge M$; if $Z\ge1$, using $\mu\ge Z$ implies
$\lambda F_d\ge(M-\lambda)Z\ge M-\lambda$.
Therefore the next active set, after also removing certified pairs, has
size at most
\[
\frac{\lambda}{M-\lambda}h|\mathcal I|a^{R-1}
\le 2h|\mathcal I|a^R\le |\mathcal I|/2.
\]
Here $a\le1/2$ and $h a^R\le1/4$ by the choice of $P$.
A genuine witness is never discarded unless its pair is certified YES.

Restart with $d=1$ on the new active set and repeat
$\lceil\log_2(2n)\rceil$ stages. Initially there are at most $2n^3/h$
incidences. Scanning every remaining block then costs $O(n^2)$ and answers
all remaining pairs exactly. Each candidate modulus is a product of at
most $R$ distinct primes, hence at most $(2P)^R=h\operatorname{polylog}(n)$
for fixed $R$; choose $P$ within a constant factor of its stated threshold.
There are $\softO(n^{b/R})$ candidate primes and $O(R\log n)$ rounds.
A sieve, comparisons, minima, and survivor bookkeeping are charged, along
with every candidate count call. Moduli and counts have $O(\log n)$ bits.
No CRT tuples of different witnesses or randomized tests are used.
\end{proof}
\begin{lemma}[Lopsided composite incidence certification]
\label{lem:composite-incidence-lopsided}
Let the two outer parts have $R$ and $C$ vertices and the middle part $H$
vertices, all polynomial in an ambient size $n$, with integer weights of
magnitude $n^{O(1)}$, and let $h=\Theta(n^b)$ with $b>0$ and $h\le H$.
Suppose a deterministic counting oracle computes, for any modulus
$d\le h\operatorname{polylog}(n)$ and any set $\mathcal I$ of incidences
$x=(a,b,B)$ with $|B|\le h$, the exact numbers $C_d(x)$ of $v\in B$ with
$d\mid S(a,b,v)$, every call costing at most $\softO(\Gamma)$ including
writing and reading its data. Then for every fixed integer $\Lambda\ge1$,
all-edges Exact Triangle on this instance has deterministic time
\[
\softO\bigl(n^{b/\Lambda}(\Gamma+RCH/h)+RC\bigr),
\]
where the polylogarithmic power depends on $\Lambda$ and the magnitude bound.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Repeat the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:composite-incidence} with the following
replacements. The initial active set has at most
$RC\lceil H/h\rceil\le2RCH/h$ incidences. Each stage halves the active set,
so after $\lceil\log_2(2H)\rceil$ stages at most $RC/h$ incidences remain,
and scanning their blocks with the original integer sums costs $O(RC)$.
Every stage performs $\Lambda$ rounds, each evaluating all
$\softO(n^{b/\Lambda})$ candidate primes with one oracle call per candidate
and $O(|\mathcal I|)$ bookkeeping per candidate for the sums, minima,
comparisons, and survivor lists. The prime pool in $[P,2P)$ with
$P\ge4\zeta(4h)^{1/\Lambda}$ and $P\ge8\zeta$, the bound $\lambda$ on the
prime divisors of a nonzero sum, the contraction $ha^\Lambda\le1/4$, the
soundness of both rules, and the leak bound are statements about single
incidences and do not depend on the three part sizes. Every modulus is a
product of at most $\Lambda$ distinct primes below $2P$, hence at most
$(2P)^\Lambda=h\operatorname{polylog}(n)$. A genuine witness is never
discarded unless its pair is certified YES, so all $RC$ answers are exact.
Finitely many small $n$ use brute force.
\end{proof}
Theorem~\ref{thm:cyclic-factor-apsp} below inherits the same lopsided
reading, since its oracle costs depend only on $(N,D,q)$ with
$N=R\lceil\sqrt d\rceil$ on both sides; Corollary~\ref{cor:lopsided-five-clique}
uses it for cliques.

The residue-folding construction above extends from primes to every integer
modulus $d\ge2$: it uses only congruence and the digit representation
$\rho_1+\lceil\sqrt d\rceil\rho_2$, without division or inversion in a field.
It computes the exact modular count by an ordinary integer product.
For a block of size $h$ the folded dimensions are
$D=hd$, $N=n\lceil\sqrt d\rceil$, and each active incidence requests one
entry. Pad $D$ to the next power of four and $N$ to complete tiles; strict
fit slack absorbs the resulting constants. In particular no fixed query
density is needed: use preprocessing plus the actual number of queries.

\begin{corollary}[An explicit five-term deterministic APSP exponent]
\label{cor:five-apsp}
Using the five-term embedding of \cite{M26}, integer all-edges Exact
Triangle, min-plus product, and APSP admit deterministic
$O(n^{2.993676})$ algorithms. Inputs have polynomially bounded integer
weights and APSP has no negative cycles.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
Apply Lemma~\ref{lem:five-profile} and the composite incidence
certification of Lemma~\ref{lem:composite-incidence} with the exact rational parameters
\[
\begin{gathered}
(c,\theta,r,e,y)=
(10.74132662,\ 0.056740653,\ 0.038571087,\ 0.083812911,\ 0.025146880),\\
b=e/2=0.0419064555.
\end{gathered}
\]
Restrict every slot to exactly $rm+O(1)$ inner coordinates among the first
$rcm+O(1)$ physical coordinates. The slot count differs from $K$ only by
a polynomial factor. Put
\[
C_K=\frac{c\HH(1/c)}{\ln4},\qquad T=C_K+c-1,\qquad
G=\frac{c\HH(1/c)-c\HH((1-\theta)/c)-\theta\ln4}{\ln2}.
\]
Charge the two encoder families separately:
\[
E_{\rm aug}=T-r(C_K-3/2),\qquad E_{\rm box}=T-G.
\]
The direct-prefix residual and stored-box queries have respective exponents
\[
q_1=r+\frac{(1-r)\ln((1+3y)(1+2y))-2\theta\ln y}{\ln4},\qquad
q_2=\frac{2(1-r)\HH(\theta/(1-r))+2\theta\ln4}{\ln4}.
\]
The exact-rational verifier gives
\[
\begin{array}{ll}
T=12.140552723405380\ldots,&G=0.075467246110185\ldots,\\
E_{\rm aug}=12.105868595138261\ldots,&E_{\rm box}=12.065085477295195\ldots,\\
q_1=0.424532754092257\ldots,&q_2=0.424532753969640\ldots.
\end{array}
\]
Use $R=100000$ distinct factors per composite. Candidate enumeration costs
exponent $\eta=b/R=0.000000419064555$. At the final modulus scale $n^b$,
the savings below exponent three are
\[
\begin{array}{lll}
\text{boxes}&eG-\eta&>0.0063247105170,\\
\text{residual queries}&e(1/2-q_1)-\eta&>0.0063247105001,\\
\text{stored queries}&e(1/2-q_2)-\eta&>0.0063247105104,\\
\text{augmented encodings}&1-e(E_{\rm aug}-1/4)-\eta&>0.0063247215434,\\
\text{ordinary box encodings}&1-e(E_{\rm box}-1/4)-\eta&>0.0097428733695,\\
\text{matrix formation}&1-3e/4-\eta&>0.9371398976854,\\
\text{incidence bookkeeping}&b-\eta&>0.0419060364354.
\end{array}
\]
The unchanged geometric margin is
$1-e(T-1/4)>0.0034181628524$.
For intermediate modulus $d=n^u$, $0\le u\le b$ up to polylogarithms,
partition the middle vertices into $O(n/h)$ blocks of size
$h=\Theta(n^b)$. There are $O(n/h)$ folded products with dimensions
$D=hd$ and $N=n\lceil\sqrt d\rceil$, and at most $O(n^3/h)$
queries across them. Multiplying the preprocessing and query costs in
Lemma~\ref{lem:five-profile} by these counts gives savings
$b-u+(b+u)G$ for boxes, $b-(b+u)q_i$ for queries,
$1+b-u/2-(b+u)E_j$ for each encoder, and $1-3u/2$ for formation,
before subtracting $\eta$. All decrease with $u$, as does the fit margin
$1+u/2-(b+u)T$, so the displayed final-scale checks cover every call.

Every runtime saving exceeds $\delta=0.006324$ with a strict gap greater
than $7.1\cdot10^{-7}$. This absorbs all fixed-$R$ logarithmic factors,
profile rounding, integer bit costs, bit scaling, and APSP squaring.
The data-structure lemma already charges both encoder families and all
intermediate arrays. The exact all-edges interface supplies the previously
proved min-plus and APSP transfers. This proves $O(n^{2.993676})$.
The result does not change the separately analyzed clique or counting
claims, and does not establish a bound below $2.99$.
\end{proof}

\subsection*{Splitting the modulus between geometry and coefficients}

\begin{lemma}[Zero-prefix five-term queries over cyclic coefficient rings]
\label{lem:five-zero-prefix-ring}
Put $D=4^m$, $L=cm+O(1)$, $c>5$, and
\[
 C_K=\frac{c\HH(1/c)}{\ln4},\qquad T=c-1+C_K,\qquad
 G=\frac{c\HH(1/c)-c\HH((1-\theta)/c)-\theta\ln4}{\ln2}.
\]
Fix $0<\theta<4/5$ and a rational $0<y<1$, and set
\[
 q_1=\frac{\ln((1+3y)(1+2y))-2\theta\ln y}{\ln4},\qquad
 q_2=\frac{2\HH(\theta)+2\theta\ln4}{\ln4}.
\]
Let $N,D,q\le n^{O(1)}$, $q\ge1$, and assume every input polynomial
has coefficient $\ell_1$ norm at most $n^{O(1)}$. Over
$\mathcal R_q=\mathbb Z[x]/(x^q-1)$, the source's full-grid five-term embedding
preprocesses $N\times D$ by $D\times N$ matrices over $\mathcal R_q$ in
\[
 \softO\left(qND^T+qN^2D^{-G}+qND\right)
\]
time and space, under the original layout condition $N\ge KN_0$, where
$K=\binom Lm$, $N_0=4^{L-m}$; the sufficient exponent condition is
$N\ge D^T\operatorname{poly}(\log D)$.
Given a product position and a specified residue $j\bmod q$, its
coefficient can be queried in
\[
 \softO\left(qD^{q_1}+D^{q_2}\right).
\]
For the monomial inputs below, all stored coefficients and exact
arithmetic intermediates have $O(\log n)$ bits. There is no
independent-product batching hypothesis.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
This is the zero-prefix endpoint of the sparse-Yates query construction,
with its two query terms charged separately. Use all good inner sets;
there is no profile restriction and no augmented projection encoder.
Write $t=2\theta m+O(1)$. The local identities
\[
 p_1=f_{11}+f_0+f_{21},\qquad p_2=f_{22}-f_{21},\qquad
 y_1=g_{11},\qquad q_{\rm loc}=g_{11}-g_{21}
\]
give the same suffix transform as before, on all $2m$ inner coordinates.
Its truncated source count is bounded by
\[
 \operatorname{poly}(L)\sum_{a+b<t}\binom ma\binom mb3^a2^b
 \le \operatorname{poly}(L)y^{-t}((1+3y)(1+2y))^m.
\]
The private output uses only the private source, and an inner output uses
private or nonprivate sources. Consequently every predecessor of a
retained output has no greater nonprivate degree; all intermediate
transform arrays lie in the same truncated family. No full Cartesian
array is formed first. Multiply only matching transformed outputs; a degree-$j$ output receives
weight $(-1)^{t-j-1}\binom{2m-j-1}{t-j-1}$, as in
Lemma~\ref{lem:five-profile}. The partition identity gives the exact
residual, with exponent $q_1$.
The complementary part uses $4^t\binom{2m}{t}$ stored boxes, giving
$q_2$. This argument permits prefix length zero directly; it does not
invoke a limiting positive prefix whose parameters depend on $n$.

The ordinary encoder retains at most $m$ private labels in each half.
Every retained depth-$k$ prefix has all $4^{2L-k}$ ordinary completions,
which pay for its entire remaining input array. Summing over depths
charges formation and compact storage; the dense root size $12^L$ is
below the retained all-ordinary count $16^L$. Its leaf and internal-array bound is
$\softO((KN_0)^2)$ per band, where $K=\binom Lm$ and $N_0=4^{L-m}$.
The full balanced box family, including its dynamic-program closure,
has $\softO((KN_0)^2D^{-G})$ states per tile. Sharing encodings across
the ordinary grid of tiles therefore gives scalar costs $ND^T$ and
$N^2D^{-G}$. The re-indexing maps and fixed degree-split runs have the
same polynomial-logarithmic overhead as in the scalar construction.

Every local identity, encoder transform, box recurrence, and query
identity is an integer linear or bilinear identity. It therefore remains
valid over the commutative ring $\mathcal R_q$. Store a ring element as
its $q$ coefficients. Linear operations cost $O(q)$ word operations;
cyclic products cost $\softO(q)$ by exact integer polynomial arithmetic.
For example, split signs and use Kronecker substitution with an
$O(\log n)$-bit radix exceeding the convolution coefficient bound,
multiply the resulting $O(q\log n)$-bit integers, and fold degrees
modulo $q$.
This proves the preprocessing bound, including the conservative charge
$qND$ for forming and initializing the ring-valued input arrays.

The output coefficient map is linear. In the stored-box portion, read
only coefficient $j$ of each requested box and apply its scalar query
weight. This costs one coefficient access per box, with no factor $q$.
For the residual, perform its truncated linear transforms on all
coefficients, costing $O(q)$ per retained source or intermediate entry.
For each matching pair of transformed entries, compute only coefficient
$j$ of their cyclic product, by a length-$q$ dot product. The residual
therefore costs $\softO(qD^{q_1})$; it does not require extracting the
whole output polynomial.

For completeness, the ring extension does not multiply bit lengths at
every recursion level. Input coefficient norms are polynomially bounded; in the application
below each entry is zero or a monomial of coefficient one. A fixed local linear transform increases coefficient
$\ell_1$ norm by a constant factor, and there are $O(L)=O(\log n)$
levels. Each encoded input thus has polynomial norm. Each leaf performs
one bilinear cyclic product; cyclic reduction cannot increase
$\ell_1$ norm, and the product norm is at most the product of input
norms. Subsequent box recurrences are linear, with polynomially many
summands. Query transforms and binomial query weights likewise have
polynomial norm. Formal degree-split vectors are only polynomially long
and preserve these bounds. Thus coefficients and intermediates have
polynomial magnitude, hence $O(\log n)$ bits. This charges the internal
encodings as well as the retained leaves.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[A folded modulus with an unfurled cyclic factor]
\label{lem:folded-cyclic-factor}
Let $d,q\ge1$ be coprime. Partition the middle vertices into blocks of
size at most $h$. Fold only the modulus $d$ into the outer indices, so
$d_1=\lceil\sqrt d\rceil$, $d_2=\lceil d/d_1\rceil$ and the middle
indices are $(v,\sigma)\in B\times\mathbb Z_d$. Over $\mathcal R_q$
use the entries
\begin{align*}
 X[(a,\rho_1),(v,\sigma)]
 &=\boldsymbol1_{\sigma\equiv w(a,v)+\rho_1\ (d)}\,x^{w(a,v)\bmod q},\\
 Y[(v,\sigma),(b,\rho_2)]
 &=\boldsymbol1_{\sigma\equiv-w(v,b)-d_1\rho_2\ (d)}\,x^{w(v,b)\bmod q}.
\end{align*}
For $w(a,b)\bmod d=\rho_1+d_1\rho_2$, coefficient
$-w(a,b)\bmod q$ at this product position is exactly the number of
vertices $v\in B$ for which $dq$ divides the original triangle sum.
The dimensions are $N=n\lceil\sqrt d\rceil$ and $D=hd$, with one
coefficient query per requested incidence.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
For a fixed $v$, the two indicator entries have exactly one common
$\sigma$ if and only if the original sum is zero modulo $d$.
Its monomial product has exponent $w(a,v)+w(v,b)$ modulo $q$; the
designated coefficient selects exactly those sums that are also zero
modulo $q$. Coprimality makes these two tests equivalent to divisibility
by $dq$. Negative input weights are reduced to their usual residues.
Padded middle vertices are absent, represented by zero entries.
The special cases $d=1$ or $q=1$ follow from the same formulas.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Composite incidence certification with cyclic factors]
\label{thm:cyclic-factor-apsp}
Fix integers $R>S\ge1$, a rational $0<b<1$, and parameters of
Lemma~\ref{lem:five-zero-prefix-ring}. Put
\[
 a=b/R,\qquad u=Sb/R,\qquad v=b-u,\qquad e=b+u.
\]
Suppose $0<G<1$, $q_1,q_2>0$, $T>1/2$, and the following strict
inequalities hold for some $\delta>0$:
\begin{align*}
 eG-a&>\delta,& u-eq_1-a&>\delta,& b-eq_2-a&>\delta,\\
 1+u/2-eT-a&>\delta,&1-b-u/2-a&>\delta,&b-a&>\delta,
 \qquad 1+u/2-eT>0.
\end{align*}
Then integer all-edges Exact Triangle has deterministic time
$O(n^{3-\delta})$. The established deterministic transfers give the same
exponent for integer min-plus product and APSP without negative cycles.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Use blocks of size $h=\Theta(n^b)$ and exactly the candidate-prime pool
and certification rule of Lemma~\ref{lem:composite-incidence}, with $R$
factors. Its primes have scale
$P=n^{b/R}\operatorname{polylog}(n)$, with the logarithmic power fixed.
Within each halving stage the active incidence set stays fixed while the
greedy candidates are evaluated. For the first $S$ selected factors, use
ordinary scalar folded calls. Let their product be $d$. Freeze this
folded modulus for the rest of the stage. Start $q=1$ and accumulate the
remaining selected primes into $q$, evaluating each candidate $qp$
through Lemma~\ref{lem:folded-cyclic-factor}. The final certification
round evaluates $R$-factor moduli $dqp$, without needing to select its
last prime. Since primes are distinct within a stage, $d$ and every
candidate cyclic factor remain coprime.

The exponent of the frozen modulus is exactly $u=Sb/R$, up to
polylogarithmic factors; the largest cyclic factor has exponent
$v=(R-S)b/R$. This equality is part of the parameter choice, not a
continuous relaxation of the number of factors. Rebuilding the folded
or ring-valued encodings separately for every candidate is fully charged;
no reuse of a candidate's input arrays or boxes is assumed. The original
certification argument applies unchanged because every returned number
is the exact count for the full candidate modulus. It halves all active
incidences, including true incidences, and $O(\log n)$ restarts leave
$O(n^2)$ final scanning work.

There are $O(n/h)$ folded products and at most $O(n^3/h)$ requested
incidences in any call, even before any contraction occurs. During the
first phase let the current scalar folded modulus have exponent $s$,
where $0\le s\le u$. Before candidate enumeration the savings below
exponent three are
\[
\begin{array}{ll}
\text{boxes}&b-s+(b+s)G,\\
\text{residual and stored-box queries}&b-(b+s)q_i\quad(i=1,2),\\
\text{encodings}&1+b-s/2-(b+s)T,\\
\text{matrix formation}&1-3s/2,\\
\text{incidence bookkeeping}&b.
\end{array}
\]
The layout margin is $1+s/2-(b+s)T$. All these expressions are minimized
at $s=u$, using the sign hypotheses.

During the second phase $N=\widetilde\Theta(n^{1+u/2})$ and
$D=\widetilde\Theta(n^e)$ are fixed. For a cyclic factor of exponent
$w$, $0\le w\le v$, the corresponding savings are
\[
\begin{array}{ll}
\text{boxes}&b-u+eG-w,\\
\text{residual queries}&b-eq_1-w,\\
\text{stored-box coefficient reads}&b-eq_2,\\
\text{encodings}&1+b-u/2-eT-w,\\
\text{matrix formation}&1-3u/2-w,\\
\text{incidence bookkeeping}&b.
\end{array}
\]
The formation line includes the entire $qND$ ring-input allocation for
every block. These expressions are minimized at $w=v$, except the two
constant lines. Substituting $v=b-u$ yields precisely the six runtime
inequalities in the theorem. The first phase's endpoint is the second
phase's $w=0$ endpoint, so no earlier large call is omitted. The original
full-grid layout is checked separately by $1+u/2-eT>0$.

There are $\softO(n^a)$ candidate primes per round and $O(R\log n)$
rounds. Subtracting $a$ from every runtime saving charges all candidate
calls, including formation, extraction, comparisons, and survivor lists.
A sieve costs $\softO(n^a)$ and is smaller than these bounds. The prime
products, ring dimensions, residues, and actual witness counts have
$O(\log n)$ bits; Lemma~\ref{lem:five-zero-prefix-ring} additionally
charges encoded arithmetic. All polynomial logarithmic factors have
fixed powers because $R,S,c,\theta,y,b$ are fixed constants. Strict
slack absorbs these factors, rounding of block and tensor sizes, and the
min-plus and APSP transfers. No density assumption about the active
queries or folded residue classes is used.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[An explicit cyclic-factor deterministic exponent]
\label{cor:five-ring-apsp}
Use
\[
 (c,\theta,y,b)=(10.67333763,\ 0.0590858825,\ 0.025178142,\ 0.04368276),
 \qquad (R,S)=(100000,92277).
\]
Then
\[
 u=0.0403091404452,\quad v=0.0033736195548,\quad
 e=0.0839919004452,\quad a=0.0000004368276.
\]
The exact-rational certificate verifies every first- and second-phase
endpoint and the parameter conditions. Its smallest runtime saving is
greater than $0.0065597670136$, while the original layout margin is
greater than $0.0065608839$. Consequently all-edges Exact Triangle,
integer min-plus product, and integer APSP admit deterministic
$O(n^{2.993441})$ algorithms under the source five-term embedding and the
stated integer-weight assumptions. The gap above the target saving
$0.006559$ exceeds $7.67\cdot10^{-7}$. This does not establish a bound
below $2.99$.
\end{corollary}

\subsection*{Caching a partial set of inner projections}

\begin{lemma}[Global partial inner-projection cache]
\label{lem:five-partial-cache}
Keep the five-term embedding and the exponents $T$ and $G$ of
Lemma~\ref{lem:five-zero-prefix-ring}. Fix
$0<\theta<2/3$, $0<\rho<\min\{2\theta,2/3\}$, and $0<y<1$.
Define
\begin{align*}
 C_\rho&=\frac{\HH(\rho)+\rho\ln2}{\ln4},& E&=T+C_\rho,\\
 q_{\rm in}&=\frac{2\HH(\theta)+2\theta\ln2}{\ln4},&
 q_{\rm old}&=\frac{\ln((1+3y)(1+2y))-2\theta\ln y}{\ln4},\\
 q_{\rm hi}&=q_{\rm old}-\frac{\rho\ln(3/2)}{\ln4},&
 q_1&=\max\{q_{\rm in},q_{\rm hi}\},\\
 q_2&=\frac{2\HH(\theta)+2\theta\ln4}{\ln4}.
\end{align*}
Under the original condition $N\ge KN_0$, the modified data structure
over $\mathcal R_q=\mathbb Z[x]/(x^q-1)$ has preprocessing and storage
\[
 \softO\left(qND^E+qN^2D^{-G}+qND\right)
\]
and a specified coefficient query costs
$\softO(qD^{q_1}+D^{q_2})$. The input-size and coefficient-norm assumptions
are those of Lemma~\ref{lem:five-zero-prefix-ring}. This cache is allowed
at arbitrary physical positions; it does not restrict the good inner
sets to a fixed physical prefix.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Let $t=\lceil2\theta m\rceil$ and $\ell=\lfloor\rho m\rfloor$.
In an input's normal half, augment the four ordinary forms and the
private form $P=f_0$ with the two direct projections $p_1,p_2$.
Retain words with at most $m$ private-or-projection positions and at most
$\ell$ projection positions. In the opposite half retain the old words
with at most $m$ private positions. The other input exchanges its normal
and opposite halves. The projections are actual extra stored values.

Put
\[
 U=\sum_{k=0}^{m}\binom Lk4^{L-k},\qquad
 C_\ell=\sum_{a=0}^{\ell}\binom ma2^a.
\]
The normal-half leaf count is
\[
 A_\ell=\sum_{k=0}^{m}\binom Lk4^{L-k}
                   \sum_{a=0}^{\min\{k,\ell\}}\binom ka2^a.
\]
Here $k$ specifies all private-or-projection positions and $a$ specifies
the projected ones. Thus each input's leaf count is
$A_\ell U\le U^2C_\ell$.
Since $c>5$, the ordinary tail is bounded by a geometric series ending
at $k=m$, so $U=\softO(KN_0)$. Since $\rho<2/3$,
$C_\ell=\softO(D^{C_\rho})$.
Every retained depth-$d$ prefix has all $4^{2L-d}$ ordinary completions.
These completion sets are disjoint for different prefixes at a fixed
depth and pay for each entire remaining input array, whose local
dimensions are at most four. There are at most seven constant-size
child forms. Therefore all internal arithmetic, arrays and leaf writes
cost $O(LA_\ell U)$ per band and degree run. This also pays for the
$12^L$-entry dense source array. Sharing band encodings gives
$\softO(ND^E)$ scalar work, without a full augmented Cartesian allocation.

We now change only the residual query. Its desired output words are on
the $2m$ queried inner positions, with symbols private, first inner
factor, or second inner factor, and with total nonprivate degree below
$t$. Process the two input sides separately. On one side order the $m$
normal positions increasingly. Write $a$ and $b$ for the normal and
opposite nonprivate counts of an output word.

If $a<\ell$, read its normal inner factors directly from the cache,
fix all other normal positions to private, and perform the old
two-source transform on the opposite positions with degree below $t-a$.
If $a\ge\ell$, let $J_0$ be the first $\ell$ normal nonprivate positions,
and let $p=\max J_0$ in their order. Group outputs by $J_0$ and the
$2^\ell$ choices of their direct inner factors. Every normal position
before $p$ outside $J_0$ is private. On normal positions after $p$ use
the three-source transform, and on all opposite positions use the
two-source transform, with free degree below $t-\ell$.
These are exactly the identities
\[
 p_1=f_{11}+P+f_{21},\quad p_2=f_{22}-f_{21},\qquad
 y_1=g_{11},\quad q_{\rm loc}=g_{11}-g_{21}.
\]
Every transform predecessor has no greater nonprivate degree than its
output. The private predecessor in the expression for $p_1$ occurs
only after the fixed pivot, so it cannot change $J_0$.
Consequently each group is generated on a predecessor-closed truncated
grid. At every intermediate step a normal axis has at most three
nonprivate choices and an opposite axis at most two; the group's
source count pays for all intermediate arrays up to polynomial factors.
Every source has at most $\ell$ direct projections, private plus direct
count at most $m$ in the normal half, and at most $m$ private positions
in the opposite half, so all sources belong to the stored cache.

The low groups use
\[
 C_{\rm low}=\sum_{\substack{a+b<t\\a<\ell}}
               \binom ma\binom mb2^{a+b}
 \le \sum_{j<t}\binom{2m}{j}2^j=\softO(D^{q_{\rm in}})
\]
sources. To count the high groups, fix the pivot and apply
\[
 \sum_p\binom{p-1}{\ell-1}\binom{m-p}{a-\ell}=\binom ma.
\]
This identity assigns every $a$-set to its unique first $\ell$ positions.
It gives the exact high-source count
\begin{align*}
 C_{\rm high}
 &=\sum_{\substack{a+b<t\\a\ge\ell}}
       \binom ma\binom mb2^\ell3^{a-\ell}2^b\\
 &\le(2/3)^\ell y^{-t}((1+3y)(1+2y))^m
   =\softO(D^{q_{\rm hi}}).
\end{align*}
For bounded small $m$ with $\ell=0$, use one empty high group and the
old transform; this does not affect the asymptotic bounds.

The left and right partitions generally differ, because their normal
halves differ. Each side therefore writes its transformed values to a
table indexed by the same full word on the $2m$ physical inner positions;
we do not pair lexicographic groups. On each side these groups are
disjoint and cover all output words of degree below $t$. The common
table has $\sum_{j<t}\binom{2m}{j}2^j$ entries and is already charged.
Multiply equal full keys, weight degree $j$ by
$(-1)^{t-j-1}\binom{2m-j-1}{t-j-1}$, and sum the source degree runs.
This is the unchanged exact residual, including words which lie in a
low group on one side and a high group on the other.

The high-box recurrence is unchanged. Its base leaves have no direct
projections and are contained in the cache, so it costs
$\softO(N^2D^{-G})$ and requires $\softO(D^{q_2})$ query reads.
Over $\mathcal R_q$, cache formation, all transform arrays and residual
coefficient dot products retain their $q$ factor. Stored boxes contain
all coefficients, permitting a single scalar access for a requested
coefficient. The new forms are projections with coefficients zero or
one. The polynomial coefficient-norm and exact bit-complexity bounds
of Lemma~\ref{lem:five-zero-prefix-ring} therefore apply unchanged.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Deterministic APSP with partial inner caches]
\label{cor:five-cache-apsp}
Use the same composite certification and fixed prime-stage construction,
with
\[
 \begin{split}
 c&=10.767898969,\quad \theta=0.060343925615,\quad
 \rho=0.066059242397,\\
 y&=0.025749961,\quad b=0.04337385,\qquad (R,S)=(100000,88618).
 \end{split}
\]
Then integer all-edges Exact Triangle, min-plus product, and APSP without
negative cycles have deterministic time $O(n^{2.993401})$, under the
same source embedding and polynomially bounded integer-weight assumptions.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
Here
\[
\begin{gathered}
 a=b/R=0.0000004337385,\qquad u=Sb/R=0.038437038393,\\
 v=b-u=0.004936811607,\qquad e=b+u=0.081810888393.
\end{gathered}
\]
Apply Lemma~\ref{lem:five-partial-cache} to each scalar or cyclic call in
Theorem~\ref{thm:cyclic-factor-apsp}. The geometry still uses $T$;
only the query-source encoding cost changes from $T$ to $E$.
In the first phase, at folded exponent $s\in[0,u]$, this cost's saving
before candidate enumeration is $1+b-s/2-(b+s)E$.
In the second phase, at cyclic exponent $w\in[0,v]$, it is
$1+b-u/2-w-eE$. All other lines in the two phase tables are unchanged,
using $q_1=\max\{q_{\rm in},q_{\rm hi}\}$ for residual queries.
The exact certificate checks both endpoints of every affine expression,
both residual branches separately, and every parameter condition.

At the last endpoint, after charging all $\softO(n^a)$ candidate primes,
the savings are bounded below by
\[
\begin{array}{lc}
\text{stored boxes } eG-a &0.0065996962020\\
\text{direct residual } u-eq_{\rm in}-a &0.0065996854801\\
\text{high residual } u-eq_{\rm hi}-a &0.0065996854801\\
\text{stored-box queries } b-eq_2-a &0.0065997069234\\
\text{cached encodings } 1+u/2-eE-a &0.0066000053788.
\end{array}
\]
Matrix formation and incidence bookkeeping also exceed the target
saving $0.006599$. The original tile margin
$1+u/2-eT$ exceeds $0.0236621656710$; the larger cache exponent must not
be substituted for $T$ in that geometric condition.
The minimum runtime slack above $0.006599$ exceeds $6.85\cdot10^{-7}$.
Thus all fixed powers of logarithms, rounding, exact coefficient
arithmetic, bit scaling and APSP squaring are covered, giving the stated
exponent. The previous $2.993441$ and fixed-profile $2.993676$ witnesses
remain valid. This still does not establish an exponent below $2.99$.
\end{proof}

\subsection*{A shared private factor and polynomial query caches}

The next construction changes the local five-term identity and its matrix
embedding. Its private and inner kernels share an opposite-side factor.
A Walsh transform of the input's outer coordinates preserves the full
good-set layout while making that shared factor useful for queries.

\begin{lemma}[Walsh embedding with a shared private factor]
\label{lem:aligned-walsh-embedding}
Let $D=4^m$, $L=cm+O(1)$, $c>5$, $K=\binom Lm$, and
$N_0=4^{L-m}$. The following five-term identity admits a full-grid
embedding with a constant fraction of the original $K$ good inner sets,
the same $N_0$ and $D$, and $2m+1$ degree runs:
\[
\begin{array}{c|c|c}
\lambda&\varphi_\lambda&\psi_\lambda\\\hline
11&x_1+p_1+p_2&s+v\\
12&x_1+p_1&s-v\\
21&x_2-p_2&s+v-w\\
22&x_2&s-v+w\\
0&-x_1-x_2&2s
\end{array}
\qquad
\sum_{\lambda}\varphi_\lambda\psi_\lambda
 =2p_1s+p_2w=:I.                                      \tag{A1}
\]
The second physical half exchanges the normal and opposite inputs.
For integer input matrices, the complete embedded output is $4^L$
times the corresponding matrix-product entry. The Walsh preprocessing
of the original inputs costs $\softO(ND)$ per matrix; formation of the
source arrays is charged by the compact encoder below. The complete output is recovered by exact
division by $4^L$. The same statements hold coefficientwise over
$\mathcal R_q=\mathbb Z[x]/(x^q-1)$, with a factor $\softO(q)$ in work.
The tile condition remains $N\ge KN_0$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Identity (A1) follows by expansion. Put $k=L-m$ and retain the source's
binary maps $\Gamma:\mathbb F_2^L\to\mathbb F_2^m$ and
$\Delta:\mathbb F_2^L\to\mathbb F_2^k$. For every good $m$-set $Q$,
$\Gamma_Q$ and $\Delta_{\overline Q}$ are invertible. These are the
same combinatorial conditions and constant-density good-set construction
as before; no new independence condition is imposed.

Index a left row within a good-set block by $(i,a)\in
\mathbb F_2^k\times\mathbb F_2^k$, and a right column by $(a,i)$.
For each block $Q$, apply the unnormalized Walsh transform only to its
opposite outer index:
\begin{align*}
 \widehat X_Q[(i,z),h]
 &=\sum_{a\in\mathbb F_2^k}(-1)^{z\cdot a}X_Q[(i,a),h],\\
 \widehat Y_Q[h,(z,i)]
 &=\sum_{a\in\mathbb F_2^k}(-1)^{z\cdot a}Y_Q[h,(a,i)]. \tag{A2}
\end{align*}
Fast Walsh transforms use $O(k)$ additions per original entry. They
are independent of the queried good set in the opposite physical half.

For a normal word $u$, write $Q(u)$ for its $p_1,p_2$ positions,
$P_2(u)$ for its $p_2$ positions, and $i(u)$ for its $x_1,x_2$ bits
outside $Q(u)$. For an opposite word $v$, let $V(v)$ and $W(v)$ be its
$v$ and $w$ positions. In degree run $d\in\{-m,\ldots,m\}$, define
the left source array by
\[
 A_d[u'u'']=2^{|P_2(u')|}
 \widehat X_{Q(u')}\bigl[
 (i(u'),\Delta[V(u'')]),
 (\Gamma[P_2(u')],\Gamma[W(u'')])\bigr]                \tag{A3}
\]
when $Q(u')$ is good and $|W(u'')|-|P_2(u')|=d$, and zero otherwise.
Define the right source array symmetrically by
\[
 B_d[v'v'']=2^{|P_2(v'')|}
 \widehat Y_{Q(v'')}\bigl[
 (\Gamma[W(v')],\Gamma[P_2(v'')]),
 (\Delta[V(v')],i(v''))\bigr]                         \tag{A4}
\]
when $Q(v'')$ is good and $|P_2(v'')|-|W(v')|=d$.
Each input has local dimensions four and three, hence root-array
volume $12^L$ per degree run; the compact encoder below charges this
volume and its formation.

For an original row $(Q_L,i_L,a_L)$ and column $(Q_R,a_R,i_R)$,
place inner symbols at $Q_L$ in the first half and $Q_R$ in the second.
Use normal outside bits $i_L,i_R$ and opposite outside bits
\[
 j_L=\Delta_{\overline{Q_L}}^{\mathsf T}a_R,
 \qquad j_R=\Delta_{\overline{Q_R}}^{\mathsf T}a_L.     \tag{A5}
\]
These transpose character maps replace the earlier inverse locator;
they are bijections and are computable in polynomial time in $L$.

Every inner symbol forces a normal $p$ letter. Since a source word
has exactly $m$ such letters, its normal inner set is exactly the
query's good set. Its outside normal factors consequently reduce to
the indicated $x_i$ with coefficient one. Inner $p_2$ pairs only with
$w$, and inner $p_1$ only with $s$. If the two normal $p_2$ sets have
sizes $a,b$, and there are $e_L,e_R\ge0$ additional outside $w$ letters,
the two run values are $b+e_L-a$ and $b-a-e_R$. Equality forces
$e_L=e_R=0$. Thus every outside opposite factor is $s+(-1)^jv$.

The local $p_1s$ coefficient and the source weights in (A3)--(A4)
together supply the uniform factor $2^{2m}$, independently of the
middle-bit choices. The good $\Gamma$ restrictions enumerate all
$4^m$ middle indices exactly once. In the second physical half,
the outside $v$ subsets $V\subseteq\overline{Q_R}$ have signs
$(-1)^{a_L\cdot\Delta[V]}$ by (A5). The map $V\mapsto\Delta[V]$
is bijective, so Walsh inversion of (A2) gives $2^k$ times the desired
left input entry. The first half similarly gives $2^k$ times the
desired right entry. Hence the degree-summed output equals
\[
 2^{2m}2^{2k}(XY)_{ij}=4^L(XY)_{ij}.                  \tag{A6}
\]
Only the \emph{complete} query numerator is divided by $4^L$;
individual residual terms or boxes need not be divisible. Over
$\mathcal R_q$ every complete coefficient is exactly divisible, so
this uses integer division, never inversion modulo $q$. The scaled
identity holds over any commutative ring, but decoding over a general
ring would require division by $4^L$ to be justified separately.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Two-sided polynomial cache]
\label{lem:aligned-polynomial-cache}
Use Lemma~\ref{lem:aligned-walsh-embedding}, the original five-term box
geometry, and
\[
 T=c-1+\frac{c\HH(1/c)}{\ln4},\qquad
 G=\frac{c\HH(1/c)-c\HH((1-\theta)/c)-\theta\ln4}{\ln2}.
\]
For fixed $0<\theta<1/2$, set
\[
 q_1=\frac{2\HH(\theta)}{\ln4},\qquad E=T+q_1,
 \qquad q_2=\frac{2\HH(\theta)+2\theta\ln4}{\ln4}.
\]
Under $N\ge KN_0$, integer matrices over $\mathcal R_q$ can be
preprocessed in time and space
\[
 \softO\left(qND^E+qN^2D^{-G}+qND\right).
\]
A specified coefficient of a selected product entry is then obtained
in $\softO(qD^{q_1}+D^{q_2})$ time. Input-size and polynomial
coefficient-norm assumptions are as in the previous cyclic-ring lemma.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Write $P_N=-x_1-x_2$, $P_O=2s$, and $P=P_NP_O$. Introduce a separate
formal variable $z$, truncate modulo $z^t$ for
$t=\lceil2\theta m\rceil$, and put $H(z)=P_N+zp_1$. Then
\[
 P+zI=H(z)P_O+zp_2w.                                \tag{A7}
\]
This identity uses a single alternative channel at each coordinate.

In a normal half retain four ordinary forms and two distinguished
forms $H(z),p_2$; in an opposite half retain four ordinary forms and
two distinguished forms $P_O,w$. Retain at most $m$ distinguished
positions per half and at most $t$ channel labels $p_2$ or $w$ across
both halves. The channel forms themselves carry no $z$ factor in the
cache. The other input exchanges the two halves.

Put $U=\sum_{j\le m}\binom Lj4^{L-j}$ and
$B_t=\sum_{h\le t}\binom{2m}{h}$. The exact retained count is
\[
 S_t=\sum_{j,k\le m}\binom Lj\binom Lk4^{2L-j-k}
                  \sum_{h\le\min\{t,j+k\}}\binom{j+k}{h}.
                                                               \tag{A8}
\]
Indeed $j,k$ choose the distinguished supports, and choosing $h$
positions in their union determines all channel labels. Every other
distinguished position has its unique baseline $H$ or $P_O$.
There is no factor $2^h$. Since $j+k\le2m$,
\[
 (KN_0)^2B_t\le S_t\le U^2B_t
       =\softO\bigl(D^{2T+q_1}\bigr).               \tag{A9}
\]
The lower bound is the $j=k=m$ summand. The upper bound uses $c>5$
for the geometric ordinary tail and $\theta<1/2$ for the increasing
unweighted binomial tail. Integer ceilings contribute polynomial
factors; the entropy expression is not extended beyond that regime.

All internal arrays are charged. Every retained depth-$d$ prefix
has all $4^{2L-d}$ ordinary completions. They preserve both
distinguished caps and the joint channel cap, and their sets are
disjoint across prefixes at fixed depth. The remaining input array
has local dimensions at most four, so the completions pay for its
entire volume. Thus each layer has at most $S_t$ entries. Every node
has at most six forms of constant support; its polynomial operations
are additions, fixed integer scaling, and the one-place shift in
$H(z)$. With $t=O(m)$ coefficients per entry, full initialization,
intermediate arithmetic and storage, and leaf writes cost
$O(\operatorname{poly}(m)S_t)$. In particular $12^L\le16^L\le S_t$
pays for each dense source array. Generate the capped recursion
directly, without a full $36^L$ leaf allocation. Sharing these
encodings across the tiles of a band gives $\softO(ND^E)$ scalar
preprocessing.

For a query let $Q$ be its $2m$ inner physical positions. For every
channel set $C\subseteq Q$ with $|C|<t$, read one polynomial from
each cache: use $p_2$ or $w$ at channel positions according to the
input's role, $H(z)$ or $P_O$ at other inner positions, and the fixed
ordinary forms outside $Q$. Each word has exactly $m$ distinguished
positions per half and fewer than $t$ channels, so it is retained.
Both inputs use the same full physical set $C$. Denote the values
by $L_C(z),R_C(z)$. Tensoring (A7) gives, modulo $z^t$,
\[
 F(z)=\operatorname{Contr}\!\left(\prod_{i\in Q}(P_i+zI_i)\right)
     =\sum_{\substack{C\subseteq Q\\|C|<t}}
                z^{|C|}L_C(z)R_C(z),                 \tag{A10}
\]
where the contraction includes the fixed outside ordinary kernels.
All polynomial degrees are nonnegative, so truncation discards no
needed coefficient. The standard exact low residual is
\[
 \sum_{j<t}\sigma_j[z^j]F(z),\qquad
 \sigma_j=(-1)^{t-j-1}\binom{2m-j-1}{t-j-1}.          \tag{A11}
\]
Equivalently, a channel set contributes
$\sum_{j=|C|}^{t-1}\sigma_j[z^{j-|C|}](L_CR_C)$.
This applies one $z$ shift per physical channel, not one per input.
The exact number of keys is $\sum_{h<t}\binom{2m}{h}$, so enumeration,
lookups, polynomial products and weighting cost $\softO(D^{q_1})$.

The high-box recurrence still expands a star using the four ordinary
terms and $P$ of (A1). Retain the usual at-most-$m$ private-or-star
positions per half, at most $2m-t$ in total, and stars before private
positions. Its full state count is unchanged. A base leaf has no
channel label and is present in the cache. At normal private positions
take the constant coefficient $H(0)=P_N$; its base product is
$L_{\rm leaf}(0)R_{\rm leaf}(0)$, not the product of the full cached
polynomials. Thus no separate ordinary input encoder is required.
The box preprocessing costs $\softO(N^2D^{-G})$ and the query reads
$4^t\binom{2m}{t}=\softO(D^{q_2})$ stored values. Adding these values
to (A11), summing degree runs, and then dividing the complete numerator
by $4^L$ yields the original product entry by (A6).

Over $\mathcal R_q$, $z$ is separate from the cyclic variable $x$.
Cache entries have $O(m)$ $z$ coefficients, each of width $q$.
Linear formation and exact cyclic box products cost their usual
$\softO(q)$ factor. A specified cyclic coefficient of a product
$L_CR_C$ is obtained by polynomially many length-$q$ coefficient
dot products, so the residual costs $\softO(qD^{q_1})$.
Stored boxes contain every cyclic coefficient, and their requested
coefficient is read in constant time.

The Walsh transforms increase coefficient $\ell_1$ norms by at most
$2^{L-m}$, source weights by at most $2^m$, and every subsequent
constant-support linear circuit by at most a fixed constant to the
$O(L)$ power. Bilinear products, the coefficients in (A11), and the
polynomial number of degree runs preserve a polynomial norm bound.
Thus all scalar coefficients have $O(\log n)$ bits under the existing
fixed-parameter assumptions. Exact cyclic arithmetic, auxiliary
polynomial operations, Walsh transforms, and final exact division
contribute only the stated polynomial-logarithmic factors.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Deterministic APSP with shared-factor polynomial caches]
\label{cor:aligned-cache-apsp}
For polynomially bounded integer weights, all-edges Exact Triangle,
min-plus product, and APSP without negative cycles admit deterministic
$O(n^{2.99294792})$ algorithms.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
Use the existing composite-incidence certification and fixed prime-stage
construction with
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 c=10.66550171,\quad\theta=0.06529782140,\quad b=0.04604634600,\\
 (R,S)=(1000000000,768979045).
 \end{gathered}
\]
Put
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 \eta&=b/R=0.000000000046046346,\\
 u&=S\eta=0.03540867517281957,\\
 v&=(R-S)\eta=0.01063767082718043,\\
 e&=b+u=0.08145502117281957.
 \end{aligned}
\]
These are fixed constants independent of $n$. The earlier increase in the
fixed stage count gave $2.992948$ from $2.992949$. The present refinement
to $2.99294792$ retains exactly those parameters and tightens only the
reported target; it introduces no new oracle.
For sufficiently large $n$, the candidate pool contains more than $2R$
primes; with $R=10^9$ this threshold, like the absorption of the
$(\log n)^{\Theta(R)}$ factors by the strict slack, holds only for
astronomically large $n$, so the bound is asymptotic. The factor $(\log n)^R$ in the completed modulus remains a fixed
polylogarithmic factor, and its bit length is
$b\log n+O(R\log\log n)=O(\log n)$. The $O(R\log n)$ stage work
therefore fits the same asymptotic proof; no practical speedup is asserted.
Replace the selected-entry oracle in Theorem~\ref{thm:cyclic-factor-apsp}
by Lemma~\ref{lem:aligned-polynomial-cache}. All normalization occurs
inside that oracle, before its exact nonnegative modular counts are
used by the incidence certificate. In particular, signed Walsh and
cache intermediates do not alter the certificate's count interpretation.

In the scalar phase, for folded exponent $s\in[0,u]$, the savings
after charging all $\softO(n^\eta)$ candidate primes are
\[
\begin{array}{ll}
\text{boxes:}&b-s+(b+s)G-\eta,\\
\text{residual queries:}&b-(b+s)q_1-\eta,\\
\text{stored queries:}&b-(b+s)q_2-\eta,\\
\text{polynomial cache:}&1+b-s/2-(b+s)E-\eta,\\
\text{matrix formation:}&1-3s/2-\eta.
\end{array}                                                   \tag{A12}
\]
The tile margin is $1+s/2-(b+s)T$. In the cyclic phase, for
$w\in[0,v]$, the respective savings are
\[
\begin{array}{ll}
\text{boxes:}&b-u-w+eG-\eta,\\
\text{residual queries:}&b-w-eq_1-\eta,\\
\text{stored queries:}&b-eq_2-\eta,\\
\text{polynomial cache:}&1+b-u/2-w-eE-\eta,\\
\text{matrix formation:}&1-3u/2-w-\eta.
\end{array}                                                   \tag{A13}
\]
The tile margin is $1+u/2-eT$, and incidence bookkeeping has saving
$b-\eta$ in both phases. These expressions charge the full cache
exponent $E=T+q_1$; geometry still uses $T$.

The exact-rational certificate checks 47 strict inequalities, including
both endpoints of every affine expression in (A12)--(A13), the stage
identities, and every tail and parameter condition. At the final cyclic
endpoint the five savings exceed, respectively,
\[
\begin{gathered}
 0.00705208124172,\qquad 0.00705208125745,\qquad
 0.00705208123527,\\ 0.00705208151941,\qquad
 0.93624931636754.
\end{gathered}
\]
The original tile margin exceeds $0.03540867543478$. The minimum
runtime slack above the target saving $0.00705208$ is greater than
$1.23527\cdot10^{-9}$. Thus polynomial logarithmic factors from the
Walsh transforms, source runs, auxiliary polynomial caches, exact
coefficient arithmetic, integer rounding, bit scaling, and APSP
squaring fit within the strict gap. This proves the stated exponent.
The earlier certified witnesses remain valid; a bound below $2.99$
has not been established.
\end{proof}

% Locally reproduced finite evidence:
% check_five_term_aligned_embedding.py: 352 signed original-matrix outputs.
% check_five_term_aligned_cache.py: 34 arbitrary-array queries, 20 compact
% encoders, 423918 retained leaves and 1088138 total retained/intermediate entries.
% check_five_term_aligned_integration.py: 530 normalized original-product
% coefficients, including all targets in a signed cyclic q=3 fixture.

\subsection*{Light sources and shared completion of residual queries}
\label{sec:light-source}

The following construction improves Corollary~\ref{cor:aligned-cache-apsp}
by changing the source encoder and completing requested residual values
jointly across middle blocks. It retains the full-grid geometry, the
complete-output normalization, and the composite-incidence reduction.
In this subsection write
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 D=4^m,\quad L=\lceil cm\rceil,\quad k=L-m,\quad
 K=\binom Lm,\quad N_0=4^k,\\
 C_K=\frac{c\HH(1/c)}{\ln4},\qquad T=c-1+C_K,\qquad
 Q=\frac{2\HH(\theta)}{\ln4},\\
 G=\frac{c\HH(1/c)-c\HH((1-\theta)/c)-\theta\ln4}{\ln2},
 \qquad q_2=Q+2\theta,\qquad t=\lceil2\theta m\rceil .
 \end{gathered}
\]
Here $Q$ without a subscript is an entropy exponent; physical good sets
will have subscripts. Formal polynomials use $z$, cyclic coefficients
use $x$ in $\mathcal R_q$, and the cache dimension exponent is $\zeta$.

\begin{lemma}[Shifted private factor and light source decomposition]
\label{lem:light-source}
The embedding of Lemma~\ref{lem:aligned-walsh-embedding} also applies to
the four ordinary pairs
\[
 (x_1+p_2,s+v),\quad (x_1,s-v),\quad
 (x_2-p_2,s+v-w),\quad (x_2,s-v+w)
\]
and private pair $(-x_1-x_2+p_1,2s)$. Their sum is
$I=2p_1s+p_2w$. For $\rho=51/100$, each original product decomposes
deterministically into $\operatorname{poly}(m)$ products whose source
arrays have at most $\lfloor\rho m\rfloor$ normal $p_2$ letters.
In each product one may additionally set to zero every source entry
with more than $\lfloor\rho m\rfloor$ opposite $w$ letters, before
both the residual and high-box encoders. The complete degree-summed
output still equals $4^L$ times that product entry.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Expansion proves the shifted identity. The source definitions
(A2)--(A5) are unchanged: every complete contraction has $I$ at the
$m$ normal distinguished positions, which force all $m$ normal
$p_1,p_2$ letters. Thus outside normal forms reduce to the same
$x_1,x_2$ as before. The proof of (A6), including its weights,
degree filter, and transpose locator, applies.

Here is a deterministic construction of the required translations.
Put $\epsilon=1/50$, $J=10000m$, $r=101/100$, and
$C=(r+r^{-1})/2=20201/20200$. For a list
$\delta_1,\ldots,\delta_J\in\mathbb F_2^m$, allowing repetitions, set
\[
 S_a=\sum_{j=1}^J(-1)^{a\cdot\delta_j},\qquad
 \Phi=\sum_{a\ne0}(r^{S_a}+r^{-S_a}).
\]
A uniform independent list has expectation $2(2^m-1)C^J$.
The exact rational inequality $r^{200}>4C^{10000}$ gives
$\mathbb E\Phi<r^{\epsilon J}$. Reveal the $Jm$ bits one at a time,
choosing a bit that does not increase the conditional expectation.
For each character its factor for the current list entry is $r$,
$r^{-1}$, or $C$, according as its sign is determined positive,
determined negative, or still unbiased. Enumerating all $2^m-1$
characters computes both choices exactly. Common denominators and
numerators have $O(m)$ bits, with fixed constants, so construction
costs $2^m\operatorname{poly}(m)$ bit operations. The resulting
