$\Phi<r^{\epsilon J}$ forces $|S_a|<\epsilon J$ for every $a\ne0$.

For every good set $Q_n$ and middle half-index $h$, invertibility of
$\Gamma_{Q_n}$ and this bias bound imply
\[
 J^{-1}\sum_j
 \operatorname{wt}\bigl(\Gamma_{Q_n}^{-1}(h+\delta_j)\bigr)
 \le (1+\epsilon)m/2=\rho m.
\]
Let $\iota(Q_n,h)$ be the first list index attaining weight at most
$\lfloor\rho m\rfloor$. Indices resolve repetitions in the list.
For a left row with normal block $Q_L$ and right column with
normal block $Q_R$, define, for each pair of list indices $(r,s)$,
\[
\begin{aligned}
 X^{rs}_{(Q_L,i),(h'_1,h'_2)}
 &=X_{(Q_L,i),(h'_1+\delta_r,h'_2+\delta_s)}
   {\bf1}\{\iota(Q_L,h'_1+\delta_r)=r\},\\
 Y^{rs}_{(h'_1,h'_2),(Q_R,j)}
 &=Y_{(h'_1+\delta_r,h'_2+\delta_s),(Q_R,j)}
   {\bf1}\{\iota(Q_R,h'_2+\delta_s)=s\}.
\end{aligned}
\]
Both inputs use the same global middle translation. Masks are evaluated
at the original middle indices and depend only on the input's own
normal good set. Each original summand belongs to exactly one pair,
so $XY=\sum_{r,s}X^{rs}Y^{rs}$, coefficientwise over $\mathcal R_q$.
The masks commute with the opposite-outer-index Walsh transform.
In (A3)--(A4), a surviving normal $p_2$ set therefore has size at most
$\lfloor\rho m\rfloor$.

Let the two normal $p_2$ counts in a complete contraction be $a,b$,
and the additional outside opposite $w$ counts be $e_L,e_R\ge0$.
The two degree runs are $b+e_L-a$ and $b-a-e_R$. Their equality
forces $e_L=e_R=0$; hence the opposite $w$ counts are $b,a$, both
at most $\lfloor\rho m\rfloor$. Every discarded coordinate vanishes
termwise in the complete contraction. Crop both inputs before
\emph{all} encoders: individual residuals and boxes may change.
Only their complete sum is divided coefficientwise by $4^L$.
The $J^2$ products, mask preparation, translations, Walsh transforms,
and output summation cost polynomial factors in $m$ per original
coefficient. The bias-list construction is below the $D$ input-formation
budget. Sharing across outer bands is preserved.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[An exact half-weight source cap]
\label{lem:half-source}
Lemma~\ref{lem:light-source} also holds with $\rho=1/2$. An ordered
list of $J_m=64m^3$ translations can be constructed deterministically
in $2^m\operatorname{poly}(m)$ bit operations so that, for every
invertible $A\in\mathbb F_2^{m\times m}$ and $h\in\mathbb F_2^m$,
some list index $j$ has
$\operatorname{wt}(A(h+\delta_j))\le\lfloor m/2\rfloor$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Allow the bias to shrink with the tensor parameter $m$. Set
\[
 \epsilon_m=\frac1{2m},\qquad r_m=1+\frac1{4m},\qquad
 C_m=\frac{r_m+r_m^{-1}}2,\qquad J_m=64m^3 .
\]
For $x=1/(4m)\le1/4$ the elementary logarithm inequalities give
\[
 \ln r_m\ge x-x^2/2\ge\frac7{32m},\qquad
 \ln C_m\le C_m-1=\frac{x^2}{2(1+x)}\le\frac1{32m^2}.
\]
Therefore
\[
 J_m(\epsilon_m\ln r_m-\ln C_m)\ge5m
 >\ln(2(2^m-1)).
\]
The last inequality follows from $2(2^m-1)<4^m$ and $\ln4<2$.
The same two-sided character potential as in
Lemma~\ref{lem:light-source} has initial expectation strictly below
$r_m^{\epsilon_m J_m}$. Exact conditional expectations produce a
list with every nonzero character bias strictly below $\epsilon_m$.
Here common rational numerators and denominators have
$O(J_m\log(m+1))$ bits, rather than the earlier $O(m)$ bound.
There are $J_m m$ choices and $2^m-1$ characters, so all bit costs
are still $2^m\operatorname{poly}(m)$.

The rows of an invertible $A$ are nonzero characters. Thus, uniformly
in $A,h$,
\[
 J_m^{-1}\sum_j\operatorname{wt}(A(h+\delta_j))
 <m/2+\epsilon_m m/2=m/2+1/4.
\]
If every integer weight exceeded $\lfloor m/2\rfloor$, their
average would be at least $\lfloor m/2\rfloor+1$, a contradiction
for either parity of $m$. Use the first valid \emph{list index}
in the two original-coordinate masks. The same translated-product
decomposition, Walsh commutation, and same-degree argument permit
cropping both opposite sources to $\lfloor m/2\rfloor$ before
both encoders. Complete outputs retain their normalization by $4^L$.
The $J_m^2=O(m^6)$ products, their masks, and their output sums
contribute only fixed polylogarithmic factors. The larger rational
bit lengths are confined to the separately charged list construction;
the matrix coefficients still have $O(\log n)$ bits.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Grouped completion with asymmetric row chunks]
\label{lem:light-completion}
Suppose $B$ middle-block products have the same outer size $N$, inner
size $D$, cyclic width $q$, good-set layout, folded locator, modulus,
and padding. Suppose an exact rectangular algorithm
multiplies dimensions $s^a\times s$ by $s\times s^\kappa$ in
$O(s^w)$ operations, where $\kappa\ge a\ge1$ and $w$ includes a fixed
positive allowance above a proved exponent bound. Write
\[
 \alpha=w-\kappa-1\ge0,\qquad \beta=w-\kappa-a\ge0,\qquad
 \gamma(r)=\frac{\HH((\rho-r)/(c-1))}{\ln4}.
\]
Use either $\rho=51/100$ from Lemma~\ref{lem:light-source} or
$\rho=1/2$ from Lemma~\ref{lem:half-source}.
Assume $c>5$, $0<2\theta<\rho$, and
\[
 \zeta>0,\quad \zeta<(c-1)\gamma(2\theta),\qquad
 \frac{2\ln((1-\theta)/\theta)}{\ln4}
       +\frac{\zeta\gamma'(\theta)}{2\gamma(\theta)^2}>0.
                                                               \tag{L1}
\]
Under the original geometry $N\ge KN_0$ and the strict batch fit
$Bq\ge D^{\kappa\zeta+\xi}$ for some fixed $\xi>0$, requested residual
values for $M_0$ original output incidences across the common shape can
be obtained with costs
\[
 \softO\left(qBN(D^{E_{\rm root}}+D^{E_{\rm res}})
                    +BqM_0D^{q_1}\right),                    \tag{L2}
\]
where
\[
\begin{aligned}
 E_{\rm root}&=2C_K+(2c-1)\log_4 3+Q-T,\\
 E_{\rm res}&=T+Q-\left(\frac1{2\gamma(\theta)}-1-\alpha\right)\zeta,
 &q_1&=Q+\beta\zeta .
\end{aligned}
\]
The incidence list may depend on the middle block if $M_0$ bounds
its union over blocks. Duplicate requested rows may be shared.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
For the shifted identity put
$H(z)=-x_1-x_2+(1+z)p_1$. Then
\[
 P+zI=H(z)(2s)+zp_2w.
\]
The residual remains (A10)--(A11), with the same physical channel
set in both inputs and a single shift by its size. Work modulo
$z^t$ throughout.

Fix distinguished sets $Q_n,Q_o$ of size $m$ and channels
$C_n\subseteq Q_n$, $C_o\subseteq Q_o$ with total size below $t$.
Contract these positions first. Normal nonchannel forms have three
raw supports $x_1,x_2,p_1$, normal channels are $p_2$, and opposite
distinguished forms are the singletons $2s,w$. Every root has
$3^{2k}$ remaining raw coordinates, each formed using at most $3^m$
explicit Walsh-source lookups. Across all groups, even zero ones,
the cost per band per block is at most
\[
 \softO\left(qK^2
       \sum_{h<t}\binom{2m}{h}\,3^{2k+m}\right).
\]
There are $\softO(N/D^T)$ bands. This proves the root term in (L2)
and pays for all root writes and intermediate distinguished
contractions.

The remaining normal rare count is at most
$\lfloor\rho m\rfloor-|C_n|$, and the opposite rare count at most
$\lfloor\rho m\rfloor-|C_o|$. For the opposite half use the forward
integer evaluation map $(s,v,w)\mapsto(s+v,s-v,-w)$; after permuting
ordinary labels its four forms have the normal pattern
$(y_1+w',y_1,y_2-w',y_2)$. This map preserves the rare support.
Every one of its $3^{2k}$-entry layers is paid by the root bound;
no inverse or division by two is used.

Choose the half with the larger channel count, breaking ties
deterministically. For any suffix length $\ell\le k$, a raw word
with at most $r_0$ rare positions has a cyclic window of length
$\ell$ containing at most $\lfloor r_0\ell/k\rfloor$ of them,
by averaging the $k$ windows. Assign the raw source coordinate to
its first such window \emph{before} transformation. This is a
disjoint linear partition. Keep all $k$ components, and sum their
reconstructed values on each input before multiplying the two
residual polynomials.

In each component, transform the other $2k-\ell$ coordinates to
their four ordinary outputs. In the suffix keep raw coordinates
with at most $\lfloor r_0\ell/k\rfloor$ rare letters. For each
nonrare pair-bit string of length $\ell$ create a separate group.
A raw suffix with $j$ rare letters belongs to $2^j$ such strings;
copy it to every one of those groups. The exact length of a group
vector is
\[
 s_\ell=\sum_{j\le\lfloor r_0\ell/k\rfloor}\binom{\ell}{j}.
\]
Thus each prefix has $2^\ell s_\ell$ explicitly stored entries,
including all duplication. An ordinary suffix output chooses its
pair-bit group and an activity set $A$; its selector is
${\bf1}\{J\subseteq A\}$ for each rare support $J$, with the fixed
signs absorbed in the stored vector. The selector is independent
of the middle block and of the formal and cyclic coefficient.
To charge intermediate transforms explicitly, the raw suffix volume is
$V_\ell=\sum_{j\le\lfloor r_0\ell/k\rfloor}
\binom{\ell}{j}2^{\ell-j}\le2^\ell s_\ell$.
After $d$ prefix transforms a full allocated layer has
$4^d3^{2k-\ell-d}V_\ell$ entries, at most
$4^{2k-\ell}2^\ell s_\ell$. Thus every layer, including its
initialization and zeros, fits the final duplicated group volume.
The source partition scans and the opposite forward map were
already charged in root formation.

For channel fractions $x=|C_n|/m$, $y=|C_o|/m$, choose
$\ell=\zeta m/\gamma(\max(x,y))+O(1)$. More precisely use the actual
$k$ and rare budget with a rational upper entropy enclosure of
error $O(m^{-2})$, and round the quotient down. Binary logarithm
enclosures compute this in polynomial time in $m$; the common
padding dimension is $s_*=\operatorname{poly}(m)D^\zeta$.
Condition (L1) ensures $\ell\le k$ with strict slack.
The number of groups before band sharing is bounded by
\[
 \softO\left(
 D^{2T+\max_{x+y\le2\theta}
  \{(\HH(x)+\HH(y))/\ln4-\zeta/(2\gamma(\max(x,y)))\}}\right).
                                                               \tag{L3}
\]
The factor $2^{-\ell}$ comes from replacing $4^\ell$ suffix
outputs by $2^\ell$ pair-bit groups; the $s_*$ entries per group
are charged separately, not omitted.

At fixed $x+y=2r$, entropy concavity and decreasing $\gamma$
make the expression in braces largest at $x=y=r$. Since $\gamma$
is positive, decreasing, and concave, $1/\gamma$ is convex.
Consequently $2\HH(r)/\ln4-\zeta/(2\gamma(r))$ is concave.
Its positive endpoint derivative in (L1) makes its maximum on
$[0,\theta]$ equal to $Q-\zeta/(2\gamma(\theta))$.
Ceilings in $L,t$ change these bounds only by polynomial factors.

For $P_*$ groups and a total of $M_*$ requested selector rows,
form separately for each group an $r\times s_*$ selector and an
$s_*\times Bq$ data matrix. Chunk rows into size $s_*^a$ and
columns into size $s_*^\kappa$. Even a short final row chunk is
charged: there are
$O((1+r/s_*^a)Bq/s_*^\kappa)$ products per group. Their total cost is
\[
 \softO\bigl(Bq[P_*s_*^{1+\alpha}+M_*s_*^\beta]\bigr). \tag{L4}
\]
This dominates data formation $BqP_*s_*$, selector formation
$M_*s_*$, and every output write $BqM_*$. It never concatenates
unrelated groups for free. Formal coefficients, degree runs,
window components, and translations contribute polynomial factors.
Each output incidence fixes its own two distinguished sets; hence
$M_*\le\softO(M_0D^Q)$, without another factor $K^2$.
Substitution of (L3), the $\softO(N/D^T)$ bands, and (L4)
gives (L2). Complete cyclic vectors are recovered for both inputs
before their residual products, so the factor $q$ is retained.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[A separate jointly capped high-box encoder]
\label{lem:light-box}
For the shifted and cropped sources above, the high-box bases have
encoder exponent $E_{\rm box}=T-G$. The stored-box preprocessing
and query exponents remain $G$ and $q_2$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Use private $H(0)=-x_1-x_2+p_1$ on the normal side and $2s$ on
the opposite side. Retain at most $m$ private-or-star positions
per half and at most $2m-t$ jointly. Expanding a star into an
ordinary or private position preserves both caps, so all high-box
states have the required base leaves. Their count is
\[
 S_{\rm box}=
 \sum_{\substack{j,l\le m\\j+l\le2m-t}}
       \binom Lj\binom Ll4^{2L-j-l}
 =\softO(D^{2T-G}).
\]
Indeed $\binom Lj4^{-j}$ increases up to $m$ for $c>5$ and is
log-concave. For each sum $j+l$ the maximum is balanced; the
largest sum is $2m-t$, so the exponent is
$2c-2+2\theta+2c\HH((1-\theta)/c)/\ln4=2T-G$.
All-ordinary completions pay for every intermediate array; the
root volume $12^L$ is below the $16^L$ all-ordinary leaves already
included. Sharing over $\softO(N/D^T)$ bands gives
$\softO(qND^{T-G})$ per block. All bases and intermediate boxes
store their full cyclic vectors. The unchanged high-box recurrence
costs $\softO(qN^2D^{-G})$ per block, and a requested coefficient
uses $\softO(D^{q_2})$ stored scalar reads. Cropping is applied
before these encoders as required by Lemma~\ref{lem:light-source}.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[A rectangular endpoint for the grouped completion]
\label{lem:light-cw}
There is a fixed exact characteristic-zero algorithm with exponent
$w=16.7220002$ for aspect $(a,1,\kappa)$, where
$a=861/500$ and $\kappa=15$. Thus Lemma~\ref{lem:light-completion}
may use $\alpha=0.7220002$ and $\beta=0.0000002$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Specialize Proposition 5.1 and Theorem 3.2 of \cite{VXXZ24}
to level one of the big Coppersmith--Winograd tensor with parameter
$q_{\rm CW}=16$. Put probabilities
$(ap,p,\kappa p,d_0,e_0,f_0)$ on its six types
$(011)$, $(101)$, $(110)$, $(002)$, $(020)$, $(200)$, where
\[
 p=\frac{4000}{75249},\quad e_0=\frac1{18},\quad
 f_0=\frac{646603}{10^9},\quad d_0=e_0-p-f_0>0.
\]
They are positive and sum to one. Their marginals are
\[
\begin{aligned}
 X&=(ap+d_0+e_0,(\kappa+1)p,f_0),\\
 Y&=(p+d_0+f_0,(\kappa+a)p,e_0),\\
 Z&=(\kappa p+e_0+f_0,(a+1)p,d_0).
\end{aligned}
\]
The index-two marginals determine the three corners; the index-one
marginals uniquely determine the other three probabilities.
Thus the compatibility entropy penalty is zero. Choose the theorem's
$A_1=1$, $A_2=A_3=0$; at level one the complete split distributions
are point masses, so their penalty is zero as well. The extraction
rate is $\min\{H(X),H(Y),H(Z)\}$, where $H$ here is ternary entropy.
The constituent dimensions are
$\langle16^{pN},16^{\kappa pN},16^{apN}\rangle$.
Cyclic permutation and the asymptotic sum inequality give
\[
 \omega(a,1,\kappa)\le
 \frac{\ln18-\min\{H(X),H(Y),H(Z)\}}{p\ln16}.
\]
For this rational witness $Y=(1,16,1)/18$, and
$H(Y)+p(\kappa+a)\ln16=\ln18$ exactly. Rational logarithm
enclosures give $H(X),H(Z)>H(Y)$, so the infimum is at most
$\kappa+a=16.722$. The verifier separately checks all three
marginal inequalities using $w-10^{-7}$; the remaining fixed
$10^{-7}$ allowance permits choosing a finite exact algorithm.
This does not assert a finite algorithm attaining the infimum.

The characteristic-zero tensor construction uses rational
degenerations, interpolation, and bilinear arithmetic. Choose its
finite base size and interpolation once, within that fixed allowance,
then recurse. Intermediate coefficient growth is at most a constant
per recursion level; input integers of $O(\log n)$ bits therefore
retain $O(\log n)$ bits. Clearing the fixed rational denominators
and exact recovery cost polynomial logarithmic factors. The same
argument applies to the scalar coefficient matrices in (L4);
their complete cyclic and formal coefficient counts have already
been charged.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Deterministic APSP with light structured sources]
\label{cor:light-source-apsp}
For polynomially bounded integer weights, all-edges Exact Triangle,
min-plus product, and APSP without negative cycles admit deterministic
$O(n^{2.9926863})$ algorithms.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
Use the composite-incidence construction of
Theorem~\ref{thm:cyclic-factor-apsp} with
\[
\begin{gathered}
 c=10.422903143551736,\quad \theta=0.0656840679765022,\quad
 b=0.04884094,\\
 R=10^{14},\quad S=76777119351634,\quad
 \eta=b/R,\quad u=S\eta,\quad v=b-u,\quad e=b+u,\\
 \rho=51/100,\qquad \zeta=1/4,\qquad \delta=0.0073137.
\end{gathered}
\]
These are exact rational constants. In the first phase let the
folded exponent $x$ range over $[0,u]$ with cyclic exponent $y=0$;
in the second fix $x=u$ and let $y$ range over $[0,v]$.
The common middle-block count, outer dimension, inner dimension,
and cyclic width are, up to the already charged logarithmic factors,
\[
 B=n^{1-b},\qquad N=n^{1+x/2},\qquad D=n^{b+x},\qquad q=n^y.
\]
For each fixed candidate, mask, bit level and restart, the folded
location and requested coefficient depend only on the original outer
pair and the common modulus, not on the middle block. The union of
requested original pairs has size at most $n^2$, so (L2) applies
with $M_0\le n^2$. Each such call is charged separately in the
prime-stage bound. Residues are folded at their actual locations;
no extra modulus factor is inserted in the query count.

Use Lemmas~\ref{lem:light-source}--\ref{lem:light-cw}, with all
three encoder charges $E_{\rm root},E_{\rm res},E_{\rm box}$ kept
separately. After charging every candidate prime by $n^\eta$,
the savings against cubic time are
\[
\begin{array}{ll}
 \text{stored-box construction:}&b-x-y+(b+x)G-\eta,\\
 \text{residual completion and products:}&b-y-(b+x)q_1-\eta,\\
 \text{stored coefficient queries:}&b-(b+x)q_2-\eta,\\
 \text{each encoder }E_i:&1+b-x/2-y-(b+x)E_i-\eta,\\
 \text{input and Walsh formation:}&1-3x/2-y-\eta,\\
 \text{incidence bookkeeping:}&b-\eta.
\end{array}                                                   \tag{L5}
\]
The independent feasibility margins are
\[
 1+x/2-(b+x)T>0,\qquad
 1-b+y-\kappa(b+x)\zeta>0.                                    \tag{L6}
\]
The first retains the original full-grid exponent $T$, even though
the new encoders are cheaper. The second supplies the strict batch
fit for (L4); it is not implied by geometry.

The exact-rational verifier
\texttt{verify\_light\_source\_apsp.py} checks 61 strict inequalities
and the exact small-bias potential inequality. These include all
source feasibility conditions, (L1), the three rectangular marginal
bounds with positive allowance, (L5)--(L6) at both endpoints of both
phases, and the exact prime-stage identities. Every phase expression
is affine, so its endpoint checks cover the full phase.
For interpretation only, the exponents are approximately
\[
\begin{array}{lll}
 T=11.7993263169,&G=0.08470894353,&q_1=0.34960703849,\\
 E_{\rm res}=11.6676126746,&E_{\rm box}=11.7146173734,
 &E_{\rm root}=9.0305563991,\\
 q_2=0.48097512444.&&
\end{array}
\]
The minimum runtime margin above $\delta$ exceeds
$3.25\cdot10^{-8}$, and the minimum geometric margin exceeds
$1.38\cdot10^{-7}$. All cyclic coefficients are charged in
formation, encoders, completion, and residual products; stored boxes
alone permit single-coefficient reads. Walsh weights, fixed local
maps, $O(m)$ formal degrees, residual coefficients, and polynomially
many translations preserve polynomial coefficient norms.
Exact arithmetic therefore has the bit costs charged above.

As before, the fixed $R$ requires sufficiently large inputs to
supply all distinct candidate primes. Factors such as
$(\log n)^{O(R)}$, bit scaling, restarts, APSP squaring, padding,
and the deterministic translation construction fit within the
strict runtime margin. The resulting pre-absorption bound is
$\softO(n^{2.9926863-\epsilon_*})$ for a fixed $\epsilon_*>0$,
hence the stated ordinary $O(n^{2.9926863})$ bound. Earlier
certificates and their separately parameterized counting, clique,
real-input, 3SUM, and subset-convolution consequences remain valid.
This corollary does not substitute its witness into those reductions.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Exact-half sources and a refined numerical witness]
\label{cor:half-source-apsp}
For polynomially bounded integer weights, all-edges Exact Triangle,
min-plus product, and APSP without negative cycles admit deterministic
$O(n^{2.9926862626})$ algorithms.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
Apply Lemma~\ref{lem:half-source} and the same grouped completion,
jointly capped boxes, and rectangular endpoint, with
\[
\begin{gathered}
 c=10.42290323194818,\qquad\theta=0.06568406426999,\qquad
 b=0.04884094420,\\
 R=10^{14},\qquad S=76777127384957,\qquad
 \eta=b/R,\quad u=S\eta,\quad v=b-u,\quad e=b+u,\\
 \rho=1/2,\qquad \zeta=11/50,\qquad \delta=0.0073137374.
\end{gathered}
\]
Retain $a=861/500$, $\kappa=15$, $w=16.7220002$ and the fixed
$10^{-7}$ matrix-algorithm allowance of Lemma~\ref{lem:light-cw}.
In particular $\alpha=0.7220002$ and $\beta=0.0000002$ are unchanged.
Only the source parameter in
$\gamma(r)=\HH((\rho-r)/(c-1))/\ln4$ and the explicit rational
witness change. All three encoder terms and every complete cyclic
coefficient remain charged.

The verifier \texttt{verify\_half\_source\_apsp.py} checks 61 strict
rational inequalities. It verifies (L1), suffix feasibility, the
rectangular endpoint with its allowance, all three encoders, both
queries, input formation, bookkeeping, and both endpoints of
both phases in (L5)--(L6). The exact identity $\rho=1/2$ and the
positive uniform potential margin use Lemma~\ref{lem:half-source},
in place of the earlier fixed-bias condition $\rho>1/2$.
The minimum runtime margin above $\delta$ exceeds $8.43\cdot10^{-11}$,
and the original geometry margin exceeds $2.19\cdot10^{-10}$.
At the last cyclic endpoint the box, residual-query, and stored-query
savings each exceed $0.0073137374843$; the residual encoder's saving
exceeds $0.0075728$. The additional polynomial factors in $m$ from
the shrinking-bias list, together with all earlier rounding, exact
bit arithmetic, prime stages and APSP squaring, fit within these
fixed positive gaps.

This is a small numerical refinement of the preceding corollary,
accompanied by a stronger source-support lemma. Much of the change
from the displayed $2.9926863$ is slack in that earlier target.
The original $G,q_2,T$ and $q_1\ge Q$ remain in force; no escape
from the geometry/query cost family's bottleneck is claimed.
The earlier witnesses and separately parameterized downstream
statements retain their own certificates.
\end{proof}

\subsection*{Two black-box uses of the five-term data structure}
Lemma~\ref{lem:five-profile} is a statement about an arbitrary
$N\times D$ by $D\times N$ integer product with $N\ge KN_0$. Two earlier
reductions use the thin-product data structure only as such a black box,
and both improve when the five-term lemma replaces
Theorem~\ref{thm:refined}. Neither uses composite certification, cyclic
coefficient rings, or caches, so the exponent functions are exactly those
of Lemma~\ref{lem:five-profile}.

\begin{corollary}[Five-term thin products in the source's regimes]
\label{cor:five-thin}
Let $X\in\mathbb Z^{N\times D}$, $Y\in\mathbb Z^{D\times N}$ have entries
of magnitude $N^{O(1)}$, and let $W$ contain at most $N^2/\sqrt D$
positions. If $N\ge D^{18}$, the entries $(XY)[I,J]$, $(I,J)\in W$, are
computed deterministically in $O(N^2/D^{0.0991})$ time, and after that
preprocessing any single entry is returned in $O(D^{0.4009})$ time. If
$N\ge D^{14}$, the times are $O(N^2/D^{0.0853})$ and $O(D^{0.4147})$.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
Pad $D$ to a power of four and apply Lemma~\ref{lem:five-profile} with
\[
(c,\theta,r,y)=(16.2514,\;0.05033,\;0.04997,\;0.02004)
\]
for $N\ge D^{18}$ and with
\[
(c,\theta,r,y)=(12.420851,\;0.05478,\;0.0150708,\;0.01)
\]
for $N\ge D^{14}$. The costs are $N^2D^{-G}+|W|D^{\max(q_1,q_2)}
+ND^{E_{\rm aug}}+ND^{E_{\rm box}}$ with the tile condition $N\ge D^T$.
The exact-rational certificate checks, for the first witness,
$G=0.099159\ldots>0.0991$, $1/2-q_1=0.099179\ldots>0.0991$,
$1/2-q_2>0.0991$, $q_1=0.400820\ldots<0.4009$, $T=17.9613\ldots<18$,
and $E_{\rm aug}+0.0991<18$, so that both encoder families are below
$N^2D^{-0.0991}$; and for the second witness $G=0.085309\ldots>0.0853$,
$1/2-q_i>0.0853$, $q_2=0.414690\ldots<0.4147$, $T=13.9296\ldots<14$, and
$E_{\rm aug}+0.0853<14$. The strict gaps absorb the padding and the
polylogarithmic factors of the lemma.
\end{proof}
The source displays $O(N^2/D^{0.063})$ and $O(D^{0.437})$ at $N\ge D^{18}$;
the ten-term analysis of Theorem~\ref{thm:refined} gives $0.07195$ and
$0.42805$ there; Majdoddin~\cite{M26} displays $0.092$ and $0.403$ at
$N\ge D^{18}$ and $0.077$ and $0.421$ at $N\ge D^{14}$. The fixed-profile
slots, the two separately charged encoders, and the prefix queries account
for the difference from~\cite{M26}; the tile condition is the same.

\begin{corollary}[Real inputs with the five-term data structure]
\label{cor:five-real}
In the real RAM with comparisons, additions, and subtractions on real
numbers, real 3SUM has a Las Vegas algorithm with expected time
$O(n^{1.993805})$, and real min-plus product, APSP without negative
cycles, and Exact Triangle have Las Vegas algorithms with expected time
$O(n^{2.993805})$.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
Lemma~\ref{lem:prefix-count} calls the thin-product data structure on
explicit integer matrices with entries at most $d$ and inner dimension
$D=\Theta(d^2\log^5n)$, rounded up to a power of four, and uses no other
property of it. Replace Theorem~\ref{thm:refined} by
Lemma~\ref{lem:five-profile} with
\[
(c,\theta,r,y,e)=(10.7769,\;0.0567,\;0.03854,\;0.02511,\;0.08183),
\qquad d=\max\{1,\lfloor n^{e/2}\rfloor\}.
\]
The certified comparison counts, their complementary certification, the
fresh primes for adaptive calls, and the expected-round argument are
unchanged. For real 3SUM the expected cost is
\[
\softO\bigl(nD^{E_{\rm aug}}+nD^{E_{\rm box}}+n^2D^{-G}
+n^2D^{\max(q_1,q_2)-1/2}+nD+n^2D^{-1/2}+nd\bigr),
\]
and for real min-plus, APSP, and Exact Triangle it is
\[
\softO\bigl(n^2D^{E_{\rm aug}}+n^2D^{E_{\rm box}}+n^3D^{-G}
+n^3D^{\max(q_1,q_2)-1/2}+n^2D+n^2d+n^3/d\bigr),
\]
exactly as before with the two encoder families and the two query
exponents of the five-term lemma. The exact-rational certificate gives
the savings
\[
\begin{gathered}
eG=0.0061952\ldots,\qquad e(1/2-q_1)=0.0061954\ldots,\\
e(1/2-q_2)=0.0061952\ldots,\qquad 1-eE_{\rm aug}=0.0062665\ldots,
\end{gathered}
\]
all above $0.006195$, with $1-eE_{\rm box}>0.0096$, the remaining terms
far from binding, and the unfolded tile condition
$1-eT=0.003422\ldots>0$. The strict gaps absorb the rounding of $D$, the
logarithms, and the expected number of certification rounds. Hence real
3SUM takes expected $O(n^{2-0.006195})=O(n^{1.993805})$ time and the three
cubic problems expected $O(n^{2.993805})$ time.
\end{proof}
Majdoddin~\cite{M26} notes that the real-valued bounds of the source
follow by inserting his matrix theorem into the source's argument, which
keeps a quarter of the saving, and leaves open whether real-input
reductions can keep the whole saving. Lemma~\ref{lem:prefix-count} keeps
the whole unfolded saving with a Las Vegas guarantee, matching the guarantee
type of the source's real-input reduction; the corollary above is its
five-term instance. The deterministic integer bounds
of this section are also Las Vegas bounds, so for integer inputs the
Las Vegas rows of the summary table are superseded by $2.9926862626$ and
$1.9931055$. For lopsided clique instances see
Corollaries~\ref{cor:chunked-clique} and~\ref{cor:lopsided-five-clique}.

\section{Further displayed bounds}
\label{sec:further}
The consequences below include both substitutions into source reductions
and explicitly proved changes to their blocking, counting, or covering
steps. Logarithmic factors are absorbed only after checking strict slack.

\subsection*{Weighted $k$-Clique}
Use the standard weighted Ne\v{s}et\v{r}il--Poljak reduction underlying
Corollary 39 of the source. Group the $k$ vertex positions into groups of
$a,a,m$ positions, with $k=2a+m$. The tripartite graph has outer parts of
size at most $R=n^a$ and a middle part of size at most $H=n^m$.
Vertices represent valid internal cliques; edges require disjoint,
compatible choices. Assign each original edge weight to exactly one
tripartite edge, using the source's designated-edge-class convention for
weights internal to one group. A triangle then has exactly the original
$k$-clique weight. Missing edges can be filled with a sufficiently large
polynomial sentinel. Construction takes $O(R^2+RH)$ time for fixed $k$.
Minimum weight is obtained by one rectangular min-plus product followed
by adding the outer-edge weights and minimizing; maximum weight follows
by negating all original weights.

\begin{corollary}[Every fixed weighted clique size]
\label{cor:all-k}
For each fixed integer $k\ge3$, Zero-, Min-, and Max-Weight $k$-Clique
with polynomially bounded integer weights have Las Vegas expected time
\[
 O\left(n^{k-0.004057\lfloor k/2.02914\rfloor}\right)
\]
and deterministic time
\[
 O\left(n^{k-0.002125\lfloor k/2.09687\rfloor}\right).
\]
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
For the Las Vegas bound, put $a=\lfloor k/2.02914\rfloor$ and
$m=k-2a$. Group the $k$ vertex parts into groups of $a,a,m$ parts, as in the
lopsided reduction above. Then $m\ge0.02914a$, so its middle part of
$n^m$ vertices is at least $\sqrt D$ for $D=\Theta(n^{0.05828a})$,
with power-of-four rounding downward. Apply Lemma~\ref{lem:isolation}
with $R=\Theta(n^a)$ and the already certified folded parameters
$(c,\theta,e)=(21.0477,0.111971,0.05828)$. Each of its four cost terms
has strict saving greater than $0.004057a$ below $n^{2a+m}$, and the
folded tile condition has positive slack. The graph construction costs
$O(R^2+RH)$: its $R^2$ term saves $m\ge0.02914a>0.004057a$, and
its $RH$ term saves $a$. The same bit-scaling argument gives the min-plus product used
for minimum weight, and negating the input weights gives maximum weight.
This removes the previous upper restriction on $k$ by allowing the middle
group to grow and by eliminating the successful-scan term.

For the deterministic bound put $a=\lfloor k/2.09687\rfloor$, $m=k-2a$,
and retain the already certified parameters
$(c,\theta,e,z)=(21.6844,0.1163026,0.0565835,0.0021252)$, with
$D=\Theta(n^{ae})$. Then $m\ge0.09687a$. The cyclic-polynomial
multiplication argument below computes prime-selection ring operations in
quasilinear time in the modulus, giving a factor $P^2$ over all candidate
primes, where $P=\Theta(n^{a(e+z)/2})$. Uniform square blocking of an
$R\times H$ by $H\times R$ product costs
$\softO(R^2H/\min(R,H)^{3-\omega})$ ring operations: use blocks of
order $H$ when $H\le R$, and split the inner dimension into blocks of
order $R$ otherwise. This also covers $k=4$ without a separate exception.
Thus the saving for prime selection is at least
\[
 0.628\min(a,m)-a(e+z)
 \ge a(0.628\cdot0.09687-e-z)
 =0.00212566a>0.002125a.
\]
Successful scans save $m-a(e-z)/2\ge0.06964085a$, and the other costs
have the previously certified savings scaled by $a$. The middle part
contains full blocks, and both tile and graph-construction costs have
strict slack. The deterministic bit-scaling transfer and negation give
the minimum- and maximum-weight versions.
\end{proof}
Corollary~\ref{cor:det-all-edges-moments} above improves the deterministic
bound to $O(n^{k-0.003739\lfloor k/3\rfloor})$ for every fixed $k\ge3$,
with $O(n^{4.99276})$ for $k=5$. The square-case five-term bound improves
it further without any lopsided accounting.

\begin{corollary}[Chunked clique bounds from the square case]
\label{cor:chunked-clique}
For every fixed $k\ge3$, Zero-, Min-, and Max-Weight $k$-Clique with
polynomially bounded integer weights take
$O(n^{k-0.00705208\lfloor k/3\rfloor})$ deterministic time.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
Put $a=\lfloor k/3\rfloor$ and $m=k-2a\ge a$, and group the parts as
$(a,a,m)$ as in Corollary~\ref{cor:det-clique-decision}. Partition the
middle part of $n^m$ vertices into $n^{m-a}$ chunks of $n^a$ vertices.
Each chunk, with the two outer parts, is a square all-edges Exact Triangle
instance of size $N=n^a$ with weights polynomial in $N$, since $a$ is
fixed. Corollary~\ref{cor:aligned-cache-apsp} solves it deterministically in
$O(N^{3-0.00705208})$ time; the zero-weight all-edges answers are the
entrywise disjunction over chunks, and the min-plus product of the
lopsided instance is the entrywise minimum of the chunk products, so the
deterministic bit-scaling transfer gives the minimum- and maximum-weight
versions. The total is
$n^{m-a}N^{3-0.00705208}=O(n^{k-0.00705208a})$, and the graph construction
$O(R^2+RH)=O(n^{k-a})$ is smaller.
\end{proof}
Chunking needs $m\ge a$. The composite-incidence construction itself
needs only one block to fit in the middle part, which allows a split with
two nearly equal outer groups.

\begin{corollary}[Lopsided five-term clique bounds]
\label{cor:lopsided-five-clique}
For every fixed $k\ge3$, Zero-, Min-, and Max-Weight $k$-Clique with
polynomially bounded integer weights take deterministic time
\[
O\!\left(n^{\,k-0.00705208\lfloor k/2.046046346\rfloor}\right),
\]
where $2.046046346=2+b$ with the aligned-cache block exponent
$b=0.04604634600$. For example, $k=5$ gives $O(n^{4.98589584})$ and
$k=7$ gives $O(n^{6.97884376})$.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
Put $\alpha=\lfloor k/(2+b)\rfloor\ge1$ and $m=k-2\alpha$, so that
$m\ge b\alpha$, and group the parts as $(\alpha,\alpha,m)$: outer parts
$R=n^\alpha$, middle part $H=n^m=R^\mu$ with $\mu=m/\alpha\ge b$.
Run the algorithm of Corollary~\ref{cor:aligned-cache-apsp} on this
instance with every exponent measured in powers of $R$: blocks of
$h=\Theta(R^b)$ middle vertices fit because $b\le\mu$; for a modulus
$d=R^u$ each block is one folded product with $D=hd=R^{b+u}$ and outer
dimension $R\lceil\sqrt d\rceil=R^{1+u/2}$ on both sides; the incidences
$(a,b,B)$ number at most $2R^2H/h$. Lemma~\ref{lem:composite-incidence}
and Theorem~\ref{thm:cyclic-factor-apsp} use only $h$, the prime scale,
the divisor bound, and sums of exact per-incidence counts, so their
proofs apply with $2R^2H/h$ incidences, $\lceil\log_2(2H)\rceil=O(\log n)$
halving stages, and a final scan of $O(R^2)$ pairs. Every per-block cost
carries the factor $H/h$ and every per-incidence cost the factor
$R^2H/h$, while the budget is $R^2H=n^k$; hence the savings per unit
$\alpha$ are exactly the entries of (A12)--(A13), all exceeding
$0.00705208$ at the certified endpoints, and the tile condition
$1+u/2>(b+u)T$ is unchanged. Unlike Theorem~\ref{thm:moment-et}, the
selection is linear in the number of candidates and incidences, so no
regime depends on $H$ against $R$. The remaining costs are the graph
construction $O(R^2+RH)$ and the final scan, with savings $\mu\ge b$ and
$1$ below $R^{2+\mu}$, both above $0.00705208$. The zero-weight all-edges
answers decide and locate zero cliques; the rectangular $R\times H\times R$
min-plus product follows by the deterministic bit-scaling transfer, and
adding the outer edge weights and minimizing gives minimum weight, with
negation for maximum weight.
\end{proof}
This contains Corollary~\ref{cor:chunked-clique} as the case
$\alpha=\lfloor k/3\rfloor$, and it supersedes the $k=5$ bounds
$O(n^{4.991886})$ and $O(n^{4.99276})$ above and, being deterministic, the
Las Vegas clique bounds of Corollary~\ref{cor:all-k} and of the $(2,1,1)$
split below for every $k\ge3$. Majdoddin's Corollary~39
analogue~\cite{M26} gives the saving $0.0055\lfloor k/3\rfloor$. The split
is explicit and feasible, not claimed optimal; the fixed-profile parameters
of Corollary~\ref{cor:five-apsp} give the weaker
$O(n^{k-0.006324\lfloor k/2.0419064555\rfloor})$ by the same argument.


\paragraph{Four vertices.}
For $k=4$ the split $(2,1,1)$ beats $(1,1,2)$. Take outer parts
$R_1=n^2$ and $R_2=n$ and middle part $H=n$, fold the whole residue onto
the small side, so the rows are the $n^2$ vertices of $R_1$ and the columns
are the pairs $(b,\rho)$ with $\rho\in\mathbb Z_p$, and use the rectangular
tiles of Lemma~\ref{lem:subset-rect-tiles} in the clamped regime
$K_1=K$, $K_2=1$, which applies because $R_1/(R_2p)=n^{1-d/2}\ge K$ for
$D=n^d$ when $1-d/2-dC_K>0$. Per block the boxes cost $R_1R_2pD^{-G}$,
the queries $R_1R_2D^q$, and the encodings
$R_1N_0+R_2pKN_0\le R_1D^{A_0}+\sqrt{R_1R_2p}\,D^E$; over the $n/h$
blocks the savings below $n^4$ are $dG$, $d(1/2-q)$, $1-d(A_0-1/2)$, and
$3/2-d(E-1/4)$, with the small side holding a tile when $1+d/2>dA_0$.
Take $(c,\theta,d)=(19.8418,\;0.113322,\;0.06896)$: the savings are
$0.004514113\ldots$, $0.004514199\ldots$, $0.004784918\ldots$, and
$0.388997\ldots$, with $1-d/2-dC_K=0.768\ldots>0$ and
$1+d/2-dA_0=0.004784\ldots>0$. Hence Zero-, Min-, and Max-Weight
$4$-Clique with integer weights take $O(n^{3.995486})$ Las Vegas expected
time, against $O(n^{3.995943})$ from the balanced split; with the symmetric
fold of Lemma~\ref{lem:fold} the same split gives $O(n^{3.995561})$.

\subsection*{Real weighted cliques for every fixed $k$}
The rank-prefix oracle also permits a split that works for every fixed
$k\ge3$. Put
\[
e_1=0.056846,\quad u_1=e_1/2=0.028423,\quad
a=\left\lfloor\frac{k}{2+u_1}\right\rfloor,\qquad m=k-2a.
\]
Then $a\ge1$ and $m\ge au_1$. Group the $k$ vertex parts into groups of
$a,a,m$ parts. The standard weighted Ne\v{s}et\v{r}il--Poljak reduction
has outer sizes $R=n^a$ and middle size $H=n^m$. Use the certified real
parameters $(c,\theta)=(21.2733,0.111731)$, list length
$d=\max\{1,\lfloor R^{u_1}\rfloor\}\le H$, and the actual rounded
dimension $D=\Theta(R^{e_1}\log^5 R)$ of
Lemma~\ref{lem:prefix-count}.

The source's Lemma 36(a) applies to these rectangular products by
partitioning their $H$ middle indices into blocks of at most $d$ indices.
There are $\softO(H/d)$ counting calls, each with $d$ comparison
instances on $R$ row and $R$ column lists whose prescribed pair sets
have total size $R^2$. Its additional list and minimum-selection costs
are $\softO(RHd+R^2H/d)$. The resulting complete expected time is
\[
\softO\left(RH D^E+R^2H D^{-G}+R^2H D^{q-1/2}
             +RH D+RHd+R^2H/d\right).
\]
Relative to $R^2H=n^k$, the first three savings are respectively
$a(1-e_1E)$, $ae_1G$, and $ae_1(1/2-q)$, each strictly greater than
$0.003999a$. The remaining savings are $a(1-e_1)$, $a(1-u_1)$,
and $au_1$, and the strict tiling condition is
$1-e_1T>0$. All polylogarithmic factors are therefore absorbed.
Constructing the tripartite graph takes $O(R^2+RH)$ time for fixed
$k$; this fits as well, because $m\ge au_1>0.003999a$ and
$a>0.003999a$.

Exact Triangle tests the zero-weight case. For minimum weight, compute
the rectangular min-plus product on the same three parts, add the edge
between the two outer groups, and minimize over its $R^2$ entries;
negating the weights handles maximum weight. Thus Zero-, Min-, and
Max-Weight $k$-Clique with real weights have Las Vegas expected time
\[
O\!\left(n^{\,k-0.003999\lfloor k/2.028423\rfloor}\right)
\qquad\text{for every fixed }k\ge3.
\]
This is an algorithmic guarantee in the same real model as the source,
not an assertion of literature priority. The choice of $a$ is one
explicit feasible split, not a claim that the split is optimal.

With the five-term data structure the same split improves. Take the
parameters of Corollary~\ref{cor:five-real},
$e_1=0.08183$, $u_1=e_1/2=0.040915$, $a=\lfloor k/(2+u_1)\rfloor$, and
$m=k-2a\ge au_1$; the lists have length $d=\max\{1,\lfloor R^{u_1}\rfloor\}
\le H$ and the rounded dimension is $D=\Theta(R^{e_1}\log^5R)$. The cost
has the two encoder terms $RHD^{E_{\rm aug}}+RHD^{E_{\rm box}}$ in place of
$RHD^E$ and the query exponent $\max(q_1,q_2)$, and otherwise is the
display above. Relative to $n^k$ the savings are $a(1-e_1E_{\rm aug})$,
$a(1-e_1E_{\rm box})$, $ae_1G$, and $ae_1(1/2-q_i)$, each above
$0.006195a$ by the certificate of Corollary~\ref{cor:five-real}, with the
remaining savings $a(1-e_1)$, $a(1-u_1)$, $au_1$, the tiling condition
$1-e_1T>0$, and graph construction fitting because
$m\ge au_1>0.006195a$. Hence Zero-, Min-, and Max-Weight $k$-Clique with
real weights have Las Vegas expected time
\[
O\!\left(n^{\,k-0.006195\lfloor k/2.040915\rfloor}\right)
\qquad\text{for every fixed }k\ge3,
\]
which is below the display above for every $k\ge3$ and no worse than the chunked
square bound $0.006195\lfloor k/3\rfloor$ for every $k$.

\subsection*{Problems solved by one product or one APSP}
Negative Triangle and Min-Weight Triangle, also in their all-edges forms,
Metricity, min-plus product verification, Diameter, Radius, Median, the
Wiener index, and directed Minimum Weight Cycle with nonnegative weights
(the minimum over edges $(u,v)$ of $w(u,v)+d(v,u)$) each need a constant
number of $n\times n$ integer min-plus products or one APSP plus $O(n^2)$
work. By Corollary~\ref{cor:half-source-apsp} they inherit
$O(n^{2.9926862626})$ deterministic time, which is also a
Las Vegas bound, and with real weights $O(n^{2.993805})$ Las Vegas
expected time; the source displays $O(n^{2.9995})$. Exact Triangle with real
weights is covered by the source's Lemma 36(a) together with the min-plus
product, with the same call counts, so it also takes $O(n^{2.993805})$ Las
Vegas expected time.

\subsection*{Directed APSP with small integer weights}
The source's Theorem~33 turns a bound $O(n^{2+\mu-\varepsilon_1})$ for one
rectangular min-plus product into a bound $O(n^{2+\mu-\varepsilon''})$ for
directed APSP with weights of magnitude $\operatorname{polylog}n$, with
$\varepsilon''$ left unspecified. We make the step quantitative. Throughout,
$\bar\mu=0.5275$ and $\bar\omega=2.372$ are the source's displayed bounds:
$\omega(1,\bar\mu,1)\le1+2\bar\mu$ follows from $\mu<\bar\mu$ and the trivial
$\omega(1,\kappa+t,1)\le\omega(1,\kappa,1)+t$ (the table of
\cite{ADVXXZ25} lists $2.054999$ at $\bar\mu$), and $\omega\le\bar\omega$. Zwick's algorithm runs in $\softO(n^{2+\bar\mu})$.

\begin{lemma}[Uniform lopsided product bound]
\label{lem:uniform-lopsided}
Let $b=0.04604634600$ and $\delta=0.00705208$ be the block exponent and the
saving of Corollary~\ref{cor:aligned-cache-apsp}. For every fixed rational
$\rho\in[b,1]$, the min-plus product of an $n\times n^\rho$ by an
$n^\rho\times n$ matrix with integer entries of magnitude $n^{O(1)}$ and
$\infty$ entries is computed deterministically in $\softO(n^{2+\rho-\delta})$
time, where the logarithmic power depends only on the magnitude exponent
and the fixed parameters; a middle part of size $\widetilde\Theta(n^\rho)$ is
allowed. Like Corollary~\ref{cor:aligned-cache-apsp}, the bound is
asymptotic and holds only for astronomically large $n$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Replace $\infty$ by a sentinel $S=3W+1$, where $W$ bounds the finite
magnitudes; a sum containing a sentinel exceeds $2W$, so outputs above $2W$
are $\infty$. Run the bit-scaling transfer of the subsection on integer
APSP: $O(\log n)$ levels, each with two all-edges Exact Triangle calls on
the tripartite graph with outer parts of $R=C=n$ vertices and middle part
$H=n^\rho$, all weights polynomial. Solve each call exactly as in
Corollary~\ref{cor:lopsided-five-clique} with $\alpha=1$ and middle
exponent $\mu=\rho\ge b$: blocks of $h=\Theta(n^b)$ middle vertices fit,
every per-block cost carries the factor $H/h$, every per-incidence cost the
factor $R^2H/h=n^{2+\rho-b}$, and the budget is $R^2H=n^{2+\rho}$; so the
savings are exactly the entries of (A12)--(A13), all above $\delta$ at
their certified endpoints, with the unchanged tile condition. The graph
construction $O(R^2+RH)$ and the final scan $O(R^2)$ save $\rho\ge b>\delta$
and $1$. The strict gap absorbs the $O(\log n)$ levels.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Directed APSP with small integer weights, explicit exponent]
\label{thm:directed-apsp-explicit}
APSP in directed $n$-vertex graphs with integer weights of magnitude
$(\log n)^{O(1)}$ and no negative cycles, in particular directed unweighted
APSP, is solved deterministically in $O(n^{2.525754})$ time. More precisely,
for every certified chord slope $m<1$ with
$\omega(1,\kappa,1)\le1+2\bar\mu+m(\kappa-\bar\mu)$ on $[\bar\mu,\kappa_1]$,
and $c=1-m$, the exponent is $2+\bar\mu-\varepsilon''$ for every
\begin{gather*}
\varepsilon''<\frac{\delta c}{1+c},\qquad
\text{provided }\ \bar\mu+\frac{\delta}{1+c}<\kappa_1\\
\text{and }\ 1-\kappa+\omega(1,\kappa,1)<2+\bar\mu-\varepsilon''
\ \text{ at the certified nodes }\kappa\in[\kappa_1,1].
\end{gather*}
With the chord from $(\bar\mu,1+2\bar\mu)$ to $(1,\bar\omega)$ this is
$\varepsilon''<\delta(2+\bar\mu-\bar\omega)/(3-\bar\omega)
=0.0017461\ldots$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Run the deterministic reduction of the source's Theorem~33 with level ratio
$R=\lceil n^\gamma\rceil$, $\gamma=10^{-8}$. Its tables cost
$\softO(n^{\omega+\gamma})$ and its bridges $\softO(n^{2+\gamma})$ per
stage, with $O(1/\gamma)$ stages; both are below $n^{2+\bar\mu-\varepsilon''}$
since $\bar\omega+\gamma<2+\bar\mu-\varepsilon''$. The stage for
$s=t_{i+1}=n^{\sigma}$ needs one min-plus product of an $n\times n^\rho$ by
an $n^\rho\times n$ matrix with $\rho=1-\sigma+\gamma$ and finite entries of
magnitude $\softO(n^{1-\rho+\gamma})$, together with $\infty$ entries. We
bound its exponent $\Phi(\rho)$ by the minimum of three deterministic
methods. (i) Naive: $2+\rho$. (ii) Lemma~\ref{lem:uniform-lopsided}, for
$\rho\ge b$: $2+\rho-\delta$. (iii) Zwick's Lemma~3.3, cited by the source:
$\softO(W n^{\omega(1,\rho,1)+\varepsilon})$ for entries of magnitude $W$,
giving $g(\rho)=1-\rho+\gamma+\varepsilon+w(\rho)$ with
$\varepsilon=10^{-8}$ the matrix-multiplication exponent allowance.

\emph{The property of $\omega(1,\cdot,1)$ used.} Only this: if
$\omega(1,\kappa_0,1)\le w_0$ and $\omega(1,\kappa_1,1)\le w_1$ are
algorithmic bounds, then for rational $\lambda\in[0,1]$,
$\omega(1,(1-\lambda)\kappa_0+\lambda\kappa_1,1)\le(1-\lambda)w_0+\lambda w_1$,
by tensoring the two algorithms in rational proportions, exactly as in the
interpolation paragraph after Lemma~\ref{lem:rectangular-moment-selection}.
No global convexity, monotonicity, or lower bound $\omega(1,\kappa,1)\ge2$ is
needed; the certified point at $\bar\mu$ follows from $\mu<\bar\mu$ by
padding the middle dimension, since $\omega(1,\kappa+t,1)\le\omega(1,\kappa,1)+t$.
Hence $w(\rho)=1+2\bar\mu+m(\rho-\bar\mu)$ on $[\bar\mu,\kappa_1]$ with
$m=(w_1-1-2\bar\mu)/(\kappa_1-\bar\mu)$, and $g$ decreases on this segment at
rate $c=1-m>0$ from $g(\bar\mu)=2+\bar\mu+\gamma+\varepsilon$.

\emph{Crossover.} For $\rho<b$, $\Phi\le2+b$. For $b\le\rho\le\rho^*$ use
(ii), and for $\rho^*\le\rho\le\kappa_1$ use (iii), where
$2+\rho^*-\delta=g(\rho^*)$, that is
$\rho^*=\bar\mu+(\delta+\gamma+\varepsilon)/(1+c)$. Both bounds equal
$2+\bar\mu-\varepsilon_2$ there, with
\[
\varepsilon_2=\frac{\delta c-\gamma-\varepsilon}{1+c},
\]
and (ii) increases while (iii) decreases, so
$\max_{\rho\le\kappa_1}\Phi(\rho)\le2+\bar\mu-\varepsilon_2$. On
$[\kappa_1,1]$ the piecewise-linear $g$ is checked at its nodes. With the
single chord to $(1,\bar\omega)$, $c=(2+\bar\mu-\bar\omega)/(1-\bar\mu)$ and
$\varepsilon_2\to\delta(2+\bar\mu-\bar\omega)/(3-\bar\omega)$ as
$\gamma,\varepsilon\to0$. The exact-rational certificate gives
$\varepsilon_2=0.001746160812\ldots>0.001746$ with
$\rho^*=0.5328059\ldots\in[b,1]$, so every stage product costs
$O(n^{2+\bar\mu-0.001746})$ and the strict gap absorbs the fixed number of
stages, the $O(\log n)$ bit-scaling levels, and all polylogarithmic
factors. Every step is deterministic. The exponents are certified in the script
\texttt{verify\_\allowbreak directed\_\allowbreak apsp.py}.
\end{proof}
The reduction's own hypothesis form, with entries bounded by $n^{1-\mu}$
only and a saving $\varepsilon_1$ at $\rho=\bar\mu$ alone, gives instead
$\varepsilon''<\min\{\varepsilon_1(1-\bar\mu)/(3-\varepsilon_1),\
\varepsilon_1(2+\bar\mu-\bar\omega)/(3-\bar\omega)\}$: for $\rho<\bar\mu$
the padded instance of size $n^{(1-\rho)/(1-\bar\mu)}$ meets the naive
bound at $\bar\mu-\rho=\varepsilon_1(1-\bar\mu)/(3-\varepsilon_1)$, and
this side binds ($0.0011133$ at $\varepsilon_1=\delta$). The uniform lemma
removes that side. Chunking the outer parts into $n^{\bar\mu}$-sized square
products gives only $\varepsilon_1=\bar\mu\delta$. One further certified
row, $\omega(1,0.6,1)\le2.092351$ (\cite{ADVXXZ25}, Table~1), gives the chord slope
$m=0.5151\ldots$, $c=0.4848\ldots$, $\rho^*=0.5322\ldots<0.6$, and
$\varepsilon_2>0.0023025$, that is $O(n^{2.5251975})$; the SODA~2024 row
$\omega(1,0.55,1)\le2.066134$ gives $\varepsilon_2>0.0023667$ and
$O(n^{2.5251333})$. These two refinements use the additional external table rows of
\cite{ADVXXZ25} and \cite{VXXZ24}, recorded in the bibliography; the main
statement uses only the two numbers the source displays. The Las Vegas route through Lemma~\ref{lem:isolation} is not
better: a deterministic bound is a Las Vegas bound, and no certified Las
Vegas saving exceeds $\delta$.

% Compiled standalone with tectonic (no errors, no overfull boxes) at
% scratchpad/directed/standalone.tex; cross-references resolve inside the main file.

\subsection*{Hinted OMv}
The source's Corollary 40 refutes the three hinted OMv conjectures of
van den Brand, Nanongkai, and Saranurak for thin hints by preprocessing the
hint with the data structure. With $N=n$ and $D=n^\tau$, Phase 2 costs
$\softO(n^{1+\tau E}+n^{2-\tau G})$, Phase 3 is $n$ queries,
$\softO(n^{1+\tau q})$, and the tile condition is $\tau T<1$; for the
$uMv$ conjecture, $n/t_2$ products with $N=t_2$ and $D=t_1$ give the same
conditions with $\tau=t_1/t_2$. Two observations follow.
\begin{enumerate}
\item For $0<\tau\le1/18$, the parameters of the first corollary give
Phase 2 in $O(n^{2-0.07195\tau})$ and Phase 3 in $O(n^{1+0.42805\tau})$,
since $G>0.07195$, $q<0.42805$, and $18-E>0.07195$; the source displays
$0.063$ and $0.437$. Correspondingly Conjecture 5.12 of that paper fails for
$\tau_1\le\tau_2/18$ with Phase 3 in $O(n^{1+\tau_2-0.07195\tau_1})$ and
Phase 4 in $O(n^{\tau_2+0.42805\tau_1})$. Preprocessing $U$ times $N_{I,J}$ as
one $n\times t_1$ by $t_1\times t_2$ product with the rectangular tiles of
Lemma~\ref{lem:subset-rect-tiles}, instead of $n/t_2$ square products,
costs $\softO(n^{1+\tau_1A_0}+n^{(1+\tau_2)/2+\tau_1E}+n^{1+\tau_2-\tau_1G})$,
so the same Phase 3 and Phase 4 bounds hold for all
$\tau_1\le\min\{\tau_2/16.54,\,(1+\tau_2)/36\}$, since
$16.54>A_0+0.07195$ and $36>2(E+0.07195)$ at these parameters.
\item The conjectured Phase 3 bound is $n^{1+\tau-\varepsilon}$, so any
query exponent $q<1$ refutes it, not only $q<1/2$. The branch $c<10$ of
Theorem~\ref{thm:refined} then reaches beyond the source's limit
$\varepsilon^*=0.1204\ldots$: for $0<\tau\le0.14$ take $c=8.58$ and
$\theta=0.335$, where $G=0.00971\ldots>0.0097$, $q=0.99093\ldots<0.991$,
$1/0.14-E=0.01282\ldots>0.0097$, and $1/0.14>T$, giving Phase 2 in
$O(n^{2-0.0097\tau})$ and Phase 3 in $O(n^{1+0.991\tau})$; for
$0<\tau\le0.145$ take $c=8.29$ and $\theta=0.3385$, where $G>0.001$,
$q<0.999$, $1/0.145-E>0.001$, and $1/0.145>T$, giving $O(n^{2-0.001\tau})$
and $O(n^{1+0.999\tau})$. Conjecture 5.12 fails accordingly for
$\tau_1\le0.145\,\tau_2$ with square products, and with one clamped
rectangular product for all $\tau_1\le\min\{0.1726\,\tau_2,\,(1+\tau_2)/13.8\}$,
using the $c<10$ encoding of Lemma~\ref{lem:subset-rect-tiles}: at
$c=8.29$, $\theta=0.3385$ one has $1-0.1726(A_{\rm cl}+0.001)>0$,
$1-0.1726A_0>0$, and $1-2(E+0.001)/13.8>0$. The limits of these families,
about $0.1457$ and $0.1736$, are not claimed; the savings vanish there.
\end{enumerate}
All of these are deterministic, as in the source.

\subsection*{Rectangular min-plus convolution}
The square block size in the usual min-plus-convolution reduction loses
nearly half the available saving. A rectangular block choice retains much
more. For any integer $1\le s\le n$, put $r=\lceil n/s\rceil$ and form
\[
 Q[u,v]=b[su+v],\qquad P_z[d,v]=a[sd+z-v],
\]
where $0\le u<r$, $0\le v,z<s$, and \emph{$0\le d\le r$}. Replace
absent entries by a sentinel $S>3W$, where $W$ bounds all input
magnitudes. For $F_z=P_z\star Q^{\mathsf T}$, every output satisfies
\[
 c[sw+z]=\min_{\substack{0\le u<r\\0\le w-u\le r}}F_z[w-u,u],
 \qquad 0\le sw+z\le2n-2.
\]
Indeed, writing the index of the $b$ entry as $j=su+v$ forces the index
of the $a$ entry to be $i=s(w-u)+z-v$; every genuine pair occurs, and
values of $d=w-u$ outside $[0,r]$ cannot give a valid $a$ index. The
sentinel rule prevents absent entries from beating a genuine sum, also
for negative inputs. Both boundary rows are retained and all $2n-1$
outputs are produced.

There are $s$ products of an $(r+1)\times s$ by $s\times r$ matrix.
Padding the outer dimensions to $r+1$ changes only constants. Forming all
inputs costs $O(ns+s^2)$ and reading all product entries and taking their
diagonal minima costs $O(sr^2)$. Take
\[
 s=\lceil n^\beta\rceil,\quad r=\Theta(n^\alpha),\quad
 \alpha=1-\beta,\quad
 D=4^{\lfloor\log_4(r^e)\rfloor}=\Theta(r^e).
\]
Multiplying \eqref{eq:isolation-costs} by the $s$ products gives the four savings below $n^2$
\begin{equation}
 \alpha\bigl(1-e(E-1/4)\bigr),\quad
 \alpha eG,\quad
 \alpha e(1/2-q),\quad
 \alpha(1-3e/4).
\label{eq:rectangular-lv-savings}
\end{equation}
The folded tile condition has positive gap
$\alpha(1-e(T-1/4))$, and the middle-size condition holds when
$\beta-\alpha e/2>0$. Formation and extraction save $1-\beta$ and
$\beta$, respectively. Choose
\[
 (c,\theta,e,\beta)=(21.487,\;0.111507,\;0.057093,\;0.027755).
\]
Then $\beta-\alpha e/2=0.0000008081075>0$. The box, query, and encoding
savings are respectively
\[
 0.003943484351370\ldots,\quad
 0.003943490849588\ldots,\quad
 0.003953476962084\ldots.
\]
All terms have strict saving greater than $0.003943$. Thus integer
min-plus convolution takes Las Vegas expected
\[
 \boxed{O(n^{1.996057})}.
\]
This improves the $O(n^{1.997972})$ square-block consequence.

The deterministic version also benefits from rectangular blocks. Use the
explicit folded parameters
\[
 (c,\theta,e,z)=(23.1026,\;0.1151488,\;0.053132,\;0.00212056),
\]
and write $A=(e+z)/2$, $B=(e-z)/2$. For each product choose a prime of
scale $r^A$ minimizing its modular triangle count, and use middle blocks
of scale $r^B$. The same averaging and distinct-false-triple charging as
in the deterministic reduction give, after all $s$ products, savings
\begin{equation}
 \alpha(eG-z),\quad
 \alpha\bigl(e(1/2-q)-z/2\bigr),\quad
 \alpha z,\quad
 \alpha\bigl(1-e(E-1/4)-3z/4\bigr)
\label{eq:rectangular-det-savings}
\end{equation}
for boxes, queries, failed scans, and encodings. Construction and successful
scans save $\alpha(1-3A/2)$ and $\beta-\alpha B$. The tile condition
has gap $\alpha(1+A/2-eT)>0$.

Prime selection adds a constraint, but its ring operations admit a useful
improvement. Degree-$p$ cyclic-polynomial multiplication takes
$\softO(p)$ word operations, rather than quadratic time, when coefficients
have $O(\log n)$ bits. To justify that bit bound within the matrix
algorithm, fix an exact rational bilinear matrix-multiplication algorithm
of constant block size and exponent less than $2.372$, using the same
matrix-multiplication bound as the source. Its recursion has depth
$O(\log s)$. Each input linear form and output reconstruction uses fixed
rational coefficients; their numerators, denominators, and the numbers of
terms grow at most as a fixed constant to this depth. Clearing the
predetermined common denominator therefore uses $O(\log n)$ bits.
The bilinear leaf products and all reconstructed coefficients also have
$O(\log n)$ bits, since the degree $p$ and all dimensions are polynomial
in $n$. The bilinear identities remain valid over the commutative
$\mathbb Q$-algebra $\mathbb Q[x]/(x^p-1)$.

For a concrete multiplication, split signed integer coefficient arrays
into positive and negative parts, use a power-of-two Kronecker base larger
than any coefficient of their ordinary convolution, and multiply the
resulting $O(p\log n)$-bit integers exactly. Fast integer multiplication,
coefficient unpacking, and folding degrees modulo $p$ cost
$\softO(p)$ word operations; the fixed common-denominator bookkeeping
does not change this bound. The final triangle counts are exact integers.
Consequently $O(P)$ candidate primes of size $\Theta(P)$ cost a factor
$\softO(P^2)$ in total.

Since $s\le r$, split the $r\times s$ by $s\times r$ matrix product
into $O((r/s)^2)$ square products of order $O(s)$. The resulting
prime-selection cost is $\softO(r^2s^{0.372}r^{2A})$ per rectangular
product. Over all $s$ products it saves
\[
 0.628\beta-2\alpha A
\]
below $n^2$. With $\beta=0.083712$, this saving is $0.00194387830272$.
The box, query, failed-scan, and encoding savings in \eqref{eq:rectangular-det-savings} are respectively
\[
 0.001943037983720\ldots,\quad
 0.001943040668583\ldots,\quad
 0.001943043681280,\quad
 0.001945978936429\ldots.
\]
All conditions and auxiliary costs have strict slack above saving
$0.001943$. Hence the deterministic bound is
\[
 \boxed{O(n^{1.998057})},
\]
improving the square-block $O(n^{1.998938})$ consequence of the single-prime
product bound. Polynomial input
weights remain polynomial in the dimensions of each product, and all
rounding, bit-scaling, and amplification logarithms are absorbed by the
strict gaps before the final exponents are rounded. The same square-block
reduction, $\sqrt n$ min-plus products of $\sqrt n\times\sqrt n$ matrices
with weights polynomial in $\sqrt n$, applied to the deterministic product
bound of Corollary~\ref{cor:aligned-cache-apsp}, gives
$2-0.00705208/2=1.99647396$, so deterministic integer min-plus convolution
takes $O(n^{1.996474})$ time; the rectangular Las Vegas bound
$O(n^{1.996057})$ remains smaller.

\begin{corollary}[Five-term rectangular min-plus convolution]
\label{cor:five-convolution}
Integer min-plus convolution of two length-$n$ sequences with entries of
magnitude $n^{O(1)}$ has a Las Vegas algorithm with expected time
$O(n^{1.993927})$ and a deterministic algorithm with running time
$O(n^{1.993927})$.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
Use the block reduction of this subsection unchanged, writing $\bar r$ for
its row count $r=\lceil n/s\rceil$: $s=\lceil n^\beta\rceil$,
$\bar r=\Theta(n^\alpha)$ with $\alpha=1-\beta$, both boundary rows
$0\le d\le\bar r$, the sentinel $S>3W$, and all $2n-1$ outputs. There are
$s$ min-plus products with outer dimensions $R=C=\bar r+1$ and middle
dimension $H=s$, whose entries are polynomial in $\bar r$ because $\alpha$ is
fixed. Forming them costs $O(ns+s^2)$ and reading all product entries and
taking the diagonal minima costs $O(s\bar r^2)$, with savings $1-\beta$ and
$\beta$ below $n^2$. Each product is computed by the priority-preserving
bit-scaling transfer through $O(\log n)$ all-edges Exact Triangle calls on
the parts $R,H,C$ with matrix-local shifts. Write $\varrho$ for the prefix
parameter $r$ of Lemma~\ref{lem:five-profile}, fix
\[
(c,\theta,\varrho,y,e,\beta)=
(11.0588596,\;0.0563850479,\;0.0383287249,\;0.0249694743,\;0.0814836742,\;0.03914792),
\]
and put $D=4^{\lfloor\log_4\bar r^{\,e}\rfloor}=\Theta(\bar r^{\,e})$ and
$b=e/2$. The exact-rational certificate gives
\[
\begin{array}{lll}
T=12.480128412\ldots,&G=0.077579669\ldots,&\\
E_{\rm aug}=12.444817353\ldots,&E_{\rm box}=12.402548742\ldots,&\\
q_1=0.422420330988\ldots,&q_2=0.422420331044\ldots,&
\end{array}
\]
the tile margins $1-e(T-1/4)>0.003444$ and $1-bT>0.49$, and the
middle-size margin $\beta-\alpha e/2>1.04\cdot10^{-6}$, so $H=s\ge\sqrt D$
for all sufficiently large $n$.

\emph{Las Vegas.} Apply Lemma~\ref{lem:isolation} to every all-edges call
with $R=\bar r+1$ and $H=s$, using Lemma~\ref{lem:five-rect-tiles} with
$R=C=(\bar r+1)\lceil\sqrt p\rceil$ in place of Theorem~\ref{thm:refined}.
The lemma uses the data structure only through its preprocessing bound, now
$\softO(ND^{E_{\rm aug}}+ND^{E_{\rm box}}+N^2D^{-G})$, its query bound
$\softO(D^{\max(q_1,q_2)})$, and its tile condition
$N\ge D^T\operatorname{polylog}$; no relation between $E$ and $T$ is used in
its proof, so the five-term exponents $E_{\rm aug},E_{\rm box}<T$ are
admissible. The folded instances have $N=\Theta(\bar rD^{1/4})$ up to logarithms,
which satisfies the tile condition by the first margin, and there are
$\softO(HD^{-1/2})$ blocks by the middle-size margin. Multiplying
\eqref{eq:isolation-costs}, with the two encoder terms, by the $s$ products
gives the savings
\[
\alpha eG,\quad\alpha e(1/2-q_1),\quad\alpha e(1/2-q_2),\quad
\alpha\bigl(1-e(E_{\rm aug}-1/4)\bigr),\quad
\alpha\bigl(1-e(E_{\rm box}-1/4)\bigr),\quad\alpha(1-3e/4)
\]
for boxes, residual queries, stored queries, the two encoder families, and
folded matrix formation; the certificate bounds them below by
\[
0.0060740038,\quad0.0060740038,\quad0.0060740038,\quad0.0060740032,\quad
0.00938,\quad0.90,
\]
while $1-\beta>0.96$ and $\beta>0.039$. The isolation trials, label
decoding, exact checks, and fallback scans are charged inside
\eqref{eq:isolation-costs}. All savings exceed $\delta=0.006073$ with a
strict gap above $1.0\cdot10^{-6}$, which absorbs the logarithmic factors of
amplification, isolation, bit scaling, and the rounding of $D$ and $s$.
Fresh randomness in every adaptive call preserves the expected bound.

\emph{Deterministic.} Apply Lemma~\ref{lem:composite-incidence-lopsided}
with $\Lambda=100000$, $h=\Theta(\bar r^{\,b})$, and the oracle obtained by
folding the composite modulus into the outer indices, as in the paragraph
following Lemma~\ref{lem:composite-incidence}, and preprocessing with
Lemma~\ref{lem:five-rect-tiles}. For a modulus $d=\bar r^{\,u}$,
$0\le u\le b$ up to polylogarithms, there are $O(H/h)$ folded products with
$N=(\bar r+1)\lceil\sqrt d\rceil$ and inner dimension
$hd=\bar r^{\,e}\operatorname{polylog}(n)$ at the completed modulus, padded to
a power of four with the polylogarithmic factor absorbed as in
Corollary~\ref{cor:five-apsp}, and at most $O(\bar r^2H/h)$ requested incidences. Charging
$\softO(\bar r^{\,\eta})$ candidates per round with $\eta=b/\Lambda$ and
$O(\Lambda\log n)$ rounds, and multiplying by the $s$ products, the savings
below $n^2$ are
\[
\alpha\bigl(b-u+(b+u)G-\eta\bigr),\quad
\alpha\bigl(b-(b+u)q_i-\eta\bigr),\quad
\alpha\bigl(1+b-u/2-(b+u)E_j-\eta\bigr),\quad
\alpha(1-3u/2-\eta),\quad\alpha(b-\eta)
\]
for boxes, the two query terms, the two encoder families, folded matrix
formation, and incidence bookkeeping, with tile margin
$\alpha(1+u/2-(b+u)T)$. All decrease in $u$, so the endpoint $u=b$ is
binding, where they equal the Las Vegas expressions minus $\alpha\eta$; the
tile margin is certified at both endpoints. Computing the residues
$w(a,b)\bmod d$ saves $\beta-\alpha\eta$ and the final scans cost $O(RC)$
per product, saving $\beta$. The certificate gives
$\eta=4.07\cdot10^{-7}$ and endpoint savings above
\[
0.0060736123,\quad0.0060736123,\quad0.0060736123,\quad0.0060736117,\quad
0.00938,\quad0.90,\quad0.039,
\]
all above $\delta=0.006073$ with a strict gap above $6.1\cdot10^{-7}$. This
absorbs the fixed-$\Lambda$ logarithmic factors, including the
$(\log n)^{\Theta(\Lambda)}$ in the completed modulus, bit scaling, and
rounding; as in Corollary~\ref{cor:five-apsp}, the bound is asymptotic and
no practical speedup is asserted. Both guarantees therefore give
$O(n^{2-0.006073})=O(n^{1.993927})$, improving the rectangular Las Vegas
$O(n^{1.996057})$, the deterministic $O(n^{1.998057})$, and the square-block
consequence $O(n^{1.996474})$ of Corollary~\ref{cor:aligned-cache-apsp}. The
unrounded maximal cost exponents are $1.9939259967\ldots$ and
$1.9939263882\ldots$; the floating-point suprema of this family, about
$0.0060740$ and $0.0060736$, are not claimed. The exponents are certified
in the script
\texttt{verify\_five\_\allowbreak term\_\allowbreak convolution.py}.
\end{proof}

\subsection*{MonoConvolution}
The source quotes, from Lincoln, Polak, and Vassilevska Williams, that an
$O(n^{2-\varepsilon})$-time 3SUM algorithm gives MonoConvolution in
$\softO(n^{3/2-\varepsilon/(8-2\varepsilon)})$ time. With the deterministic
$\varepsilon=0.0068945$ proved in
Theorem~\ref{thm:deterministic-cyclic-3sum}, the inherited exponent is
\[
 \frac32-\frac{0.0068945}{8-2(0.0068945)}
 =\frac{11972422}{7986211}
 =1.49913669949\ldots<1.4991368.
\]
Hence MonoConvolution takes deterministic $O(n^{1.4991368})$ time through
the quoted reduction. That reduction loses a factor two in the saving
because its light-value step calls All-Integers 3SUM through the
reduction of Vassilevska Williams and Williams, which turns an
$O(n^{2-\varepsilon})$ decision algorithm into an
$O(n^{2-\varepsilon/2})$ All-Integers algorithm.
Corollary~\ref{cor:all-integers-3sum} gives All-Integers 3SUM directly in
$O(n^{2-\varepsilon})$ time with $\varepsilon=0.0068945$. Rerunning the
same argument, set $a=(1-\varepsilon)/(2-\varepsilon)>1/3$ and
$t=\lceil n^a\rceil$. The $t$ most frequent values are handled by $t$
Boolean convolutions in $\softO(tn)$ time. For each remaining value $i$,
let $A_i,B_i,C_i$ be its three sets of occurrence indices, with total
size $n_i\le3n/t$; over all such values, $\sum_i n_i\le3n$.
When $n_i\le t$, enumerate $A_i\times B_i$ and look up each sum in
sorted $C_i$. These small classes cost
$\softO(\sum_{n_i\le t}n_i^2)\le\softO(3nt)$ in total.
For every remaining class $n_i>t\ge n^a$, its indices are at most
$n<n_i^{1/a}<n_i^3$, so the polynomial-magnitude hypothesis of
Corollary~\ref{cor:all-integers-3sum} holds with a fixed exponent.
Applying that corollary only to these classes costs at most
$\softO(\sum_{n_i>t}n_i^{2-\varepsilon})
\le\softO(3n(3n/t)^{1-\varepsilon})$. Sorting and writing all answers
cost $\softO(n)$ and are covered as well. The choice of $t$ balances
$tn$ and $n(n/t)^{1-\varepsilon}$ up to constant factors, giving total time
\[
\softO\!\left(n^{(3-2\varepsilon)/(2-\varepsilon)}\right),\qquad
\frac{3-2\varepsilon}{2-\varepsilon}=1.498270412680\ldots<1.498271,
\]
so MonoConvolution takes deterministic $O(n^{1.498271})$ time. The
exponent is certified in the script
\texttt{verify\_five\_\allowbreak term\_\allowbreak realinput.py}.
The earlier Las Vegas $\varepsilon=0.003999$ also retains its valid
$O(n^{1.4995})$ expected-time consequence, now dominated by the
deterministic bound. These use the source's
reduction and its assumptions unchanged; the strict numerical gap
absorbs its logarithmic factors.

\subsection*{Deterministic exact witness counting}
\begin{lemma}[Purifying a window with known genuine multiplicity]
\label{lem:known-window-purification}
Let $A,B,T$ be integer matrices with polynomially bounded entries, and
partition the middle labels into blocks $B_0$ of actual sizes at most
$h=\Theta(n^b)$, where $0<b<1$.  Write
$s_k(a,b)=A_{ak}+B_{kb}-T_{ab}$.  Suppose the exact counts
\[
 G_\ell(a,b,B_0)=\#\{k\in B_0:s_k(a,b)=\ell\},
 \qquad \ell\in\{-1,0,1\},
\]
are already known.  Given any two entry masks
$\alpha_{ak},\beta_{kb}\in\{0,1\}$, the twelve counts
\[
 N_{\ell,i,j}(a,b,B_0)=
 \#\{k\in B_0:s_k(a,b)=\ell,\ \alpha_{ak}=i,\ \beta_{kb}=j\}
\]
can be recovered deterministically using the same asymptotic number of
modular counting calls and the same modulus scale as composite incidence
certification.  In particular, a counting oracle for all three shifted
targets at moduli $d\le h\operatorname{polylog}(n)$, applied to the
entry-masked input matrices, gives the twelve
counts in $\softO(n^{3-s+b/R}+n^3/h)$ time if each oracle call costs
$\softO(n^{3-s})$ and $R\ge1$ is a fixed integer.  The polylogarithmic
power may depend on $R$ and the input-magnitude bound.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
For an incidence $x=(a,b,B_0)$ put $G(x)=G_{-1}(x)+G_0(x)+G_1(x)$.
An incidence with $G(x)=0$ has all twelve desired counts zero and can be
finished immediately.  For any modulus $d$ divisible by $3$ define
\[
 C_d(x)=\sum_{\ell=-1}^{1}
       \#\{k\in B_0:s_k(a,b)\equiv\ell\pmod d\}.
\]
The three target residues are distinct.  Each original middle label is
counted at most once, every genuine window label is counted, and hence
$C_d(x)=G(x)+F_d(x)$ with $F_d(x)$ a nonnegative integer.  Initially
$d=3$, so $C_3(x)=|B_0|$ exactly, without an oracle call.  Dummy labels
introduced by rectangular padding are absent from every count.

Fix the active incidence set $\mathcal I$ for one purification stage.
If a false label survives a modulus divisible by $3$, its target
$\ell\in\{-1,0,1\}$ is uniquely determined by its residue modulo $3$.
Thus the target does not change when further prime factors are appended.
For this false label $(s_k-\ell)/3$ is a nonzero integer.  Let
$U'\ge1$ bound the magnitude of every such nonzero integer and put
$\zeta=\max(1,\ln U')$.  Choose
\[
 P\ge 4\zeta(4h)^{1/R},\qquad P\ge8\zeta,
\]
within a constant factor of these bounds, and sufficiently large.
Enumerate the primes in $[P,2P)$, excluding $3$ and all primes previously
selected in this stage.  For all sufficiently large $n$, the number $M$
of remaining candidates is at least $P/(4\ln P)$, even after these
constant-in-$n$ exclusions.  Each false label has at most
$\lambda=\lfloor\ln U'/\ln P\rfloor$ candidate prime divisors.
Consequently $\lambda/M\le4\zeta/P=:a\le1/2$.

For each of all $R$ rounds, compute $C_{dp}(x)$ for every remaining
candidate prime $p$ and every $x\in\mathcal I$.  Choose a single common
prime minimizing $\sum_{x\in\mathcal I}C_{dp}(x)$ and set $d\gets dp$.
The true mass $\sum G(x)$ is independent of the candidate.  Averaging
over candidates therefore contracts the total false mass by at most
$a$ in every round.  Starting from $F_3(x)\le h$, after all $R$ rounds
the chosen modulus satisfies
\[
 \sum_{x\in\mathcal I}F_d(x)
 \le h|\mathcal I|a^R\le |\mathcal I|/4.
\]
At this same chosen modulus, mark every incidence with $C_d(x)=G(x)$.
At least three quarters of the active incidences are marked, since
every unmarked incidence contributes an integer at least one to the
false mass.  Equality proves that every modular survivor in the union
of the three residue classes is a genuine window label.  In particular,
it proves the absence of false labels separately in each target class.

Now form the four masked products, one for each $(i,j)\in\{0,1\}^2$,
by keeping an $A$-entry only when $\alpha=i$ and a $B$-entry only when
$\beta=j$.  An excluded entry is zero in the folded or cyclic input
array (an absent incidence), not an edge of numerical weight zero.
Query the three target residues at the same chosen modulus
for every marked incidence.  Their modular counts are exactly
$N_{\ell,i,j}$: masking removes labels and introduces none.  These
four products are made only after the common modulus is chosen.
Remove the marked incidences, restart with $d=3$, and continue until
none remain.  Since initially there are $O(n^3/h)$ incidences,
$O(\log n)$ stages suffice.  No fallback scans and no whole-pair
discarding are needed.

Each candidate modulus is $3$ times a product of at most $R$ distinct
primes, so it is at most $3(2P)^R=h\operatorname{polylog}(n)$.
There are $\softO(n^{b/R})$ candidates and $O(R\log n)$ rounds in
total.  The sieve, exact comparisons, candidate summation, survivor
bookkeeping, and the last prime-selection round are charged.  Counts,
moduli, and their sums have $O(\log n)$ bits.  Three target queries and
four masked products change only constant factors.  The finitely many
small input sizes can be handled by direct enumeration.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Deterministic exact all-edges witness counting]
\label{thm:deterministic-exact-witness-counting}
For polynomially bounded integer matrices $A,B,T$, all the counts
\[
 Z_{ab}=\#\{k:A_{ak}+B_{kb}=T_{ab}\}
\]
can be computed deterministically in $O(n^{2.99294792})$ time, using the
aligned five-term cyclic counting oracle and its certified parameters.
These counts retain multiplicities of original middle labels.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Choose nonnegative integer shifts $a_0,b_0$ such that
$A+a_0$, $B+b_0$, and $T+a_0+b_0$ are entrywise nonnegative.  This
is possible with polynomially bounded shifts, and preserves every
sum $A_{ak}+B_{kb}-T_{ab}$.  Denote the shifted matrices by
$\widehat A,\widehat B,\widehat T$.  Use a common number $L=O(\log n)$
of bits, so their prefixes obtained by dividing by $2^L$ and taking
floors are all zero.  At this base level initialize
$G_0(a,b,B_0)=|B_0|$ and $G_{-1}=G_1=0$ for every actual block.

Suppose the current prefix matrices are $A',B',T'$ and the next
prefixes are
\[
 A^+=2A'+\alpha,\qquad B^+=2B'+\beta,\qquad
 T^+=2T'+\gamma,
 \qquad \alpha,\beta,\gamma\in\{0,1\}\text{ entrywise}.
\]
If a label has old signed gap $\ell=A'_{ak}+B'_{kb}-T'_{ab}$, its
new signed gap is
\[
 2\ell+\alpha_{ak}+\beta_{kb}-\gamma_{ab}.
\]
The residual lies in $[-1,2]$.  An old gap at least $2$ therefore
becomes at least $3$, and an old gap at most $-2$ becomes at most $-2$.
Thus a label in the new window $\{-1,0,1\}$ necessarily belonged
to the old window.  This nesting holds for arbitrary target matrices;
no minimum or nonnegative-gap promise is used.

Apply Lemma~\ref{lem:known-window-purification} to the known old-window
counts, using the next input bits $\alpha,\beta$ as its masks.  The
new exact per-block counts are
\[
 G_r^+(a,b,B_0)=
 \sum_{\substack{\ell\in\{-1,0,1\},\ i,j\in\{0,1\}\\
                 2\ell+i+j-\gamma_{ab}=r}}
 N_{\ell,i,j}(a,b,B_0),\qquad r\in\{-1,0,1\}.
\]
The target bit $\gamma_{ab}$ is used only in this scalar update;
it introduces no extra matrix dimension or mask family.  In particular,
an incidence with zero old-window count remains zero and needs no
oracle call.  Induction over the $L$ bit levels proves every stored
count exact.  At the last level return $Z_{ab}=\sum_{B_0}G_0(a,b,B_0)$.

For completeness, the oracle implementation is unchanged except for
constant factors.  For one candidate modulus it uses the same input
weights $A',B'$ and three targets $T'-1,T',T'+1$.  Each target changes
only the requested folded position and cyclic coefficient, and is
queried once per incidence.  The four post-selection input masks give
four more products of exactly the same dimensions.  Use $h=n^b$ with
\[
 b=0.04604634600,\qquad R=1000000000,\qquad S=768979045.
\]
In the direct phase fold the modulus $3$ times the selected prime
product.  At the split freeze $3$ times the first $S$ selected primes
as the physical modulus and place the remaining selected factors in
the cyclic coefficient ring.  Excluding prime $3$ and choosing all
other factors distinctly preserves coprimality.  The constant factor
$3$ changes no dimension exponent, and every direct or cyclic phase
endpoint is the same as in the aligned certificate.  In particular,
\[
 u=bS/R=0.03540867517281957,\quad
 v=b-u=0.01063767082718043,\quad e=b+u,
\]
and the candidate charge is still $b/R$.  Every selected and tested
modulus, including those in the final greedy round, is charged.
Complete cyclic coefficient vectors are charged in preprocessing and
residual contractions; a stored box only permits a scalar coefficient
read because its full polynomial has already been stored.  As in the
aligned oracle, divide the complete residual-plus-box numerator by
$4^{L_{\rm tens}}$, where $L_{\rm tens}$ is that oracle's tensor depth,
coefficientwise before comparing its exact integer counts;
individual terms need not be divisible.

The extra count tables, their updates, and the output accumulation use
$O(n^3/h)$ entries and work per bit level.  This is already within
the certified incidence-bookkeeping cost.  The constant number of
targets and masks, $O(\log n)$ purification stages, $O(\log n)$ bit
levels, and fixed-$R$ polynomial logarithmic and exact-bit costs are
absorbed by the same strict slack in the 47-inequality aligned
certificate.  No new numerical witness or changed exponent is needed.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Exact min-plus multiplicities]
\label{cor:exact-minplus-multiplicities}
The minimum values and the exact numbers of minimizing original middle
labels for integer min-plus product can be computed deterministically in
$O(n^{2.99294792})$ time under the same polynomial-magnitude assumption.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
First compute the min-plus product $C$ using the existing deterministic
algorithm.  Then apply Theorem~\ref{thm:deterministic-exact-witness-counting}
with target $T=C$.  The two calls have the same exponent.  This counts
minimizing middle labels in one matrix product, not shortest paths or
their decompositions across successive APSP squarings.
\end{proof}

\subsection*{Earlier counting construction with one-sided error}
Running the amplified reduction without any scans gives counts. For each
sampled prime $p_i$ and block $B$, the folded product returns
$\mathrm{Count}_i(a,b,B)=Z(a,b,B)+F_i(a,b,B)$, the number of $c\in B$ with
$S(a,b,c)\equiv0\pmod{p_i}$, where $Z$ counts exact zeros and $F_i\ge0$
counts false positives. Output $\widetilde Z(a,b)=\sum_B\min_i\mathrm{Count}_i(a,b,B)$.
Always $\widetilde Z\ge Z$, with equality unless some pair/block has
$F_i>0$ for every $i$, which by the block-level bound of
Theorem~\ref{thm:amplified} has probability at most $2^{-k}$; with
$k=\lceil(\gamma+3)\log_2n\rceil$ primes a union bound over the
$O(n^3/h)$ pair/blocks gives error probability at most $n^{-\gamma}$. The
cost is that of Theorem~\ref{thm:amplified} without the scan terms, and it
is a worst-case bound, since only the scans had random cost. So the number
of zero triangles through every outer pair, hence the total number of zero
triangles, is computed in worst-case $O(n^{2.995943})$ time by a Monte Carlo
algorithm that never undercounts and is exact with probability
$1-n^{-\gamma}$. Counting the witnesses of every entry of an integer
min-plus product first needs the product itself from the Las Vegas
algorithm above, whose time is only expected. Restart it whenever a run
exceeds twice a uniform upper bound on its expected time, for at most
$\lceil(\gamma+1)\log_2n\rceil$ independent attempts;
by Markov's inequality all attempts fail with probability at most
$n^{-\gamma-1}$, and the strict gap absorbs the logarithmic factor.
If every attempt times out, output $n$ for every witness count; this cannot
undercount. Otherwise run the counting algorithm on the product with error
parameter $\gamma+1$. For $n\ge2$, the total error probability is at most
$2n^{-\gamma-1}\le n^{-\gamma}$. Witness counting therefore has the
same worst-case time and error probability. This earlier construction has
no exactness certificate and retains its one-sided Monte Carlo guarantee.
The separate signed-window construction in
Theorem~\ref{thm:deterministic-exact-witness-counting} supersedes this
integer all-edges and min-plus witness-counting bound with deterministic
exact counts; its certificate uses genuine window counts inherited from
the preceding bit level.
The following direct reduction gives a Monte Carlo bound for integer
$\#3\mathrm{SUM}$.


\subsection{Deterministic exact 3SUM counts with fixed buckets}
\label{sec:deterministic-cyclic-3sum}

\begin{lemma}[Fixed-bucket cyclic count lifting]
\label{lem:fixed-bucket-cyclic-counting}
Let $A,B,C$ be sets of at most $n$ polynomially bounded integers.
Set $s=\lceil n^b\rceil$, where $0<b<1$ is fixed.  There is a fixed
list of $\softO(n^2/s)$ sub-bucket incidences, each containing at most
$h=s^2$ original pairs, on which the signed-window count refinement
can be implemented by exact cyclic thin-product queries.  At physical
modulus $d$ and cyclic modulus $q$, with $\gcd(d,q)=1$, these products
have dimensions
\[
 D=\Theta(d),\qquad N=O((n/s)\lceil\sqrt d\rceil).
\]
There are a constant number of target queries per incidence and a
constant number of input parity masks.  Formation of each product,
including all coefficient arrays, costs $O(n+qND)$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Choose a prime $r\in[\lceil n/s\rceil,2\lceil n/s\rceil]$
minimizing
$\Phi(r)=\sum_i(|A_i|^2+|B_i|^2)$, where these are buckets of the
\emph{original full values}.  The same prime-divisor averaging as in
Theorem~\ref{thm:3sum} gives $\Phi(r)=O(ns\log n)$ and deterministic
selection cost $\softO(n^2/s)$.  Here distinctness of the full values
