is used; their bit prefixes need not be distinct.  Cut every nonempty
bucket into sub-buckets of size at most $s$.  On either side there are
at most $n/s+r=O(n/s)$ sub-buckets.

Keep the incidences $(\alpha,\beta,z)$ with $z\in C$,
$\alpha\subseteq A_i$, and $\beta\subseteq B_{z-i\bmod r}$.
Every exact solution belongs to exactly one such incidence.  Their
number is at most
\[
 \frac{n\Phi(r)}{2s^2}+\frac{2n^2}{s}+nr
 =\softO(n^2/s).
\]
Enumerating the corresponding lists of sub-bucket identifiers achieves
this bound.  Keep these memberships and incidences fixed throughout
all bit levels.  A prefix sum need not respect the original bucket
residues: the old residue condition only specifies this fixed query
list, and is never used as a prefix congruence test.

Choose polynomially bounded shifts $a_0,b_0$ making
$x+a_0,y+b_0,z+a_0+b_0$ nonnegative for all inputs.  They preserve
$x+y-z$.  Start at a common all-zero prefix above the largest bit.
For every incidence initialize
\[
 G_{-1}=G_1=0,\qquad G_0=|\alpha|\,|\beta|.
\]
These are counts of original pairs; different original values that
coalesce at a prefix retain their multiplicities.  If the current
prefix gap is $\ell=x'+y'-z'$, the next gap is
$2\ell+\epsilon_x+\epsilon_y-\epsilon_z$, with each $\epsilon$ a
bit.  Thus only old gaps in $\{-1,0,1\}$ can enter that window at the
next level, exactly as in
Theorem~\ref{thm:deterministic-exact-witness-counting}.

Apply Lemma~\ref{lem:known-window-purification} with original pairs
$(x,y)\in\alpha\times\beta$ as its labels and $h=s^2$.  Start each
purification attempt at modulus $3$, exclude $3$, $r$, and previously
selected primes, and use all $R$ greedy prime stages.  The initial
union count is $|\alpha||\beta|$.  The targets $z'-1,z',z'+1$ are
distinct modulo every selected modulus, which is divisible by $3$.
Each false original pair therefore has a unique fixed target offset,
and the lemma's same-label nonzero-divisor argument applies without
change.  Minimizing the total modular union count minimizes false
mass because the exact true mass $G_{-1}+G_0+G_1$ is known.  At the
completed modulus, equality of these two counts certifies all three
classes for that incidence.  Four parity-masked products at that same
modulus yield the twelve exact counts $N_{\ell,i,j}$ and the update
\[
 G_t^+=\sum_{\substack{\ell\in\{-1,0,1\},\ i,j\in\{0,1\}\\
                 2\ell+i+j-\epsilon_z=t}}N_{\ell,i,j},
 \qquad t\in\{-1,0,1\}.
\]
At least three quarters of the active incidences are certified per
purification attempt; $O(\log n)$ attempts suffice.  Zero-window
incidences remain zero.  There are $O(\log n)$ bit levels, and summing
the final $G_0$ over the fixed incidence list counts all exact triples.
No witness scans or randomized decisions occur.

Here is the actual cyclic count oracle.  Write the current modulus
as $dq$, keep the factor $3$ in $d$, and put
$d_1=\lceil\sqrt d\rceil$, $d_2=\lceil d/d_1\rceil$.
Over $\mathcal R_q=\mathbb Z[x]/(x^q-1)$, with middle coordinate
$\sigma\in\mathbb Z_d$, define the histogram matrices
\begin{align*}
 X[(\alpha,\rho_1),\sigma]
 &=\sum_{\substack{a\in\alpha\\a'-\rho_1\equiv\sigma\pmod d}}
             x^{a'\bmod q},\qquad 0\le\rho_1<d_1,\\
 Y[\sigma,(\beta,\rho_2)]
 &=\sum_{\substack{b\in\beta\\d_1\rho_2-b'\equiv\sigma\pmod d}}
             x^{b'\bmod q},\qquad 0\le\rho_2<d_2.
\end{align*}
For a requested target $v=z'+\ell$, write
$v\bmod d=\rho_1+d_1\rho_2$.  The coefficient of $x^{v\bmod q}$
in the corresponding product entry counts precisely the original
pairs with $a'+b'\equiv v\pmod{dq}$.  Indeed each such pair uses one
middle residue if and only if its sum is correct modulo $d$; its
cyclic monomial tests the same pair modulo $q$.  Coprimality combines
the two tests.  These entries are multiplicity histograms, not Boolean
presence indicators.  A mask removes its excluded inputs from these
sums, rather than changing their numerical weights to zero.

For formation, first build the two-dimensional residue histogram
$(a'\bmod d,a'\bmod q)$ of each sub-bucket in
$O(n+(n/s)dq)$ time and storage.  Shift-copy these histograms into the
folded rows and columns in $O(qND)$ time, including zero initialization.
This avoids reinserting each input separately for every outer residue.
Pad the two outer dimensions to the stated common $N$ and the inner
dimension to the next power of four; all added entries are zero.
Padding changes dimensions by constant factors.  A target offset
changes only its folded position and coefficient; it causes no extra
factor $d$ or $q$ in the number of queries.  Distinct incidences whose
prefix targets coincide remain separate in the count tables, every
greedy candidate-score sum, and the final sum, even when an oracle
answer can be shared.  Reusing a queried entry does not deduplicate
the incidences in the objective or its contraction argument.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Deterministic integer 3SUM and exact counts]
\label{thm:deterministic-cyclic-3sum}
For sets of polynomially bounded integers, 3SUM and exact \#3SUM
admit deterministic $O(n^{1.9931055})$ algorithms.  The one-set form
can require three distinct elements, with either ordered or unordered
exact counts.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Use Lemma~\ref{lem:fixed-bucket-cyclic-counting} and the aligned cache
of Lemma~\ref{lem:aligned-polynomial-cache}, with
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 c=10.83107856,\qquad\theta=0.06504251269,\qquad b=0.04436367207,\\
 R=1000000000,\qquad S=884889170,\qquad
 \delta=0.0068945.
 \end{gathered}
\]
Here $s=\lceil n^b\rceil$ and the per-incidence label bound is
$h=s^2$.  Each of the $R$ prime factors comes from a pool of scale
$\softO(h^{1/R})$, so enumerating all candidates charges the exponent
\[
 \eta=\frac{2b}{R},\qquad
 u=S\eta=0.0785138659123489638,\qquad
 v=(R-S)\eta=0.0102134782276510362.
\]
In each purification attempt, fold $3$ and the first $S$ selected
factors physically, then freeze their product $d$ and put subsequent
factors into the cyclic ring length $q$.  These are actual integer
stage counts, so $u+v=2b$ holds exactly.  Distinct selected primes,
excluding $3$ and $r$, ensure $\gcd(d,q)=1$.

Write
\[
 T=c-1+\frac{c\HH(1/c)}{\ln4},\qquad
 G=\frac{c\HH(1/c)-c\HH((1-\theta)/c)-\theta\ln4}{\ln2},
 \qquad q_1=\frac{2\HH(\theta)}{\ln4},
\]
and $q_2=q_1+2\theta$, $E=T+q_1$.  The source cache, boxes,
formation, and all requested coefficient queries cost, respectively,
\[
 \softO(qND^E),\quad\softO(qN^2D^{-G}),\quad
 O(n+qND),\quad
 \softO\bigl((n^2/s)(qD^{q_1}+D^{q_2})\bigr).
\]
The separate box encoder exponent $T-G$ is smaller than $E$ and
is included.  Original full-grid tiling requires $N\ge KN_0$;
its exponent condition uses $T$, not the cache exponent $E$.

For the scalar phase let $x\in[0,u]$ denote the current physical
modulus exponent.  Then $N=n^{1-b+x/2}$, $D=n^x$ up to fixed
polylogarithmic factors.  After charging all candidate primes, the
savings below exponent two are
\[
\begin{array}{ll}
 \text{boxes:}&2b-x(1-G)-\eta,\\
 \text{residual queries:}&b-xq_1-\eta,\\
 \text{stored queries:}&b-xq_2-\eta,\\
 \text{polynomial cache:}&1+b-x(E+1/2)-\eta,\\
 \text{dense histogram formation:}&1+b-3x/2-\eta.
\end{array}
\]
The tile margin is $1-b+x/2-xT$.  In the cyclic phase, with
$d$ frozen and current ring exponent $w\in[0,v]$, the savings are
\[
\begin{array}{ll}
 \text{boxes:}&2b-u+uG-w-\eta,\\
 \text{residual queries:}&b-uq_1-w-\eta,\\
 \text{stored queries:}&b-uq_2-\eta,\\
 \text{polynomial cache:}&1+b-u(E+1/2)-w-\eta,\\
 \text{dense histogram formation:}&1+b-3u/2-w-\eta.
\end{array}
\]
The tile margin is now $1-b+u/2-uT$.  Input reading separately
has saving $1-\eta$.  Incidence enumeration, count updates, candidate
summation and survivor bookkeeping have saving $b-\eta$; the original
bucket-prime selection has the still larger saving $b$.

All these expressions are affine in the stage exponent.  The exact
rational certificate checks both endpoints of both phases, the
parameter and tail regimes, and the exact stage identities.  At the
final cyclic endpoint $w=v=2b-u$, the five displayed runtime savings
exceed, respectively,
\[
\begin{gathered}
 0.00689451426923,\qquad 0.00689451427575,\qquad
 0.00689451426351,\\ 0.00689451423864,\qquad
 0.91637939488509.
\end{gathered}
\]
The original tile margin exceeds $0.03415019380523$, and the minimum
runtime slack above $\delta$ exceeds $1.4238\cdot10^{-8}$.

Every $R,S$ here is fixed independently of $n$.  For sufficiently
large $n$ the prime pool contains enough primes after all exclusions,
and the completed modulus is at most $h$ times a fixed polynomial in
$\log n$, with constants allowed to depend on $R$ and the input
magnitude exponent.  Its bit length is $O(\log n)$, including the
fixed-$R$ logarithmic factors.  Histograms, exact counts, and sums over
incidences have polynomial magnitude.  The aligned oracle's signed
linear encodings, auxiliary polynomial caches and bilinear boxes have
polynomial coefficient norms by its existing proof.  Exact cyclic
arithmetic therefore has the stated $\softO(q)$ bit cost.  Divide the
\emph{complete} residual-plus-box numerator coefficientwise by
$4^{L_{\rm tens}}$ before using a count in a certificate; this is exact
integer division and requires no inverse modulo $q$.

The constant number of masks and offsets, all candidate calls,
$O(\log n)$ purification attempts per bit level, $O(\log n)$ bit
levels, padding, sieving and fixed-parameter logarithmic overhead fit
within the strict slack.  Small input sizes are handled by direct
enumeration.  This proves the colored exact count and hence decision.

For the one-set form use $A=B=S_0$, $C=-S_0$.  If $Z$ is the resulting
ordered count, put
$t=|\{(x,z)\in S_0^2:2x+z=0\}|$ and
$z_0=\mathbf1_{0\in S_0}$.  Inclusion--exclusion on equality of the
three labels gives the distinct-element ordered count
$Z-3t+2z_0$.  Sorted lookup computes $t$ in $O(n\log n)$ time.
Divide this count by six for unordered triples, or test positivity
for decision.  No correction is needed for the colored sets problem.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[All-Integers 3SUM and per-target counts]
\label{cor:all-integers-3sum}
For sets $A,B,C$ of at most $n$ polynomially bounded integers, the counts
$\#\{(a,b)\in A\times B:\ a+b=c\}$ for every $c\in C$ are computed
deterministically in $O(n^{1.9931055})$ time. In particular All-Integers
3SUM, which asks for every $c\in C$ whether some $a+b=c$, has the same
deterministic bound.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
Lemma~\ref{lem:fixed-bucket-cyclic-counting} fixes the incidence list
$(\alpha,\beta,z)$ with $z\in C$, and every solution $a+b=z$ lies in
exactly one incidence. The proof of
Theorem~\ref{thm:deterministic-cyclic-3sum} ends with the exact window
count $G_0(\alpha,\beta,z)=\#\{(x,y)\in\alpha\times\beta:\ x+y=z\}$ for
every incidence at the last bit level. Summing these $\softO(n^2/s)$
exact counts by target $z$ costs $\softO(n^2/s)$ and gives every
per-target count; the theorem only adds them once more.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Deterministic 3XOR and exact counts with fixed linear buckets}
\label{sec:deterministic-3xor}
The source's Remark~23 sketches deterministic 3XOR through linear hash
functions over $\mathbb F_2$ with rows chosen from an $\varepsilon$-biased set
by conditional expectations, and Majdoddin~\cite{M26} (Section~18,
Lemmas~18.7--18.9, 18.11--18.12, 18.16) proves $O(n^{1.99451})$ with cut
classes, the carry-free fold of a hash into the outer indices, halving
stages, and a minimum-over-rows certificate. The construction below keeps
his grouping, fold, and greedy rows, and replaces the stages by the
fixed-bucket exact-count lifting of Theorem~\ref{thm:deterministic-cyclic-3sum}.

Vectors of $\mathbb F_2^d$, $d\le c_0\log_2n$, occupy $O(1)$ words and an
inner product $\langle u,x\rangle$ costs $O(\log d)$ word operations.  For
a linear map $\Lambda:\mathbb F_2^d\to\mathbb F_2^r$ and an incidence
$x=(\alpha,\beta,z)$ put
$\mathrm{cnt}_\Lambda(x)=\#\{(a,b)\in\alpha\times\beta:
\Lambda(a\oplus b\oplus z)=0\}$.  A multiset $E\subseteq\mathbb F_2^d$ is
$\beta$-biased if $\bigl||E|^{-1}\sum_{u\in E}(-1)^{\langle u,w\rangle}\bigr|
\le\beta$ for every $w\ne0$.  The explicit construction of Alon,
Goldreich, H\aa stad and Peralta gives a $(1/r)$-biased multiset
$E_{d,r}$ with $|E_{d,r}|\le4(dr)^2$ in $\operatorname{poly}(dr)$ time;
its projection to any coordinate subspace is again $(1/r)$-biased, since
characters of the subspace are characters of $\mathbb F_2^d$.  For a
nonzero $w$ at most a $(1+\beta)/2$ fraction of $E$ annihilates $w$, so
rows chosen one at a time from $E_{d,r}$, each minimizing the surviving
weight of a family of nonzero vectors, leave at most $e2^{-t}$ of the
weight after $t\le r$ rows.  This replaces the prime-divisor bound: one
row doubles the modulus and halves the false mass up to the factor
$1+1/r$, so the loss over all rows is $e$ and no fixed stage count or
polynomial candidate pool is needed.

\begin{lemma}[Group-algebra coefficient ring]
\label{lem:group-algebra-ring}
Lemmas~\ref{lem:five-zero-prefix-ring}, \ref{lem:aligned-walsh-embedding}
(whose coefficient-ring clause is coefficientwise), and
\ref{lem:aligned-polynomial-cache} hold verbatim with
$\mathcal R_q=\mathbb Z[x]/(x^q-1)$ replaced by
$\mathbb Z[\mathbb F_2^m]=\mathbb Z[x_1,\dots,x_m]/(x_1^2-1,\dots,x_m^2-1)$,
$q=2^m$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Their proofs use only that the coefficient ring is commutative and free
of rank $q$ over $\mathbb Z$, that linear operations cost $O(q)$ and
products $\softO(q)$ word operations, that one product coefficient is a
length-$q$ dot product, and that coefficient $\ell_1$ norms are
submultiplicative.  Store an element as its $q$ integer coefficients
indexed by $\mathbb F_2^m$.  The product is
$(fg)(w)=\sum_vf(v)g(v\oplus w)$: coefficient $w$ is a dot product with a
permuted copy of $g$, and the whole product is obtained exactly by two
unnormalized integer Walsh--Hadamard transforms, a pointwise product, a
third transform and exact division by $2^m$, in $O(mq)$ operations on
$O(\log n)$-bit integers.  Norms satisfy $\|fg\|_1\le\|f\|_1\|g\|_1$.
The complete degree-summed numerator equals $4^L$ times the product
entry coefficientwise, so the final division is exact integer division
per coefficient.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Fixed linear buckets and prefix-exact count lifting]
\label{lem:fixed-linear-bucket-counting}
Let $S_1,S_2,S_3\subseteq\mathbb F_2^d$ be sets (no repeated vectors) of at
most $n\le2^d$ vectors, $d\le c_0\log_2n$, $s=\lceil n^b\rceil$, $h=s^2$,
$k=\lceil\log_2(n/s)\rceil$, $l=\lceil\log_2(4eh)\rceil$, and fix
$0\le l_1\le l$.  There is a fixed list of at most $8n^2/s$ incidences
$(\alpha,\beta,z)$, computable in $\softO(n^2/s)$ time, such that every
solution $a\oplus b\oplus z=0$ lies in exactly one of them, and the exact
counts $G(x)=\mathrm{cnt}_{\mathrm{Id}}(x)$ of all incidences follow from
$O(d\log n\cdot l\cdot|E_{d,l}|)=O(\log^7n)$ counting calls.  Each call
takes a linear map $(H_1,H_2)$ with $t_1\le l_1$ rows $H_1$ and
$t_2\le l-l_1+1$ rows $H_2$ and a set $\mathcal I$ of incidences, and
returns $\mathrm{cnt}_{(H_1,H_2)}(x)$ for all $x\in\mathcal I$ as one
coefficient of one entry of an $N\times D$ by $D\times N$ product over
$\mathbb Z[\mathbb F_2^{t_2}]$ with $D=2^{t_1}$, $N=O((n/s)\sqrt D)$,
formed in $O(n+2^{t_2}ND)$ time, whose entries are multiplicity
histograms of magnitude at most $s$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\emph{Buckets.}  Choose rows $w_1,\dots,w_k\in E_{d,k}$ one at a time,
each minimizing
$\Phi(\pi)=\sum_{\gamma\in\mathbb F_2^k}(|A_\gamma|^2+|B_\gamma|^2)$ for
the map $\pi$ with the rows so far followed by the candidate, where
$A_\gamma=\{a\in S_1:\pi(a)=\gamma\}$ and $B_\gamma$ likewise.  Here
$\Phi(\pi)=|S_1|+|S_2|+P_1+P_2$, where $P_i$ counts the pairs $(x,x')\in S_i^2$
with $x\ne x'$ and $\pi(x\oplus x')=0$, and $x\oplus x'\ne0$ by
distinctness, so the greedy
bound gives $\Phi(\pi)\le2n+e2^{-k}(|S_1|^2+|S_2|^2)\le2n+2ens$.  Each
evaluation costs $O(nk\log d+2^k)$, so selection costs $\softO(n)$.  Cut
every nonempty $A_\gamma,B_\gamma$ into sub-buckets of at most $s$
vectors; there are at most $n/s+2^k\le3n/s$ on each side.  The
incidences are $(\alpha,\beta,z)$ with $z\in S_3$, $\alpha\subseteq A_i$,
$\beta\subseteq B_{i\oplus\pi(z)}$.  By linearity a solution has
$\pi(b)=\pi(a)\oplus\pi(z)$, so it lies in exactly one incidence.  Using
$\lceil p/s\rceil\lceil p'/s\rceil\le pp'/s^2+p/s+p'/s+1$ and
$|A_i||B_j|\le(|A_i|^2+|B_j|^2)/2$, the number of incidences is at most
$n\Phi(\pi)/(2s^2)+2n^2/s+n2^k\le8n^2/s$.  Keep this list fixed.

\emph{Levels.}  For $0\le\ell\le d$ let $\Pi_\ell$ project to the first
$\ell$ coordinates; any complete flag of functionals serves.  Maintain
$G^{(\ell)}(x)=\mathrm{cnt}_{\Pi_\ell}(x)$ for every incidence, with
$G^{(0)}=|\alpha||\beta|$ and $G^{(d)}=G$.  Since $\Pi_{\ell+1}s=0$
implies $\Pi_\ell s=0$, an incidence with $G^{(\ell)}=0$ stays zero.
There is a single window $\{0\}$ and no signed targets, because XOR has
no carries.

\emph{One attempt at level $\ell$.}  Let $\mathcal I$ be the active
incidences with $G^{(\ell)}>0$ and $E=E_{d,l}$.  Start with no rows;
$l$ times, for every $u\in E$ evaluate
$\Sigma(H,u)=\sum_{x\in\mathcal I}\mathrm{cnt}_{(H,u)\Pi_\ell}(x)$ by one
counting call and append a minimizer.  The first $l_1$ rows form $H_1$
and the rest $H_2$.  A false pair $(a,b)$ of $x$, one with
$\Pi_\ell(a\oplus b\oplus z)\ne0$, is killed by a row $u$ exactly when
$\langle u,\Pi_\ell(a\oplus b\oplus z)\rangle=1$; genuine pairs survive
every row.  The true mass $\sum_{x\in\mathcal I}G^{(\ell)}(x)$ does not
depend on the rows, so the greedy minimizes the false mass
$F=\sum_x(\mathrm{cnt}-G^{(\ell)})$, and the projected $E$ is
$(1/l)$-biased on $\mathbb F_2^\ell$, so after $l$ rows
$F\le e2^{-l}\sum_xF_\emptyset(x)\le e2^{-l}h|\mathcal I|
\le|\mathcal I|/4$.  Certify every $x$ with
$\mathrm{cnt}_{H\Pi_\ell}(x)=G^{(\ell)}(x)$; each uncertified incidence
contributes at least one to $F$, so at least three quarters are
certified.  For a certified $x$ the pairs with
$H\Pi_\ell(a\oplus b\oplus z)=0$ are exactly its pairs with
$\Pi_\ell(a\oplus b\oplus z)=0$.  Hence one further call with the
coordinate functional $e_{\ell+1}$ appended as a group-algebra row
returns $\mathrm{cnt}_{(H\Pi_\ell,e_{\ell+1})}(x)
=\mathrm{cnt}_{\Pi_{\ell+1}}(x)=G^{(\ell+1)}(x)$ for every certified $x$.
The target bit enters only through the queried position and
coefficient; no input masks are needed.  Remove the certified incidences
and repeat; $O(\log n)$ attempts finish the level and $d$ levels give $G$.

\emph{The counting call.}  Write $H_1=(H_1',H_1'')$ with
$\lceil t_1/2\rceil$ and $\lfloor t_1/2\rfloor$ rows,
$\sigma\in\mathbb F_2^{t_1}$, and over $\mathbb Z[\mathbb F_2^{t_2}]$
\begin{align*}
 X[(\alpha,\rho_1),\sigma]&=\sum_{a\in\alpha:\ H_1a\oplus(\rho_1,0)=\sigma}
   x^{H_2a},\qquad\rho_1\in\mathbb F_2^{\lceil t_1/2\rceil},\\
 Y[\sigma,(\beta,\rho_2)]&=\sum_{b\in\beta:\ H_1b\oplus(0,\rho_2)=\sigma}
   x^{H_2b},\qquad\rho_2\in\mathbb F_2^{\lfloor t_1/2\rfloor}.
\end{align*}
At position $(\rho_1,\rho_2)=H_1z$, coefficient $x^{H_2z}$ of the
product entry counts the pairs with $H_1(a\oplus b)=H_1z$ and
$H_2(a\oplus b)=H_2z$, that is $(H_1,H_2)(a\oplus b\oplus z)=0$; the two
tests are independent linear conditions and need no coprimality.  The
split of $H_1z$ is exact, without carries.  Build the histogram
$(H_1a,H_2a)$ of each sub-bucket in $O(n+(n/s)2^{t_1+t_2})$ time and
shift-copy it into the folded rows and columns in $O(2^{t_2}ND)$ time;
pad to common dimensions with zero entries.  One coefficient is read per
incidence; incidences sharing a position and coefficient share the
answer but remain separate in every sum.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Deterministic 3XOR and exact counts]
\label{thm:deterministic-3xor}
For three lists of at most $n$ vectors of $\mathbb F_2^d$ with
$d=O(\log n)$, 3XOR, exact $\#3\mathrm{XOR}$, and the per-target counts
$\#\{(a,b):a\oplus b=z\}$ for every $z\in S_3$ admit deterministic
$O(n^{1.9931055})$ algorithms.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
For $n\le1$ use direct enumeration. Otherwise retain the original ambient
size bound $n$ and append zero coordinates to every vector until
$d'=\max\{d,\lceil\log_2 n\rceil\}$. This preserves XOR equations and
multiplicities, gives $n\le2^{d'}$, and keeps $d'=O(\log n)$; write $d$
for this padded dimension below.
Remove repeated vectors in $O(n\log n)$ time, keeping multiplicities.
Use Lemma~\ref{lem:fixed-linear-bucket-counting} with the aligned cache
of Lemmas~\ref{lem:aligned-polynomial-cache} and
\ref{lem:group-algebra-ring} and
\[
 c=10.83107856,\quad\theta=0.06504251269,\quad b=0.04436367207,\quad
 u=0.0785138659,\quad\delta=0.0068945,
\]
with $l_1=\lfloor u\log_2n\rfloor$, so $D\le n^u$ and
$2^{t_2}\le2^{l-l_1+1}=O(n^{2b-u})$.  With $T,G,q_1,q_2,E$ as in
Theorem~\ref{thm:deterministic-cyclic-3sum}, a call with $D=n^x$,
$0\le x\le u$, and ring length $q=n^w$, $0\le w\le2b-u$, where $w=0$
while $x<u$, has $N=n^{1-b+x/2}$, costs
$\softO(qND^E+qN^2D^{-G}+qND)$ to preprocess and
$\softO(qD^{q_1}+D^{q_2})$ per incidence, for $O(n^{2-b})$ incidences.
The savings below exponent two are those displayed in
Theorem~\ref{thm:deterministic-cyclic-3sum} with $\eta=0$: the pool
$E_{d,l}$ has $O(\log^4n)$ rows, each level has $O(\log n)$ attempts of
$l+1=O(\log n)$ rows, and there are $d=O(\log n)$ levels, so all
$O(\log^7n)$ calls are charged by a fixed polylogarithmic factor.
Bucket selection, incidence enumeration, count tables, and the final
summation cost $\softO(n^{2-b})$.  The exact rational certificate
\texttt{verify\_\allowbreak cyclic\_\allowbreak 3xor.py} checks 46 strict inequalities at both
endpoints of both phases; at the final group-algebra endpoint the box,
residual, stored and cache savings exceed $0.0068945143$, the tile
margin exceeds $0.03415$, and the minimum slack above $\delta$ exceeds
$1.4\cdot10^{-8}$, which absorbs the polylogarithmic factors for large
$n$; small $n$ use direct enumeration.  The sum $\sum_xG(x)$ is the
exact number of solutions over the deduplicated sets and summing by $z$
gives the per-target counts.  For lists with multiplicities, at every
incidence certified in some attempt the hash $(H\Pi_{d-1},e_d)$ of that
attempt is exact, so one weighted histogram product per attempt, with
entries $\sum m(a)x^{H_2a}$ of $O(\log n)$ bits, returns
$\sum m(a)m(b)$ over its genuine pairs; multiplying by $m(z)$ gives the
multiset count. For a single set $S$ of $N=|S|$ distinct values, let $Z$
be its ordered count with repetition allowed. Requiring three distinct
values gives $Z-3N\mathbf 1_{0\in S}+2\mathbf 1_{0\in S}$.
For a single list of length $N$, let $Z$ instead be the weighted ordered
count and let $m(0)$ be the number of zero occurrences. Requiring three
distinct occurrence indices gives $Z-3Nm(0)+2m(0)$. Indeed, each of the
three index-equality events has $Nm(0)$ solutions, since the third value
must be zero, and each intersection of two or three such events has
$m(0)$ solutions. Inclusion--exclusion proves this correction and its
set specialization.
Decision follows from the count.
\end{proof}

\subsection*{Earlier integer 3SUM counting with one-sided error}
The direct bucket-pair reduction also gives a counting bound. This is an
additional consequence of the present reductions, not a claim of literature
priority. Let $A,B,C$ be sets of polynomially bounded integers as in
Theorem~\ref{thm:3sum}. In Lemma~\ref{lem:bucket}, replace Boolean membership
by multiplicity:
\begin{align*}
X[(\alpha,\rho_1),\sigma]
 &=\#\{x\in\alpha:x-\rho_1\equiv\sigma\pmod p\},\\
Y[\sigma,(\beta,\rho_2)]
 &=\#\{y\in\beta:p_1\rho_2-y\equiv\sigma\pmod p\}.
\end{align*}
These entries are at most $s$ and have logarithmic bit length. At the folded
position for $z$, their product counts the pairs $(x,y)\in\alpha\times\beta$
with $x+y\equiv z\pmod p$, including distinct pairs that collide at a
residue. Thus for each compatible sub-query $Q=(z,\alpha,\beta)$ and sampled
prime $p_i$ its answer is $C_i(Q)=Z(Q)+F_i(Q)$, where $Z(Q)$ counts the exact
integer equalities and $F_i(Q)\ge0$ counts the false pairs. Return
\[
\widehat Z=\sum_Q\min_i C_i(Q).
\]
Every exact triple belongs to exactly one compatible sub-query, so
$\widehat Z\ge\#\{(x,y,z)\in A\times B\times C:x+y=z\}$ always.
For a fixed $Q$, the union bound over its at most $s^2$ nonzero differences
$x+y-z$ gives $\Pr[F_i(Q)>0]\le1/2$, with precisely the same sub-bucket size
and prime-divisor estimate as in Theorem~\ref{thm:3sum}. This holds even if
$Q$ has exact solutions. Independent samples imply that its minimum is
inexact with probability at most $2^{-k}$. There are at most $n^3$
sub-queries, since there are at most $n$ nonempty sub-buckets on each side
and $n$ choices of $z$. Hence
$k=\lceil(\gamma+3)\log_2 n\rceil$ ensures that all minima are exact with
probability at least $1-n^{-\gamma}$ for every fixed $\gamma>0$ and $n\ge2$.
Finitely many small sizes can be counted directly.

There are no scans. All preprocessing, query enumeration, multiplicity
construction, and queries have the same deterministic cost bounds as in
Theorem~\ref{thm:3sum}, with its scan costs omitted. The certified unfolded
parameters therefore give \emph{worst-case} $O(n^{1.996001})$ time for
integer $\#3\mathrm{SUM}$, by a Monte Carlo algorithm that never undercounts
and has error probability at most $n^{-\gamma}$. This is not a Las Vegas
counting algorithm; the folded Exact Triangle saving $0.004057$ does not
apply to this reduction.

For a single set $S$, run the algorithm with $A=B=S$ and $C=-S$. Write
$t=\#\{x\in S:-2x\in S\}$ and $z_0=\mathbf1_{\{0\in S\}}$. Among the ordered
zero-sum triples, exactly $R=3t-2z_0$ use a repeated element: each of the
three equality events has $t$ triples, and every intersection is the
all-zero triple when zero is present. Sorting computes $R$ in
$O(n\log n)$ time. Thus
\[
\left\lceil\frac{\widehat Z-R}{6}\right\rceil
\]
never undercounts the unordered zero-sum triples of three distinct elements
and is exact whenever $\widehat Z$ is exact. This counting statement is for
sets; no multiset bucket-balance guarantee is being asserted.

\subsection*{Min-sum subset convolution through rectangular restrictions}
Let $f,g:2^{[n]}\to\mathbb Z\cup\{+\infty\}$ be explicit tables with
finite values of at most $\beta$ bits, and define
$h(S)=\min_{A\mathbin{\dot\cup}B=S}(f(A)+g(B))$.
We use the restriction products and local-cover organization of Mihail
Stoian, \emph{Truly Sub-$3^n$ Min-Sum Subset Convolution and Join Ordering},
\href{https://arxiv.org/html/2610.10282v1}{arXiv:2610.10282v1}~\cite{S26},
Sections 2--4. The changed ingredient is an asymmetric restriction cost
from rectangular tiles. This improves even the direct substitution of our
square min-plus exponent into that paper's transfer theorem.

\begin{lemma}[Rectangular tiles, $c>10$]
\label{lem:subset-rect-tiles}
Put $A_0=(c-1)\ln3/\ln4$, so $N_0=\widetilde\Theta(D^{A_0})$,
and retain $M=KN_0^2=\widetilde\Theta(D^{2T})$ and $E=T$.
For outer dimensions $R,C$ satisfying $R,C\ge N_0$ and $RC\ge M$,
the refined data structure preprocesses an $R\times D$ by $D\times C$
product in
\[
\softO\left(\sqrt{RC}\,D^E+(R+C)D^{A_0}+RC D^{-G}\right)
\]
time, with individual query time $\softO(D^q)$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Use a $K_1\times K_2$ grid of block products per tile, with
$K_1K_2=\Theta(K)$ and $K_1K_2\le K$. Assign distinct inner subsets
to grid positions and zero the unused products. The source's independent
batch identity applies unchanged; a left input depends only on its row
band and a right input only on its column band. The same boxes suffice.

Choose $K_1$ within a constant factor of
$\min\{K,\max\{1,\sqrt{KR/C}\}\}$ and
$K_2=\lfloor K/K_1\rfloor$, with harmless constant-factor padding.
In the unclamped case the number of bands on each side is
$O(\sqrt{RC/M})$, and their encodings cost $O(\sqrt{RCM})$.
If $R/C\ge K$, take $K_1=K,K_2=1$: the encoding cost is
$O(RN_0+CKN_0)=O(RN_0)$. The opposite case is symmetric.
The assumptions ensure both bands fit up to constants. Ceiling terms cost
$O(M)\le O(\sqrt{RCM})$. There are $O(RC/M)$ tiles; the unchanged
box bound therefore gives $\softO(RCD^{-G})$.
\end{proof}
For $1<c<10$ the same layout applies with each band encoding costing
$10^L\operatorname{poly}(m)$ instead of $O(M)$, so in the clamped regime
$K_1=K$, $K_2=1$ the encodings cost $RD^{A_{\rm cl}}+CD^{A_{\rm cl}+C_K}$
with $A_{\rm cl}=c\ln10/\ln4-C_K-A_0$, and in the unclamped regime
$\sqrt{RC}\,D^E$ with the $c<10$ branch of $E$; the box term is unchanged.

Write the original outer dimensions of an all-edges Exact Triangle
instance as $2^y,2^z$ and its middle dimension as $2^x$, and put
$a=(y+z)/2$. Applying residue folding, amplification, and
Lemma~\ref{lem:subset-rect-tiles} gives the following complete cost list:
\begin{align*}
\text{encodings: }&2^{a+x}D^{E-1/4}
 +(2^y+2^z)2^xD^{A_0-1/4},\\
\text{boxes: }&2^{x+y+z}D^{-G},
&\text{queries: }&2^{x+y+z}D^{q-1/2},\\
\text{writing: }&(2^y+2^z)2^xD^{3/4},
&\text{successful scans: }&2^{y+z}D^{1/2}.
\end{align*}
These expressions suppress polynomial factors in the logarithms of the
dimensions. Indeed, each folded instance has outer dimensions
$\widetilde\Theta(2^yD^{1/4}),\widetilde\Theta(2^zD^{1/4})$, and there
are $\widetilde O(2^xD^{-1/2})$ instances. Every outer pair is queried
once per prime and block, as before. Taking $O(x+y+z+1)$ independent
primes makes expected failed-scan work negligible; original sums still
certify acceptance, and successful scans stop separately for each pair.
The tiling requirements are
\[
a>(T-1/4)\log_2D,\qquad
\min\{y,z\}>(A_0-1/4)\log_2D,
\]
with sufficient slack for the suppressed logarithms.

Use the fixed rational constants
\[
(c,\theta,e,\delta)=(21.0477,0.111971,0.05828,0.004057),
\quad \lambda=0.916,\quad \kappa=0.034,
\]
and choose
\[
b=\min\left\{\frac{y+z}{2},\frac y\lambda,
                 \frac z\lambda,\frac x\kappa\right\},
\qquad D=4^{\lfloor eb/2\rfloor}.
\]
Every listed cost is $\softO(2^{x+y+z-\delta b})$. The respective
savings, in units of $b$, are bounded below by
\[
1-e(E-1/4),\quad \lambda-e(A_0-1/4),\quad
eG,\quad e(1/2-q),\quad \lambda-3e/4,\quad \kappa-e/2,
\]
all strictly exceeding $\delta$. The same inequalities secure the tile
and block-size requirements. Bounded $b$, or a failure due only to
polylogarithmic padding when $b=O(\log(n+1))$, can use direct enumeration
with a polynomial factor in $n=x+y+z$.

\paragraph{Arbitrary input bit length.}
The integer magnitude assumption need not be imposed here. Put
$Q=(\beta+n+1)(n+1)^2$, $P=\lceil\sqrt D\,Q\rceil$, and
$h=\lfloor D/(2P)\rfloor$. A nonzero $\beta$-bit sum has
$O((\beta+1)/\log P)$ prime divisors in $[P,2P]$, so the probability
that a zero-free block passes one prime is
$O(h(\beta+1)/P)=O(D(\beta+1)/P^2)$, below $1/2$ for sufficiently
large $n$. When $h\ge1$, the new block counts and folded dimensions
increase each of the costs above by only $\operatorname{poly}(n,\beta)$;
the lower-bound tiling conditions become easier. If $h=0$, then
$\sqrt D<4Q$ for $D\ge4$. Direct enumeration loses at most
$2^{\delta b}=O(D^{\delta/e})=\operatorname{poly}(n,\beta)$.
Exact arithmetic on original weights has polynomial bit cost; the
folded matrices contain Boolean entries and their encoded counts have
polynomial bit length in $n$. The priority-preserving min-plus bit-scaling
reduction takes $O(\beta)$ levels and therefore gives the same rectangular
min-plus bound times $\operatorname{poly}(n,\beta)$. Address operations
are charged by their $O(n+\log(\beta+1))$ bit length.
For absent entries use $4W+1$, where $W\ge1$ bounds finite magnitudes;
a sum involving this sentinel exceeds $2W$ and is mapped back to infinity.

\begin{theorem}[Subset convolution]
\label{thm:subset-convolution}
Min-sum subset convolution on arbitrary $\beta$-bit integer tables has a
Las Vegas algorithm with expected running time
$O(2.997190^n\operatorname{poly}(n,\beta))$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
For a restriction $[n]=X\mathbin{\dot\cup}Y\mathbin{\dot\cup}Z$,
use rows $U\subseteq Z$, middle indices $T\subseteq X$, and columns
$V\subseteq Y$, with matrices
\[
F[U,T]=f(U\cup T),\qquad
G[T,V]=g((X\setminus T)\cup V).
\]
Each product entry updates $h(X\cup U\cup V)$. It enumerates exactly
the disjoint pairs compatible with this restriction: $X$ forbids outside
membership, $Y$ forbids left membership, and $Z$ forbids right membership.
Put $x=|X|,y=|Y|,z=|Z|$. The rectangular bound above is at most
$2^n w(X,Y,Z)\operatorname{poly}(n,\beta)$, where
\[
w=2^{-\delta(y+z)/2}+2^{-\delta y/\lambda}
  +2^{-\delta z/\lambda}+2^{-\delta x/\kappa}.
\]
Indeed $2^{-\delta b}$ is the maximum of these four terms. Forming
the matrices and performing the output updates fit the same bound,
since $x\ge\kappa b$ and $y,z\ge\lambda b$, with
$\kappa,\lambda>\delta$.

For a block of $r$ coordinates, summing $w$ over all restrictions gives
\[
B_1^r+2B_2^r+B_3^r\le4B^r,
\quad B_1=1+2^{1-\delta/2},\quad
B_2=2+2^{-\delta/\lambda},\quad B_3=2+2^{-\delta/\kappa},
\quad B=\max_iB_i.
\]
Each candidate ternary membership string belongs to exactly $2^r$
compatible restrictions. Round each of the four terms upward to a
multiple of $2^{-r}$. Every term is at least $2^{-r}$, so rounding
inflates total weight by at most two. Weighted greedy set cover now
produces a complete local family of weight at most
\[
8H_{3^r}(B/2)^r.
\]
Here $H_t=\sum_{j=1}^t1/j$.
For completeness, if $u$ candidates remain, the sum of the numbers
covered by all restrictions is $u2^r$. Some restriction therefore has
rounded weight per newly covered candidate at most
$8B^r/(u2^r)$. Charging successive removals and summing $1/u$ gives
the displayed harmonic bound. Explicit construction takes $2^{O(r)}$
time. Since the exponents are fixed rationals, upward rounding uses
integer root comparisons on $O(r)$-bit integers and is exact.

Partition the universe into $\lceil n/\lceil\sqrt n\rceil\rceil=O(\sqrt n)$
blocks, all but the last of size $\lceil\sqrt n\rceil$, and concatenate choices from the corresponding local families.
Compatibility is preserved and every candidate is covered. Each of the
four summands in $w$ is separately multiplicative under concatenation.
Consequently, writing $W_j$ for the total local weight,
\[
\sum_{\sigma\in\prod_j\mathcal F_{r_j}}w(\sigma)
 \le4\prod_jW_j,
\]
and the complete expected time is
\[
B^n\,2^{O(\sqrt n\log(n+1))}\operatorname{poly}(n,\beta).
\]
This includes empty and arbitrarily unbalanced restriction parts.
Exact certificates give $B_i<2.997190$ for each $i$, so the strict
gap absorbs the subexponential factor. Every matrix call is Las Vegas,
and linearity of expectation gives the claimed total bound.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Rectangular isolated recovery with square five-term tiles]
\label{lem:five-rect-isolation}
Let the two outer parts have $R$ and $C$ vertices, the middle part $H$
vertices, and let the finite weights have at most $\beta$ bits; put
$\Lambda=\log_2(R+C+H)+\log_2(\beta+1)$. Fix parameters of
Lemma~\ref{lem:five-profile} and a power of four $D$ with $H\ge\sqrt D$
and $\min\{R,C\}D^{1/4}\ge D^{T+\eta}$ for a fixed $\eta>0$. Then
all-edges Exact Triangle has an always-correct algorithm with expected time
\[
 \operatorname{poly}(\Lambda,\beta)\bigl((R+C)H(D^{E_{\rm aug}-1/4}
 +D^{E_{\rm box}-1/4})+RCH\,D^{-G}+RCH\,D^{\max(q_1,q_2)-1/2}
 +(R+C)HD^{3/4}+RC\bigr),
\]
and the $R\times H$ by $H\times C$ min-plus product, with $+\infty$
entries allowed, has the same bound.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Put $Q=(\beta+\Lambda+1)(\Lambda+1)^2$, $P=\lceil\sqrt D\,Q\rceil$, and
$h=\lfloor D/(2P)\rfloor$. If $h=0$ then $\sqrt D<4Q$ for $D\ge4$;
enumerate all $RCH$ triples directly, which costs
$RCH\beta\le\beta(16Q^2)^G\cdot RCH\,D^{-G}$ and is within the bound.
Otherwise $h\le\sqrt D/2\le H$, so the middle part splits into
$O(HQ/\sqrt D)$ blocks of at most $h$ vertices. Run the algorithm of
Lemma~\ref{lem:isolation} with Lemma~\ref{lem:five-rect-tiles} as the
data structure on the folded instances of Lemma~\ref{lem:fold}:
$\lceil L\Lambda\rceil$ independent modular filters per block, at most
$\Lambda+1$ dyadic mask levels with $C_0\Lambda$ trials each, a fresh
uniform prime from $[P,2P]$ per trial, one count product and one
label-sum product per trial, exact integer checks of decoded witnesses,
and fallback scans of every unsolved pair/block that passed all filters.
A nonzero sum has at most $\beta+2$ bits and hence
$O((\beta+\Lambda)/\log P)$ prime divisors in $[P,2P]$, so a zero-free
pair/block passes one filter with probability
$O(h(\beta+\Lambda)/P)=O((\beta+\Lambda)/Q^2)\le1/2$, and conditional
on any mask retaining exactly one genuine witness a fresh prime isolates
it with probability at least $1/2$; the arguments of
Lemma~\ref{lem:isolation} then give failure probability at most
$2^{-L\Lambda}$ per pair/block and expected fallback work
$RCH\beta\,2^{-L\Lambda}=o(1)$ for a sufficiently large fixed $L$.
Correctness holds for every random outcome.

Each folded instance has outer dimensions $R'=R\lceil\sqrt p\rceil
=\Theta(RD^{1/4}\sqrt Q)$ and $C'=\Theta(CD^{1/4}\sqrt Q)$ and inner
dimension at most $2Ph\le D$; the hypothesis
$\min\{R,C\}D^{1/4}\ge D^{T+\eta}$ gives
$\min\{R',C'\}\ge KN_0\operatorname{poly}(\log D)$ for all sufficiently
large $D$. There are $O(\Lambda^2HQ/\sqrt D)$ instances.
Lemma~\ref{lem:five-rect-tiles} charges their encodings
$(R'+C')(D^{E_{\rm aug}}+D^{E_{\rm box}})$, their boxes $R'C'D^{-G}$,
and one query per outer pair at exponent $\max(q_1,q_2)$; writing their
explicit matrices costs $(R'+C')D$. Multiplying by the instance count
gives the first four displayed terms. Residues $w(a,b)\bmod p$, label
decoding, and exact checks cost $RC\operatorname{poly}(\Lambda,\beta)$ per
instance, which the query term dominates since $D^{\max(q_1,q_2)}\ge1$.
Labels are block-local, in $\{1,\ldots,h\}$, and counts are at most $h$,
so all entries are at most $h^2\le D$, polynomial in the dimensions as
Lemma~\ref{lem:five-rect-tiles} requires. For min-plus, shift each product's two finite matrices to be
nonnegative, use the sentinel $4W+1$ for absent entries, where $W\ge1$
bounds finite magnitudes, run the bit-scaling recurrence with its
priority rule for $O(\beta)$ levels of two all-edges calls each, remove
the local shifts, and map sums above $2W$ back to $+\infty$. Fresh randomness in each adaptive call
preserves the expected bound.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Subset convolution through square five-term tiles]
\label{cor:subset-five}
Min-sum subset convolution on arbitrary $\beta$-bit integer tables has a
Las Vegas algorithm with expected running time
$O(2.995627^n\operatorname{poly}(n,\beta))$.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
Use the restriction products of Theorem~\ref{thm:subset-convolution}:
for $[n]=X\mathbin{\dot\cup}Y\mathbin{\dot\cup}Z$ with sizes $x,y,z$,
rows $U\subseteq Z$, middle indices $T\subseteq X$, columns
$V\subseteq Y$, and $F[U,T]=f(U\cup T)$, $G[T,V]=g((X\setminus T)\cup V)$,
a $2^z\times2^x$ by $2^x\times2^y$ min-plus product whose entries update
$h(X\cup U\cup V)$. Apply Lemma~\ref{lem:five-rect-isolation} with the
parameters of Corollary~\ref{cor:five-apsp},
\[
(c,\theta,r,y,e)=(10.74132662,\;0.056740653,\;0.038571087,\;
0.025146880,\;0.083812911),
\]
and the fixed rationals $\delta=0.006324$, $\lambda=1$, $\kappa=0.0425$,
\[
 b=\min\{y/\lambda,\;z/\lambda,\;x/\kappa\},\qquad
 D=4^{\lfloor eb/2\rfloor}.
\]
Here $\Lambda=O(n+\log(\beta+1))$, so every suppressed factor is
$\operatorname{poly}(n,\beta)$. Relative to $2^{x+y+z}$, the costs of the
lemma save, in units of $b$, at least
\[
 eG,\quad e(1/2-q_1),\quad e(1/2-q_2),\quad
 \lambda-e(E_{\rm aug}-1/4),\quad \lambda-e(E_{\rm box}-1/4),\quad
 \lambda-3e/4,\quad \lambda,\quad \kappa
\]
for boxes, residual queries, stored queries, the two encoder families,
folded matrix writing, input formation, and residues with output
updates. The exact-rational certificate gives
\[
\begin{gathered}
 eG=0.0063251295\ldots,\qquad e(1/2-q_1)=0.0063251295\ldots,\\
 e(1/2-q_2)=0.0063251295\ldots,\qquad
 1-e(E_{\rm aug}-1/4)=0.0063251406\ldots,
\end{gathered}
\]
all above $\delta$, with $1-e(E_{\rm box}-1/4)>0.0097$ and the rest far
from binding. The short side holds a square tile since
$\min\{y,z\}\ge b$ and $1-e(T-1/4)=0.0034181\ldots>0$, and the middle
part holds a block since $2^x\ge2^{\kappa b}\ge2^{eb/2}\ge\sqrt D$, as
$\kappa-e/2=0.0005935\ldots>0$. Thus every restriction product costs
$2^{x+y+z-\delta b}\operatorname{poly}(n,\beta)\le2^nw(X,Y,Z)
\operatorname{poly}(n,\beta)$ with the three-term weight
\[
 w=2^{-\delta y/\lambda}+2^{-\delta z/\lambda}+2^{-\delta x/\kappa},
\]
whose maximum term is $2^{-\delta b}$. The geometric term of
Theorem~\ref{thm:subset-convolution} is absent because square tiles
charge encodings to $R'+C'$, and the clamped term is absent because
Lemma~\ref{lem:five-rect-tiles} has no clamped regime: the fit is on
the two outer sides $\min\{y,z\}$ only.

For a block of $r$ coordinates, summing $w$ over all restrictions gives
exactly
\[
 2B_2^r+B_3^r\le3B^r,\qquad B_2=2+2^{-\delta/\lambda},\quad
 B_3=2+2^{-\delta/\kappa},\quad B=\max\{B_2,B_3\}.
\]
Each candidate ternary membership string belongs to exactly $2^r$
compatible restrictions. Since $\delta/\lambda<1$ and $\delta/\kappa<1$,
every term is at least $2^{-r}$, so rounding each term upward to a
multiple of $2^{-r}$ inflates the total weight by at most two; the
exponents are fixed rationals, so the rounding uses exact integer root
comparisons on $O(r)$-bit integers. Weighted greedy set cover produces a
complete local family of weight at most $6H_{3^r}(B/2)^r$: with $u$
candidates uncovered the restrictions cover $u2^r$ candidate incidences,
so some restriction has rounded weight per newly covered candidate at
most $6B^r/(u2^r)$, and summing $1/u$ gives the harmonic factor.
Partition $[n]$ into $O(\sqrt n)$ blocks of size at most
$\lceil\sqrt n\rceil$ and concatenate local choices; compatibility is
preserved, every candidate is covered, and each of the three terms is
multiplicative under concatenation, so
$\sum_{\sigma\in\prod_j\mathcal F_{r_j}}w(\sigma)\le3\prod_jW_j$. The
complete expected time is
$B^n2^{O(\sqrt n\log(n+1))}\operatorname{poly}(n,\beta)$, including
empty and arbitrarily unbalanced parts. When $b$ is bounded, when the
tile condition fails only through its $\operatorname{poly}(n)$ padding
because $b=O(\log n)$, or when $h=0$ in the lemma, direct enumeration
loses at most $2^{\delta b}\le(4D)^{\delta/e}=\operatorname{poly}(n,\beta)$.
The exact certificate gives $B_2=2.9956261\ldots$ and
$B_3=2.9020004\ldots$, both below $2.995627$, and the strict gap absorbs
the subexponential cover factor. Every matrix call is Las Vegas, and
linearity of expectation gives the bound.
\end{proof}
Writing $g=\min\{G,1/2-q_1,1/2-q_2\}$, balancing just the box/query,
augmented-encoder, and tile constraints gives the candidate ratio
$r_*=g/\max\{T-1/4,\,E_{\rm aug}-1/4+g\}$. The chosen certified ratio
$\delta/\lambda=0.006324$ is strictly below $r_*$, as the exact-rational
certificate checks; the other encoder, formation, and middle-size
constraints are checked separately above. The same rounded saving
$\delta=0.006324$ is feasible for the square folded amplified reduction
with Lemma~\ref{lem:five-profile}. Since $\lambda=1$, the base
$2+2^{-\delta/\lambda}$ coincides with the transfer $2+2^{-\delta}$ of
that certified square saving. Thus the displayed rectangular witness
adds no improvement beyond this square saving; this comparison asserts
no optimality of $r_*$. Stoian
displays $O^*(2.9987^n)$ Las Vegas and $O^*(2.9997^n)$ deterministic.
Substituting the deterministic square saving $0.00705208$ of
Corollary~\ref{cor:aligned-cache-apsp} into Stoian's transfer theorem
gives deterministic $O^*((2+2^{-0.00705208})^n)=O^*(2.995124^n)$ for
tables whose finite entries have magnitude $n^{O(1)}$: restrictions whose
block dimension is below a fixed power of $n$ are enumerated directly at
polynomial loss, and the larger products have entries polynomial in their
dimension, as that corollary requires. For arbitrary $\beta$-bit tables this
would need the composite certification with divisor bound
$\Theta(\beta)$ in place of $\Theta(\log n)$, which is not done here; the
Las Vegas bound above has no such restriction. The separate script
\texttt{verify\_five\_\allowbreak term\_\allowbreak subset.py} certifies
34 strict rational inequalities for this corollary and the comparison.

The leading candidate base is
$B_1=2.997189877936\ldots$; direct substitution of the same square
saving gives $2+2^{-0.004057}=2.997191852132\ldots$.
The source's Section 5 join-ordering reduction uses polynomially many
subset-convolution calls and only polynomial bit growth, so its
$C_{\rm out}$ application inherits $O^*(2.997190^n)$, where $O^*$
suppresses factors polynomial in $n,\beta$, under its stated
cardinality-estimate assumptions, including its undirected-query-graph
convention for forbidding cross products.

The separate script \texttt{verify\_subset\_\allowbreak convolution.py}
certifies thirteen strict rational inequalities, and
\texttt{check\_subset\_\allowbreak convolution.py} checks finite weighted
covers and 120 signed or infinite input pairs against direct subset
convolution; these checks do not replace the proof.

\section{Verification and limits of the claims}
The accompanying \texttt{verify\_exponents.py} uses exact rational interval
arithmetic. It bounds logarithms via
\[
\ln x=2\sum_{j=0}^{r-1}\frac{z^{2j+1}}{2j+1}+R_r,
\qquad z=\frac{x-1}{x+1},\qquad
0\le R_r\le\frac{2z^{2r+1}}{(2r+1)(1-z^2)}
\]
after rescaling to $1\le x\le2$, and uses $r=64$. All asserted exponent
inequalities compare rational interval endpoints. Decimal values displayed
in this note are not used as certificates. The main run certifies 172 strict
inequalities, covering historical and current witnesses for the thin
products, integer and real 3SUM, the unbatched and batched APSP reductions,
deterministic all-edges Exact Triangle by per-pair moments and its clique
instances, rectangular
convolution, the lopsided $4$-Clique split, hinted OMv and uMv,
MonoConvolution, and all-fixed-$k$ clique splits. Its output is in
\texttt{certificate-results.txt}. The subset-convolution verifier adds
13 strict inequalities, recorded in \texttt{subset-certificate-results.txt}.
The five-term black-box certificates add 89 inequalities (thin products in
the source's regimes, real inputs, the MonoConvolution transfer, and the
lopsided clique split), 44 (min-plus convolution), and 34 (subset
convolution); their recorded outputs are the three files named
\texttt{five-term-*-certificate-results.txt} with the corresponding
middle words.
The explicit directed APSP exponent adds 31 inequalities and the 3XOR
construction 46, in \texttt{directed-apsp-} and
\texttt{cyclic-3xor-\allowbreak certificate-\allowbreak results.txt}.

The script
\texttt{check\_fixed\_\allowbreak linear\_\allowbreak bucket\_\allowbreak counting.py}
checks the 3XOR construction on finite instances: the group-algebra
products against naive XOR convolution, the small-bias families and their
prefix projections, the bucket second moment and incidence bounds, the
folded products against brute force, the level certification with a fixture
that forces restarts, and end-to-end exact counts for sets, multisets, and
the one-list correction, in twelve tests.
The separate \texttt{verify\_deterministic\_apsp.py} adds 45 strict
inequalities for the two deterministic cyclic-encoding witnesses, recorded
in \texttt{deterministic-apsp-\allowbreak certificate-\allowbreak results.txt}. The two external
rectangular-multiplication theorems are explicit dependencies, not proved
by these numerical certificates.

\texttt{check\_pruned\_encoding.py} performs 3,232 exact integer checks:
full versus restricted encodings, retained-leaf counts, the prefix work
bound, and decoded matrix entries for small recursion depths. These support
the implementation of the encoding lemma; they do not replace the
asymptotic proof, implement the complete large-instance algorithms, or
benchmark practical performance.

\texttt{check\_reductions.py} adds twelve small-instance correctness tests,
including 240 signed modular-filter instances, 202 folded residue-label
instances whose product entries are compared with direct modular counts,
152 bucket-pair 3SUM instances checked against direct modular pair sums,
60 deterministic modulus selections checked against brute-force
false-positive counts, and the block reduction of min-plus convolution on
all outputs for lengths up to 100, besides 400 signed min-plus instances. Its oracle is deliberately brute force: it
checks the reductions, including different false witnesses for different
primes, rather than claiming to implement the asymptotic data structure.
All twelve tests pass.

The additional finite checkers exercise conditional modular moments
(\texttt{check\_modular\_moments.py}, eight tests, including per-pair
certificates on 300 planted all-edges instances) and certified prefix
counts (\texttt{check\_prefix\_counts.py}, five tests).
Rectangular convolution and singleton recovery are covered by
\texttt{check\_rectangular\_reductions.py} in seven tests, including
4,160 signed convolution instances, and one-sided 3SUM counting by
\texttt{check\_3sum\_counts.py} in six tests.
The subset checker additionally verifies weighted covers, their Cartesian
composition, and 120 full convolution tables.

\texttt{check\_direct\_sum\_tiling.py} places independent signed matrices in
fixed subset slots, reuses each band encoding across tiles, and decodes 5,079
entries against direct products, including partial packs and tiles. The
script \texttt{check\_aggregated\_\allowbreak queries.py} checks the
prefix-contracted query identity, the shrinking transforms, and the signed binomial aggregation
against brute-force leaf sums in five tests.
\texttt{check\_lazy\_encoding.py} adds five tests covering 9,217 cyclic
windows, 600 sparse contractions, 1,936 retained-array lookups, 146
complete free-pruned queries, and 146 complete lazy-prefix queries. It
counts sparse input reads and checks full intermediate-array storage,
as well as values against direct products. The unified command
\texttt{python3 scripts/verify.py} reruns every checker and compares all fifteen
certificate outputs with the recorded files. These are finite oracle
checks and numerical certificates, not a proof assistant or a full fast
implementation.

The additional \texttt{verify\_five\_term\_apsp.py} certifies 29 strict
inequalities for Corollary~\ref{cor:five-apsp}, with saved output in
\texttt{five-term-apsp-certificate-results.txt}. The five-term checks
validate retained internal arrays, balanced residual queries, complete box
closure, and an independent original-array oracle. The additional
\texttt{check\_five\_term\_profile.py} validates 216 complete fixed-profile
queries, both actual retained encoders, truncated-transform predecessors,
and the fixed-order box closure against an original-array oracle.
\texttt{check\_five\_term\_profile\_maps.py} checks exact conditional
expectations and bijections for five profile-map fixtures. Separate composite
checks compare residue-folded counts and incidence-halving decisions
against brute force, including true incidences that require a restart.
These checks do not independently prove the external squared embedding.

The additional \texttt{verify\_five\_term\_ring\_apsp.py} certifies 48
strict inequalities for Corollary~\ref{cor:five-ring-apsp}, including both
endpoints of each modulus phase and the exact integer prime-factor split.
Its saved output is \texttt{five-term-ring-apsp-certificate-results.txt}.
The finite \texttt{check\_five\_term\_ring.py} compares folded cyclic
counts with original signed sums, checks complete coefficient queries
against original-array products, and exercises greedy prime selection,
incidence certification, restarts, and fallback scans. Stored-box queries
read only the target coefficient; residual queries retain their full
cyclic factor. Schoolbook ring multiplication in these tests is an oracle,
not an implementation of the asymptotic polynomial-arithmetic bound.

The partial-cache \texttt{verify\_five\_term\_cache\_apsp.py} certifies 58
strict inequalities for Corollary~\ref{cor:five-cache-apsp}; its saved
output is \texttt{five-term-cache-apsp-certificate-results.txt}. It charges
the augmented encoding exponent $E$, retains $T$ in geometry, checks
both residual branches, and covers both endpoints of each modulus phase.
The finite \texttt{check\_five\_term\_cache.py} constructs the augmented
encoders from signed source arrays, checks exact leaf and intermediate
counts, verifies every truncated-transform predecessor and the disjoint
lexicographic groups, and compares complete queries with original matrix
products. Opposite low/high classifications on the two input sides are
included. Its fixtures cover 544 complete integer outputs and 96 requested
coefficients from 24 complete cyclic-ring outputs, using independent
original-matrix products as oracles. These tests supplement the general cache proof; they do not
independently prove the source embedding or an asymptotic exponent.

The aligned-cache \texttt{verify\_five\_term\_aligned\_apsp.py} adds 47
strict inequalities for Corollary~\ref{cor:aligned-cache-apsp}, with
recorded output in \texttt{five-term-aligned-apsp-certificate-results.txt}.
It charges $E=T+q_1$, retains the original $T$ geometry, and checks both
endpoints of both prime phases. The accompanying
\texttt{check\_five\_term\_aligned\_embedding.py} checks 352 original
signed matrix outputs, including nontrivial transpose locators.
\texttt{check\_five\_term\_aligned\_cache.py} checks 34 complete arbitrary
source-array queries and 20 compact encoders, with 423,918 retained
leaves and 1,088,138 total retained/intermediate entries. It verifies every residual
coefficient and the constant-coefficient box bases.
\texttt{check\_five\_term\_aligned\_integration.py} composes those steps
and checks 530 normalized original-product coefficients, including
every target in a signed cyclic-ring fixture. Its 68 encoders account
for 2,011,348 retained and intermediate entries. The finite checks
supplement the Walsh embedding and polynomial-cache proofs; they do
not replace those proofs or independently establish the asymptotic bound.

The light-source \texttt{verify\_light\_source\_apsp.py} checks 61
strict rational inequalities for Corollary~\ref{cor:light-source-apsp}
and the exact rational small-bias potential inequality. Its recorded
output is \texttt{light-source-\allowbreak apsp-\allowbreak certificate-\allowbreak results.txt}.
It retains all three encoder charges, both phase endpoints, original
geometry, the independent batch fit, source-type derivative guard,
suffix fit, and the positive rectangular-algorithm allowance.
In the full research checkout, \texttt{scripts/verify.py} also reproduces
the seven original research suites under \texttt{work/}: shifted sources, structured
completion, adaptive lengths, cropped sources, jointly capped boxes,
light-source translations, and the next-pass appendix. These include
complete integer and cyclic products, explicit duplicated selector
vectors, row/column chunk padding, exact conditional-expectation
construction, and a strict backend that reconstructs omitted leaves.
The results-only checkout retains the supporting checkers and their
import dependencies, run by \texttt{scripts/verify\_structured\_sources.py}.
Recorded outputs are compared byte for byte. Scoped ceiling checks
in those suites describe only their stated cost families.
These finite tests and rational certificates supplement the proofs
in Section~\ref{sec:light-source}; they do not establish the external
matrix-multiplication theorem or formally verify the asymptotic argument.

The exact-half refinement has its own 61-inequality certificate,
\texttt{verify\_half\_source\_apsp.py}, with recorded output in
\texttt{half-source-\allowbreak apsp-\allowbreak certificate-\allowbreak results.txt}.
In the full research checkout,
\texttt{work/\allowbreak half-\allowbreak source-\allowbreak improvement/\allowbreak verify.py} checks
22,656 exact conditional choices for $m=1,\ldots,4$ and 2,922
affine half-weight coverage cases. Its embedding checker uses the
actually constructed ordered lists and first valid indices, and
verifies 416 complete normalized signed cyclic coefficients.
It omits only translation indices whose masks are identically
zero on the fixture. The results-only checkout runs these two finite
checkers through \texttt{scripts/verify\_structured\_sources.py}.
The earlier strict grouped-residual and joint-box
checks already use the finite cap $\lfloor m/2\rfloor$ and remain
in the standard suite. These checks supplement the all-$m$ potential
argument in Lemma~\ref{lem:half-source}.

The signed-window reduction has two additional finite checkers.
\texttt{check\_exact\_counting.py} runs ten tests using actual residue-folded
cyclic products as the modular oracle, checking common-prime selection,
restarts, four parity masks, signed original matrices, arbitrary targets,
duplicate middle labels, and uneven or padded blocks. Its companion
\texttt{check\_signed\_window\_counts.py} independently checks 600 signed
matrix instances through 4,997 prefix updates and 6,065,880 certified
masked counts, plus exact contraction and restart fixtures. These check
Theorem~\ref{thm:deterministic-exact-witness-counting}'s reduction; they do
not implement the fast tensor oracle. Its exponent uses the unchanged
47-inequality aligned certificate.

The separate \texttt{verify\_cyclic\_3sum.py} supplies 48 strict rational
checks for Theorem~\ref{thm:deterministic-cyclic-3sum}, including both
endpoints of both modulus phases, all candidate costs, the original
full-grid geometry, and the inherited MonoConvolution exponent. Its
saved output is \texttt{cyclic-3sum-certificate-results.txt}. The numerical
certificate relies on the separately proved fixed-bucket count-lifting
and cyclic histogram lemmas. The additional
\texttt{check\_fixed\_bucket\_counting.py} runs six finite tests with actual
folded cyclic histograms, including 50 signed set instances, a constructed
purification restart, prefix coalescence, and an objective-multiplicity
regression for shared query answers. Its occurrence-list fixtures check a
separate extension retained in the research notes; the stated theorem is
for sets. All these finite checks supplement the asymptotic proof.

A floating-point exploration of the generalized Schönhage identities
$\langle k,1,n\rangle\oplus\langle1,(k-1)(n-1),1\rangle$ with the same box
and encoding analysis, recorded in \texttt{work/tensor/shapes.py} in the
full research repository,
indicates that the source's $3\times3$ shape maximizes the integer savings in
this framework, with or without folding. That is a numerical observation
about this parameter family, not a theorem or an optimality claim.

The paper explicitly says some better exponents were omitted, and already
notes the tight all-edges APSP transfer. Accordingly these results improve
its displayed guarantees; no claim is made that the ideas are unknown to
the authors or new to the literature. The derivations have been checked
algebraically and computationally during this work, but have not been
independently peer reviewed or formalized in Lean.
